## Live context-engine failure — 2026-09-19 21:00:09

### CE1. Chat scan receives no conversation history and passes a pronoun as a KG seed — CONTEXT HANDOFF FIXED locally

Live log the live application log at 21:00:09.056 records should_activate=True with
seeds=['the user', 'he']. Its reason explicitly says the person is only referred to by
'he' and suggests retrieval may identify them. At 21:00:09.540 activation cannot
resolve 'he' and aborts the whole background run, including the resolved the user seed.
Source: room_session_manager.py's context-engine trigger passes user_message,
primary_user and owner_id, but omits the supported recent_chat_context parameter.
chat_scan wraps its result without entity-resolution validation, and activation
raises for any unresolved label. This is a broken producer/consumer contract:
entity-name lookup cannot resolve discourse pronouns without their antecedents.
Fix direction: supply relevant preceding conversation, resolve references before
activation, and represent unresolved references explicitly without inventing KG
entities or silently pretending full context was obtained. Keep prompt instructions
in Jinja. Background logging followed by re-raising accounts for duplicate traceback
output; this evidence does not establish two separate executions. The failure belongs
to master_room's context pipeline, not the interleaved Dayflow state_mover run.
No runtime changes were made during live monitoring.

### CE1 repair checkpoint

The user explicitly approved giving the scanner the scoped history already prepared
for the room agent, with the constraint that ordinary chat remain fast. The room
trigger now reuses seeded_chat_messages after reply delivery; no extra history query
is performed. The existing background pipeline still owns all scanner/model and KG
work, and the room never joins it. Scanner inputs are structured variables and all
reference-resolution instructions/formatting live in Jinja. The model is instructed
to resolve pronouns from history and decline activation when identity is ambiguous.
KG lookup still fails visibly for unresolved entity names; no invented entities,
pronoun substitution heuristics, or silent partial activation were added.

Twelve focused tests pass: prepared-history handoff, Jinja rendering, nonblocking
background trigger, and existing room runtime regressions. Model output was mocked;
this verifies plumbing and latency isolation, not a live-model pronoun-resolution
success rate. Requires application restart for the Python caller change; no restart,
commit, push, database migration or outbound action performed by this repair.

## State-mover timing repair — 2026-09-19

### DF30. Background work held as an interruption; evening holds moved into bedtime — FIXED locally, model behavior needs live verification

Live logs: at 21:40 state mover held research_coinbase_recovery until 22:15 because
research would supposedly interrupt the user's evening. At 21:35 it moved the
school-update question to 22:15 to avoid wind-down. Another hold used the already-ended
21:00–21:30 dog walk to defer a 21:34 question until 22:00. The old prompt both restricted
holds to interruptions and prohibited next-day holds, encouraging an unsuitable same-night
retry. Both prep paths supplied only a title-first 140-character context, omitting the
full directive needed to distinguish research from user-facing work.

Repair: Jinja explicitly permits background research and scheduled device actions during
quiet/away periods; holds require an actual interruption conflict. Resume times must
resolve the conflict and avoid sleep, allowing the next known available day when needed.
Both tick and wake prep now use one structured candidate with full directive, goal,
status, due gate and prior finalizer judgment. No Python prompt prose or classification
heuristic added. Existing stored wake times were not changed and no process restarted.

Publication check: newly added prompt examples use synthetic data; live personal names and work identifiers in these follow-up notes have been generalized.


### DF31. Durable evaluator inbox replays legacy admitted email history as new intake — FIXED locally, restart required

The durable-inbox repair in 7aacd502 introduced an unbounded compatibility predicate
in state_store.load_admitted_intake: state=artifact AND (evaluator_pending=1 OR
state_reason=triage_admit). Historical artifact rows retain triage_admit even though
that legacy marker does not establish a pending handoff under the new contract.
StrategicPlannerWoPrepNode merges all these rows into admitted_artifacts on each tick.
The strategic_planner_wo user Jinja labels them NEW INTAKE and omits source dates.

Read-only live verification on 2026-09-19 found an early-August family email still
marked artifact/triage_admit with both creation and last-review dates in August,
rendered in September planner context. There are 296 pre-September email rows eligible
under this predicate, reaching back to April 9. This is stored-history replay, not
proof of a new Gmail delivery. The normal email loader's today filter and
get_dayflow_items freshness filter do not govern this separate durable inbox path.

Intended: explicitly pending admissions survive restarts until handoff; legacy
context artifacts must not become fresh requests merely because they were once
admitted. Prompts must expose source dates so historical relative deadlines are
interpreted correctly. Consequences: stale requests can create duplicate or obsolete
work and old urgent wording can be interpreted as current. Repair must distinguish
new pending admissions from legacy history while preserving genuinely pending work;
do not blindly expire all pending work. No stored data or runtime code changed during
this diagnosis. Status: open. This refines the DF9/DF20 durable-inbox repair limits.


DF31 repair: removed the legacy triage_admit fallback from load_admitted_intake;
only explicit evaluator_pending=true admissions in artifact state are reloaded.
Read-only verification found zero pre-September email artifacts carrying that explicit
marker, so the 296 legacy emails are excluded without altering stored records.
The steward Jinja now labels the section PENDING INTAKE, renders original source
dates (or an explicit unknown-date label), and anchors relative deadlines to those
dates. Intake triage also renders full source dates rather than time of day alone.
Nine focused tests passed, including exclusion of legacy/cleared/closed admissions,
retention of a 90-day-old explicitly pending admission, restart handoff recovery,
and actual Jinja rendering of historical source dates. git diff --check passed.
Requires restart for the Python query change. Existing work objects were not changed;
this repair prevents replay but does not establish that earlier replay created no work.


### DF32. Shared work prompt loader bypassed canonical path resolver — FIXED

The publication regression guard caught fixed parent-index path arithmetic in
work_context.py. Template discovery now uses get_repo_root(), following the runtime

bypassed the centralized resolver. No stored data changes.


### DF33. Explicitly pending intake has no evaluator no-action disposition — FIXED locally

Following DF31, explicit evaluator_pending admissions correctly survive age windows.
However, StrategicPlannerWoPersistNode closes only sources transferred to work objects;
intake not cited in a successful handoff remains artifact/pending. The evaluator form
has no intake-level reviewed/no-action disposition and relevance_cleaner is unwired.
Thus an admitted item repeatedly judged not to require work has no automatic exit
from the durable inbox. The ordinary 24-hour view filter does not apply to this inbox.
Consequence: newly admitted non-actionable items can accumulate and be reconsidered
indefinitely. Intended: retain unprocessed admissions through failures/restarts, while
persisting explicit reviewed/no-action decisions separately from work handoffs.
At diagnosis, no runtime or stored-data change had been made.


Original agreed scope: restore an explicit intake cleanup lifecycle. First trace the
former orchestrator summarizer and relevance_cleaner to establish their actual prior
behavior. Then design durable dispositions for transferred-to-work, reviewed/no-action,
and deferred-with-reason intake, with cleanup for stale deferred/context items.
Preserve unprocessed requests through failures/restarts and preserve active work.
Acceptance: reviewed/no-action items stop recurring in pending intake; deferred items
have an explicit reconsideration/retirement policy; unfinished handoffs remain recoverable.
Implemented explicit no-action and timed-deferral reviews for ALL admitted sources,
including camera pods, in the existing evaluator. Every presented item must be transferred
or explicitly reviewed. No-action closes immediately; concrete deferrals return at the first
cycle after their due time. Original sources remain stored. Review batches are transactional
and reject changed source snapshots. Handoffs still close only after durable source transfer.
Also found/fixed: prep merged stale blackboard admissions over stored eligibility, allowing
closed/deferred intake back into the prompt; state mover could treat reviewed no-action
sources as fresh wake candidates. Durable eligibility now wins and event candidates respect
review dispositions. The retired relevance_cleaner remains unwired; its replacement is the
existing evaluator's explicit decisions, without another agent/model call.
A cited work spec with an empty objective is rejected before persistence (work_persist otherwise skips it); it cannot count as a completed handoff.
No live stored-data cleanup or backfill performed.

Audit-inbox review found an email task created from historical intake before DF31's

The parent worker then created an ask_user request at 23:20:57 to obtain permission

Intended: propagate explicit denial through nested manager results and stop the
same action without soliciting approval again; record/finalize the task outcome.
Investigate parent handling of manager_aborted and ask_user reply recovery. This
extends the remaining nested abort propagation concerns, rather than indicating

Audit review also confirmed that the previously reported notification re-ask nodes

### SH1. Lights tool misses nested partial-failure details and reports full success — FIXED locally

Confirmed source mismatch: smart_home_bridge returns {ok, integration, command,
result: {changed, state, unreachable}}. send_smart_home_command preserves that
wrapper. LightsControlTool.execute reads unreachable and changed from the outer
object instead of result, so its partial-reachability text never runs for the real
bridge envelope. The structured nested data survives in ToolResult.data, but the
content falsely states executed successfully. Live September 19 logs show discovery
timeout at 22:00:21, tool success text at 22:00:25, and a manager claim that all lights
were off at 22:00:31. Finalization then received the overconfident manager result.
Existing partial-reachability tests exercise bridge helpers, not this envelope boundary.
Intended: report devices changed and devices unreachable accurately through the
bridge/tool/manager/finalizer chain. Partial completion must not claim whole-house
completion. This explains the previously reported lights success-reporting finding;
it is not an additional independent bug. No runtime changes in this diagnosis.


SH1 repair: LightsControlTool now reads changed/lights and unreachable from the
bridge's nested result object while preserving the complete envelope in ToolResult.data.
Malformed result envelopes fail visibly instead of yielding success text. Tool-result
prose and follow-up guidance live in prompts/result.j2: report changed-device count,
name unreachable devices with unknown state, preserve exceptions in summaries, stop
this attempt, and do not automatically retry, seek approval/user intervention, or
create follow-up tasks/reminders. The next normally scheduled command may try again.
The tool description reinforces that policy. Eight focused tests passed using fake
devices, covering partial bridge-to-tool power/list results, one command invocation,
full success, malformed responses, and existing all-unreachable/targeted failures.
No real device actions, process restart, commit, or push. The no-retry guidance is
agent-facing policy; these tests do not establish live-model compliance.


Mailbox count repair: dispatch handlers return whether context was actually appended.
Unsupported types and empty/invalid injection entries no longer increase drain_to's
return count; one message targeting multiple agents still counts once. Updated the
old regression assertion that incorrectly counted a rejected blackboard_write.


DF28 atomicity repair (user-approved small scope): _apply_node_wakes now commits
clearing the wait and attaching arrival evidence through one WorkStore.batch call.
The existing transaction rolls back both graph and audit event on failure. An
expected_updated_at fence rejects changes between the post-node read and commit;
missing evidence is rejected and terminal/in-flight nodes are not released here.
The task retains its gate on failure, so normal promotion cannot dispatch it.
Nineteen state-mover/provenance tests passed, including evidence mutation failure,
failure after SQL persistence, successful subsequent retry, missing evidence, and
concurrent gate replacement. No live database repair or process restart.

Remaining DF28 scope: evidence is still appended to the task content, as before.
Moving it to attributed provenance and matching the exact pre-LLM gate snapshot
remain follow-up work alongside DF27; the post-read version fence does not claim
to validate the model's earlier input snapshot.


### DF35. One-shot informational reminder is retried and escalated despite an explicit frequency limit — PROMPT POLICY UPDATED; LIVE VALIDATION PENDING

September 20 live read-only review: a morning reminder's objective and main-task
content explicitly permit one brief notification and no additional prompt before
lunch unless requested. Tickets were sent at 06:00 and 07:01 local, expired without
acknowledgment, and the finalizer recommended another fresh notification. The
second unmet judgment triggered ask_user; an approval-to-remind ticket was sent at
09:00. The finalizer's own outcome acknowledged both earlier attempts and the
frequency constraint, yet proposed another. The process was the existing PID from
the previous evening; judgments predate the latest escalation repair. However,
DF29's historical-counter reset alone does not address this failure: neither
attempt achieved a judged success and the finalizer chose retry, not stop.

Intended: distinguish an informational notification with no required reply from a
question requiring an answer. Nonresponse must not override an explicit one-shot
or do-not-repeat constraint. A permission-to-send-another-reminder is itself an
additional interruption. Inspect ticket outcome semantics and finalizer handling
of notification frequency limits; do not claim user acknowledgment without evidence.
No runtime changes or live task/ticket mutations made during diagnosis.

Repair: finalizer, architect, and steward now include a shared Jinja policy for
unanswered contact. Routine/stale/one-shot prompts stop; an important still-relevant
retry must respect contact limits and the retry bound. Silence does not establish
receipt, consent, or danger. Permission-to-remind loops and recreation of stopped
contact are explicitly disallowed. The finalizer uses unrecoverable/stop rather
than claiming an expired goal was achieved, and chooses stop at the retry bound.
The existing store guard preserves stop instead of overriding it with ask_user.
Architect guidance also covers old finalizer instructions asking to repeat these
prompts. No new runtime classifier, chat-path work, or live task/ticket mutation.
These are model instructions; model compliance requires subsequent live validation.

Related confirmed off-by-one: finalizer_input previously warned only after the
prior unmet count already reached the limit, although finalize_task increments
before comparing. The warning now includes the current candidate judgment, so
at one prior failure it explains that a second unmet result reaches the bound.
Focused render regression covers prior counts zero, one, and two.

### DF27 / DF28 source-context repair checkpoint — 2026-09-20

This supersedes the earlier remaining-scope notes. External waits now receive full
main-task directives and structured intake candidates (no arbitrary first-30 cap).
Matched source_item_id must resolve to the prepared intake snapshot. Persistence
copies attribution and exact pod_id from that source; it cannot be replaced by a
model-generated pod reference. Gate release and an owned evidence record commit
atomically against the pre-LLM work version. Original directives remain unchanged.
The wake record is excluded from node_result so arrival context cannot masquerade
as a completed tool call. Worker/finalizer source sections include source excerpts;
architect/steward retain concise attribution and source handles. Work-level source
context survives takeover and new-node replanning. Existing intake age/eligibility
limits and historical records remain unchanged; semantic approval matching still
requires conservative model judgment, not just a source ID.

Validation covers actual Jinja views, source transfer from originating intake,
replan/takeover context, invalid source and stale gate/task rejection, atomic failure
rollback and retry, no duplicate receipt, and a source beyond the old 30-item cap.
No live database changes or external actions were performed.

Validation also corrected a stale strategic-render test assertion from before DF29: lifetime failure history must be visible without issuing another escalation directive.

### DF36. Ticket choices are fixed by category and response semantics collapse — FIXED locally; live semantic validation pending

Confirmed by reading ticket_builder/composer/agent_form.py and prompts/system.j2,
create_dayflow_ticket._ui_policy_for_ticket_kind and composer-output extraction,
static/js/proactive_popup.js, and TicketService.respond. The composer chooses
notify/advice/decision and writes title/message, but cannot supply button count,
labels or action scope. Advice always renders Acknowledge / Will Do / No / Later;
decision always renders Allow / Deny, even when the requested answer is not an
authorization. Notify and decision popup layouts do not offer a free-text field.
This prevents users from qualifying those responses through that popup.

The service preserves the clicked label and typed text together in user_text when
supplied, and separately keeps user_action, but maps done, acknowledge, willdo,
answer and accept to accepted with user_response_parsed.decision=accept. The richer
original response is therefore available to some readers, while a consumer that
reads only state/parsed decision loses the distinction between receipt, intention,
completion and permission. No new live misinterpretation is claimed by this review.
The same-state text-loss defect is already recorded under Remaining findings.

Proposed direction, not implemented: extend the existing composer with a validated
list of contextual choices and explicit meanings/scopes; render escaped labels;
resolve submitted choice IDs against the stored ticket; preserve exact button,
meaning and typed text separately; propagate them consistently into finalizer and
planner context. Keep free text available and authoritative over generic button
meaning. Audit approval consumers separately so acknowledgement cannot become
permission. Preserve compatibility for old tickets and non-popup response surfaces.

DF36 repair checkpoint: existing composer now supplies validated contextual choices;
persistence, popup, response service, tool results and strategic views preserve their
meaning and scope alongside authoritative typed text. Acknowledgment does not imply
commitment or completion. No new agent call or automatic approval is introduced.
Contextual replies use atomic receipts and follow-up history; the previously recorded
legacy same-state text-loss defect now rejects different fields instead of false success.

Related findings during integration: proactive_popup._resolveLayout ignored the
button_layout supplied by create_dayflow_ticket for dayflow_orchestrator tickets;
those tickets fell through to advice buttons. It now honors the validated/persisted
kind for that ticket type. Popup network-error rollback retained _processing and lost
the typed draft, preventing a successful retry. Retry rollback now clears that flag
and restores the draft. Both changes are covered by isolated browser verification.
State-mover response rendering omitted acknowledgments and response details; it now
renders all prepared response categories with the exact response history. Historical
accepted/declined buckets are display groupings, not independent authorization.

### DF37 / WO1. Timeout can start recovery without stopping the original worker — FIXED locally (2026-09-20)

2026-09-20 read-only lifecycle review, following the user's question about killing
hung workers. Confirmed code path: dayflow_tick._sweep calls
sweep_stuck_work_nodes then recover_pending_finalizations. The sweeper waits for
subtree inactivity beyond ASK_WINDOW_HOURS (1 hour) plus 20 minutes, records a
failed ToolResult with aborted=True, but sends NO cancellation request. The flag
is result evidence, not a signal to the running manager. Recovery explicitly allows
finalization of dispatch_sweeper results while their original session is alive.
A retry/replan can consequently dispatch a successor before the old worker exits.

_start_registered_thread rejects duplicate registration only for the SAME epoch.
For a newer epoch it replaces the registry entry even if the old thread is alive.
An isolated AST execution of the actual function with fake threads reproduced:
old_thread_alive=True, replacement_alive=True, one registry entry tracking only
replacement. No real threads, tools or live databases were exercised.

MAMInstanceManager.cancel sets cancelled on ONE manager's own blackboard; it does
not propagate to nested manager invocations. An isolated execution with two fake
manager records confirmed parent_cancelled=True and child_cancelled=False.
MultiAgentManager._run_loop checks that flag at cycle boundaries, so even requested
cancellation cannot interrupt an already-running agent/tool call. ManagerInterface
creates separately invoked nested managers; no shared work-attempt cancellation
mechanism was found. The generic tool execution paths have no work-attempt ownership
check before each external action. DayflowToolCaller does check the epoch before
entering the top-level tool call, but that does not guard subsequent nested calls.
WorkContext and WorkPlanner/helper mutations also lack a captured main-attempt fence
(the remaining WO1 finding). Refreshing a prompt from current graph state does not
revoke a stale worker's authority to execute.

Impact: a timed-out worker may resume, add history, invoke further tools, or finish
an external action while a successor is working. The final-result epoch fence
protects graph result commits; it cannot undo an email/device action already sent.
This review establishes a lifecycle gap, not an observed live duplicate action.
Comments in dispatch_sweeper claiming there is no in-process liveness to consult
are misleading: a session registry exists, though liveness alone cannot prove
progress or cancel a hung call. Closing a work object likewise does not cancel the
worker (previously noted under WO6); it requires this shared lifecycle policy.

Recommended repair order: bind the main dispatch attempt to a shared cancellation
signal inherited by its nested managers; request cancellation on timeout/abandonment;
check cancellation/ownership before new tool execution and worker graph writes;
retain old-attempt liveness until exit; coordinate replacement so an unresolved
in-flight external action cannot be blindly repeated. Cooperative cancellation
must not be described as force-killing a Python thread. Already-started external
operations need outcome reconciliation or explicit tool-level idempotency before
retry. Keep timeout evidence and normal finalizer judgment; do not add a second
result/finalization path. No runtime repair or live-state mutation made in this review.


#### DF37 / WO1 implementation and verification — 2026-09-20

Immutable main-attempt ownership now propagates through managers, agent activations,
helper WorkContext and monitored background execution. A queued descendant is registered
before submission and retains the attempt barrier after its parent returns. Worker
writes and tool admission check durable revocation/epoch. Timeout and abandonment
revoke execution without erasing live calls. Replacement claims (including new node
IDs) are held while execution or unknown external outcomes remain. Finalization uses
the existing path and remains the only failure-counter owner.

The implementation review also found the Orchestrator child cancellation path wrote
only a blackboard flag; it now calls MAM cancellation using the invocation ID. Direct
ManagerInterface node entry and legacy discharge now establish the same attempt
registration. An existing inline worker directive in ManagerInterface was moved to
the existing discharge Jinja view. The runtime dashboard now treats cancelling or
unknown execution as a hold rather than displaying an unconditional healthy badge.

Verification: isolated fake blocked tools, stale writes/epochs, inherited attribution,
post-approval/model cancellation, uncertain receipts across connections, queued child
cleanup, additive migration, restart identity, existing ticket recovery, result/finalizer
paths, and browser rendering with synthetic data. See EXECUTION_OWNERSHIP.md for
limitations: cooperative cancellation, conservative unknown classification, inability
to reconstruct receipts for actions performed by older releases, and no exactly-once
claim. No live actions or databases were used to verify this repair.

Additional DF37 runtime inspection: cancelled executor futures could remain recorded
as submitted, and finishing a never-started task decremented another task's running
count. Completion accounting is now idempotent, decrements only a running task,
and closes cancelled/failed submission records as well as execution reservations.

DF37 integration finding: MCP transport exceptions are converted to returned
`mcp_call_failed` errors, so exception-only receipt handling would incorrectly settle
them. Missing returns, this explicit transport failure, and `outcome_unknown=True`
now retain uncertainty. Ordinary returned failures keep normal finalizer semantics.

Execution verification follow-up (2026-09-20): all 149 focused assertions passed, but
test shutdown logged a late finalizer lookup after fixture deletion and an event-hub
submission after executor shutdown. This demonstrates a harness/background-lifetime
race, not a confirmed live deployment failure. The review also removed an unnecessary
global Dayflow signal from generic execution cleanup: the owner supplies its completion
callback, so standalone/scenario stores cannot wake unrelated Dayflow work. Production
EventHub shutdown ordering is a separate follow-up if reproduced outside this harness.


DF33 verification also reproduced a pre-existing assertion mismatch in
`test_email_pod_handle.test_the_goal_line_carries_the_pod_handle`, without the DF33 overlay.
The source renderer prefers `email_summary` over the generic subject-style `summary`;
the test still required the latter. Updated the assertion to require the informative
email summary alongside the exact pod handle. No source-rendering runtime change.


### DF38. Evaluator closure failures are swallowed and cancellation can be lost — FIXED locally

Confirmed in `work_persist.persist_steward_output`: complete/abandon exceptions were
logged as warnings and the pass continued, leaving failed closures active and eligible
for dispatch. The decision lived only on the tick blackboard. Closure and concern
feedback shared one try block, although concern_feedback already handles its own
post-commit errors. This is the remaining closure finding above, now repaired.

All explicit terminal intents are saved atomically in existing work-object constraints
before applying closures or creating/changing other work. Pending closure blocks readiness,
claims, revisions, worker writes and new tool calls. Terminal mutation removes its intent
in the same commit; failure preserves it and raises to stop the pass. Evaluator prep
retries durable intents before presenting the portfolio, including after restart.
Concern feedback runs only after successful closure and logs a separate failure category.
Completed closures are not rolled back if a later closure or feedback fails.

Limits: if the database cannot save the initial intent batch, the pass raises but there
is no durable request to recover. Already-started external calls cannot be recalled.
At DF38 completion concern feedback remained best-effort; DF40 below adds durable delivery. No live
work-object cancellation, stored-data backfill, restart, commit or push performed.

Same-pass source/objective updates for a closing work object are committed with its
closure intent, preserving incoming user context before the object becomes terminal.
Verification: injected terminal-write failures, restart/second-connection recovery,
blocked claims/tool calls, atomic intent batches, partial closure progress, separate
feedback failures, and existing intake/execution/finalization regressions.


### DF6 duplicate-validation checkpoint — FIXED locally

`_dedupe_prunes` previously skipped invalid mappings while the batch could still consume
the finalizer's instruction. It now rejects blank/self/unknown/finished/non-main task
references and survivors also being abandoned or deduplicated. This matches the existing
agent form's rule: keep exactly one survivor, without chains or cycles.

Also confirmed in the same path: `work_architect_node._duplicate_pairs` silently skipped
malformed pairs and overwrote repeated duplicate IDs before graph validation. Conversion
now rejects malformed shapes, non-string/blank/self IDs and repeated normalized IDs.
The entire mapping is validated before writes. Tests compare graph snapshots and audit
events after rejection, including mixed valid/invalid pairs with a pending finalizer
instruction and proposed new tasks. Valid deduplication still redirects prerequisites
and dependents, preserves history and acknowledges the instruction in one batch.
This validates structure, not the model's judgment that two tasks mean the same thing.


### DF39. Concern feedback misses current ticket replies and conflates words with decline — FIXED locally

`concern_feedback._last_user_reply` required evidence author `reply`, while the result
recorder uses the tool name or `ask` during recovery. Current replies therefore reached
finalizers in prose but were omitted from concern feedback. Evidence now also stores
an explicit `user_reply` payload for actual ticket responses from the ticket tool or
recovery path, preserving ticket/question, text, exact choice meaning/scope and history.
Timeouts, creation receipts and arbitrary manager output are not attributed to users.
The reader selects by response/evidence time rather than node dictionary order.
Historical explicit `reply` records remain readable; no historical prose is parsed or
backfilled. Both done and abandoned concern outcomes journal the structured response.

Related confirmed bug: `apply_work_outcome` treated any nonempty user words on abandoned
work as a decline and parked the concern. That would turn acknowledgments into refusals
once missing text was restored. Only an explicit `decline` choice without qualifying
typed text uses that automatic path. Typed text, generic No/OK and other meanings are
preserved for the existing noticer to interpret; they do not imply permission, completion
or rejection. No new agent/model call. DF40 below subsequently adds durable delivery and visible handling history.


### DF40. Automatic completion loses concern feedback; noticer cannot see handling history — FIXED locally

Confirmed: `WorkStore._rollup` can finish a concern-linked work object without
calling `propagate_work_outcome`. Explicit closures alone attempted delivery, and
a register failure lost it. `_render_concern_summary` and `_render_closed_concern`
also omitted reinforcement journals, including user replies saved by DF39.
The 48-hour recent-items window could not substitute for lasting concern history.
Four-day addressing pressure then allowed a settled concern to resurface without
its handling context. The reported repeat checkup notices fit this failure mode;
the specific live event has not been reconstructed.

Fix: terminal transitions atomically enqueue a snapshot in `work_concern_feedback`.
Finalizer/explicit closure deliver after commit; evaluator prep recovers outstanding
receipts. Register receipts make partial delivery and crash retries idempotent.
Failed or unresolved deliveries remain pending. Generic WorkStore never starts a
noticer or touches the register. The register retains structured per-work outcomes,
finalizer summaries and attributed replies outside the bounded reinforcement journal;
active/addressing/closed projections expose this history and the journal.

Noticer Jinja instructions distinguish acknowledgment, user ownership, scheduling
and actual completion. Old observations/silence/age alone cannot justify another
notice; reopening requires new evidence, a new due occurrence or agreed follow-up.
Review pressure remains a decision request. `keep_active` now timestamps addressing
reviews: previously it reset only reinforcement counts, so the age test demanded
another disposition every tick. Dormant projection no longer mislabels all user
declines as accepted chronic conditions. Pressure instructions moved from Python
into the existing Jinja policy.

No historical backfill or live concern cleanup. A completed notification does not
automatically resolve the underlying need. Noticer triggering is best effort;
durable register delivery remains usable by its next scheduled run.


### DF41. Readiness skips lose the next Dayflow tick — FIXED locally

`DayflowScheduler._execute_tick` returned for incomplete setup or empty history
before reaching the finally block that schedules the next tick. The consumed
one-shot job was not replaced. Setup completion writes its flag without explicitly
poking Dayflow; progress then depended on an external event or restart.

Both skips now call the existing `_arm_ceiling_tick` helper: a 30-minute readiness
recheck, preserving any sooner scheduled tick and respecting scheduler stop. These
paths still do not invoke agents or arm work-node execution. Incomplete setup also
continues to skip the history query. No change to ordinary chat processing.

Regression coverage: both gates, repeated empty-history checks, preservation of a
sooner external wake, and no rearming after stop. Existing scheduling-failure and
work-job lifecycle tests remain applicable. No live restart or data changes.


### WO5 repair checkpoint — FIXED locally

The standalone driver now promotes ready main tasks, atomically claims them, runs
the existing discharge/result-recorder path, and invokes the standard finalizer
agent and writer against the caller's store. Recorded results awaiting judgment
are finalized before more work starts. Finalizer retry/replan/stop outcomes halt
this runner; it never invents a plan or retries a judged failure. Dependency/time/
external-event gates still apply and worker provenance is never dispatched.
Explicit-node calls support ready, preclaimed and recorded-result tasks.

The finalizer accepts an explicit store for isolated scenarios without calling the
global Dayflow store or concern delivery. Production control-node behavior stays
the same. The smoke scenario now verifies closed tasks, evidence, finalizer epochs
and goal completion; old done/verified/passed checks were not the current contract.
Regression tests fake only the worker/model boundaries; no real model run was made.


## Beliefs and KG audit — 2026-09-20

All findings below are OPEN; this pass changes audit documentation only. Full assessment, source links, design concerns and repair order: [Beliefs and KG audit](beliefs_kg_audit_2026-09-20.md).

### MEM1. Noticer belief updates are logged and counted, never applied — OPEN (P1)

The noticer schema and prompt permit belief_updates, and its routine reports their count. The writer reads that list but only counts it and includes the original output in the tick log; it never invokes a belief writer. The routine calls no subsequent belief-application stage. A user correction understood by the noticer can therefore fail to become memory.

Confirmed by call-chain inspection and a temporary-register/database probe: one reported update, no belief row. Apply through the normal belief writer with evidence and a durable delivery receipt; distinguish emitted, applied and failed counts.

Evidence: [app/assistant/subconscious/persist.py:169](../../app/assistant/subconscious/persist.py#L169).

### MEM2. Ticket outcomes are converted into stronger user-intent claims — OPEN (P1)

The aggregate collector maps expired to snoozed and emits “User snoozed/deferred ... without completing.” It also maps accepted to acceptance without reading the contextual response meaning, scope or typed text introduced by DF36. An acknowledgment can therefore become acceptance; absence of a reply becomes a claimed user decision. Thresholds use event count, not distinct days, despite the cross-day description.

Expiry misclassification reproduced with three expired tickets. Accepted-response ambiguity confirmed by the collector fields it reads. Use explicit response semantics, retain silence as silence, and separate receipt from commitment/completion.

Evidence: [belief_engine/pipeline/steps/collect_evidence.py:212](../../belief_engine/pipeline/steps/collect_evidence.py#L212).

### MEM3. Evidence polarity is assigned before the belief it is evidence for exists — OPEN (P1)

The updater attaches the source signal unchanged to every cited belief. rejects becomes contradict in the store, even when the belief is “the user does not want this suggestion.” That observation supports the negative preference. Conversely, a chronic daily-insight item defaults to confirms even when it contradicts the specific existing belief being updated. The output schema has evidence indices but no per-belief evidence relationship.

Reproduced a negative preference supported by rejection: recompute gives it zero support, positive contradiction and contested status. Valence must be a property of evidence-to-claim attachment, separate from the source event’s sentiment/action.

Evidence: [belief_engine/pipeline/steps/update_beliefs.py:62](../../belief_engine/pipeline/steps/update_beliefs.py#L62).

### MEM4. Reprocessing old evidence manufactures fresh observation metadata — OPEN (P2)

Evidence insertion deduplicates unchanged observations, but every upsert still increments observation_count and sets last_confirmed from the request. The nightly updater supplies today, including no_change with no new evidence. Live retrieval ranks by those fields. In addition, rolling ticket aggregates with overlapping constituent events produce new summaries and therefore new weighted evidence rows as the count/window changes.

Reproduced one evidence row but two observations and a new confirmation date. Exact duplicate evidence weight is protected; recency/frequency are not. Aggregate overlap is confirmed structurally, not measured in live data. Count new source observations and retain stable source-event identities; do not sum overlapping rollups as independent observations.

Evidence: [belief_engine/store/belief_store.py:316](../../belief_engine/store/belief_store.py#L316).

### MEM5. Owner corrections and suppression are not enforced at the write boundary — OPEN (P1)

The central upsert has no locked guard. The updater checks locks only while action is update/deprecate, then later converts create on an existing key into update. That path overwrites a locked statement. Other callers can also upsert it. Suppression deprecates a row; the archive later removes it from the live table, and creation does not consult an enduring suppression record or archive. Old evidence can recreate the same key under a new id.

Reproduced both the actual updater create-path lock bypass and archive/same-source recreation in temporary databases. Enforce owner authority transactionally in the shared writer and preserve suppression/correction tombstones independently of active rows.

Evidence: [belief_engine/store/belief_store.py:322](../../belief_engine/store/belief_store.py#L322).

### MEM6. A belief can commit before its evidence, and an embedding failure interrupts the evidence write — OPEN (P1)

upsert_belief commits the claim, performs the Chroma upsert, then inserts evidence in separate transactions. A failed embedding call leaves a committed active claim with no newly attached evidence. Recompute explicitly skips beliefs with no usable evidence, so it cannot subsequently age such a row out through this model. Empty/invalid evidence refs on create and reevaluator-created split rows are additional evidence-free paths.

Injected an embedding failure: the method raises, but an active row remains with no evidence. Commit claim/version and evidence atomically, then process a durable vector-sync job. Reject unsupported inference writes or represent their provenance/derivation explicitly.

Evidence: [belief_engine/store/belief_store.py:329](../../belief_engine/store/belief_store.py#L329).

### MEM7. Contested beliefs can fall out of the reevaluation work queue — OPEN (P1)

Reevaluation consumes only keys on the current run context. Recompute scans active rows and reloads persisted contested keys only when that run reports a new contested flip. Write-time dedup can mark another stored belief contested without adding its key to that context. Canonicalization can contest both sides after the reevaluator has already run. Failed reevaluations also lack an unconditional persisted-queue drain. Active-only retrieval then hides these beliefs while they wait.

Reproduced a persisted contested belief with no fresh flip: the next recompute/reevaluate sequence reports no contested beliefs and leaves it stranded. Drain persisted contested work independently of whether new conflicts were discovered, with bounded retries and visible failure state.

Evidence: [belief_engine/pipeline/steps/reevaluate_beliefs.py:72](../../belief_engine/pipeline/steps/reevaluate_beliefs.py#L72).

### MEM8. The main KG question-answering payload omits structured validity dates — FIXED locally (P1)

Both global and node-anchored ask_kg build node evidence containing label/type/description/aliases/optional attributes, omitting first-class start_date, end_date and observation fields. Edge evidence includes updated_at, a maintenance/write time rather than validity. The RAG agent is told to answer solely from that payload. Dates might occur incidentally in prose, but the authoritative temporal distinction can be unavailable. This finding concerns handle_ask_kg, not every KG reader: shared card/wiki bullets do render temporal information.

Confirmed by both payload builders and the shared ask_kg prompt; no live wrong answer claimed. Include structured validity, observation time, confidence and closure reason in every selected claim, and teach current-versus-historical interpretation.

Evidence: [app/assistant/lib/core_tools/kg_search/knowledge_graph_search.py:509](../../app/assistant/lib/core_tools/kg_search/knowledge_graph_search.py#L509).

### MEM9. One late re-observation can prevent KG state decay indefinitely — OPEN (P1)

The expiry anchor is start_date/first_observed/created_at plus the estimated duration. A last_observed timestamp at or after that fixed expected_end causes an unconditional skip. As time advances the same old timestamp continues satisfying the test. Re-observation does not establish a new expiry deadline here.

Reproduced a three-day state observed on day four and otherwise silent for roughly 296 days: it is still skipped as recent activity. Define renewed freshness relative to the latest genuine observation, without equating silence-based expiry with proof that an event actually ended.

Evidence: [app/assistant/pipelines/kg_maintenance_pipeline/step_state_decay.py:148](../../app/assistant/pipelines/kg_maintenance_pipeline/step_state_decay.py#L148).

### MEM10. A transient KG extraction failure is persisted outside the retry queue — OPEN (P2)

The pending-window query excludes every window with any extraction row. An extractor exception is caught and persisted as verdict=error, then counted as processed. The next run excludes that window, and the generic runner does not see the exception. Thus a transient model/provider error can permanently omit that window from automatic extraction until explicit repair.

Confirmed by exception, selection and pending-count paths; no automatic error requeue found in the reviewed live pipeline/routes. Retain failed work as retryable with bounded backoff, attempt count and an operator-visible dead-letter state.

Evidence: [app/assistant/pipelines/kg_pipeline/steps/critique_and_extract.py:45](../../app/assistant/pipelines/kg_pipeline/steps/critique_and_extract.py#L45).

### MEM11. Qualifiers and confidence/decay metadata have inconsistent contracts — OPEN (P2)

An update that omits conditions clears the stored conditions; no_change constructs such a request. The live retrieval API reads extraction-time confidence while the export prefers current_confidence_band. The evidence half_life_days_snapshot is stored with comments promising historical preservation, but recompute does not select it and instead applies the current belief kind to all evidence.

Qualifier erasure reproduced; confidence and half-life inconsistencies confirmed by reads. Preserve omitted qualifiers, expose a named effective-confidence contract, and decide explicitly whether reclassification recomputes historical decay or honors evidence snapshots.

Evidence: [belief_engine/store/belief_store.py:315](../../belief_engine/store/belief_store.py#L315).

### MEM12. Write-time dedup lacks the incoming evidence dates and can admit known-stale beliefs as active — OPEN (P2)

The merge verifier gets the stored belief’s dated trail but only a generic new-statement context for the incoming side. The actual incoming evidence is resolved later. If the verifier nevertheless says the stored side is current, the code deliberately proceeds to write the incoming stale side, ordinarily active, relying on a future sweep. Agent readers can see both meanwhile.

Confirmed by dedup input construction and the supersedes/current_side branch. Pass both source-dated trails before deciding; retain historical claims as historical, not competing current guidance. This is separate from ordinary semantic near-match uncertainty.

Evidence: [belief_engine/pipeline/steps/update_beliefs.py:151](../../belief_engine/pipeline/steps/update_beliefs.py#L151).

### MEM13. Belief pipeline success can conceal per-belief failures — OPEN (P2)

The pipeline marks a step successful whenever it returns without raising. UpdateBeliefsStep catches per-belief errors and returns partial_error; recompute and reevaluation also carry error counters. These do not change the pipeline’s overall success, so the adapter can report success and export a partially updated set.

Confirmed by step and adapter contracts. Preserve partial completion but propagate partial/error health, count outstanding repairs and avoid calling the entire run successful.

Evidence: [belief_engine/pipeline/pipeline.py:108](../../belief_engine/pipeline/pipeline.py#L108).

### MEM14. Additional audit discrepancies — OPEN / scope limited

Jinja-only prompt policy still conflicts with instruction strings in belief updater/
reevaluator, ask_kg and context_activation Python. Belief docs overstate incoming
dedup evidence, current-confidence consistency and historical half-life preservation;
routine JSON still describes per-domain execution. KG docs overstate the promoter's
exclusive write role and general reopening on re-observation. See the report's
additional-discrepancies section for evidence and unresolved policy boundaries.

KG runner classifies all sqlite3.OperationalError exceptions as lock retries;
non-lock operational faults need a different recovery policy. Belief evidence
dedup is check-then-insert across sessions with no matching unique constraint in
the reviewed ORM, leaving a concurrency race (inspection, not stress-tested).
No fixes, live cleanup, commit or push were performed in this audit.


### MEM15. Weekly belief candidates bypass belief reconciliation — OPEN (integration gap, P2)

The weekly synthesizer explicitly emits belief_candidates with confidence, conditions
and evidence for incorporation into the belief engine. The active v1 collector reads
14 days of daily insights and ticket aggregates only; no weekly candidate ingestion
was found. The Dayflow routine writer instead receives the first three weekly
candidates directly, reduced to domain and statement. Thus weekly findings can
influence guidance without stored evidence, reconciliation or belief decay, and
separate candidate conditions/confidence/evidence are omitted from that input.
This is a confirmed code-path gap relative to the user's intended architecture,
not a claim that a particular live weekly candidate caused a wrong action.

Evidence: weekly_insights/steps/synthesize_weekly_insights.py; agents/weekly_synthesizer/
agent_form.py; belief_engine/pipeline/steps/collect_evidence.py;
pipelines/dayflow/steps/dayflow_routine_stage.py::_format_weekly_insights.
Direction: route weekly candidates through the existing belief reconciliation path,
preserving their underlying daily evidence identities to avoid double counting.

### MEM14 follow-up: upstream prompt and consumer projection discrepancies

Daily insight system.j2 describes structured extracted_claim kinds and non-decaying
preferences from the v2 design. configs/subsystems.yaml has beliefs_v2_primary=false;
the active v1 daily collector ignores extracted_claim, using summary, first evidence
string, tags and temporal_scope. Its stable preferences have a 365-day half-life.
The reevaluator prompt promises every historical signal, while its Python formatter
selects only the latest 50 evidence records and truncates each raw quote to 200 chars.
These are confirmed contract discrepancies; neither establishes a live wrong belief.

The Dayflow routine/health formatter projects only domain, confidence, statement and
conditions. It omits exported belief keys, dates and counts. Orchestrator evaluator,
architect and state_mover receive the generated resource_dayflow_routine. This is a
context/provenance design limitation to address when solidifying architecture, not
proof that all possible consumers lack direct access to belief resources.


### MEM16. Merge bookkeeping becomes fresh supporting evidence and hides original lineage from ordinary readers — OPEN (P1)

`BeliefStore.merge_belief` appends a `canonicalization` row dated at the merge,
with signal `confirms` and weight `2.0 * len(deprecated_keys)`. It also advances
last_confirmed and transfers observation counters. `decay/recompute.py` prefers a
nonzero stored weight over the zero baseline for canonicalization, so these merge
rows contribute support despite the comment saying bookkeeping is ignored. Semantic
consolidation can therefore manufacture fresh support without a new user observation.
This extends MEM4 with a distinct, confirmed producer of synthetic reinforcement.

The survivor gets a shortened merge summary; original evidence remains attached to
the deprecated predecessor, eventually in the archive. Ordinary get_evidence and
reevaluation do not traverse those predecessor links. The copy-only pilot adds explicit
archived predecessor inspection without assuming the historical merge was correct.

Frozen database inspection on 2026-09-20 found 606 current belief rows: 174 had attached
evidence consisting only of canonicalization records; 3 had no attached evidence.
Of 3,732 current evidence rows, 2,631 were canonicalization records, with total stored
weight 5,386. These counts do not establish that the beliefs are false: original evidence
may survive elsewhere. They establish that bookkeeping is being presented as support
and that direct evidence readers can lack the original source history.

No repair to the production merger or decay path was made in this experiment. Keep
bookkeeping separate from observations, follow provenance without assigning semantic
equivalence, and preserve originals even when a merge is eventually authorized.

### Contextual review pilot (2026-09-20)

Implemented an opt-in copy-only review path under `belief_engine/review`, with standard
`belief_engine::context_search` and `belief_engine::context_review` agents, Jinja prompts
and structured forms. LLM-selected context can expand to exact archived predecessor IDs
and original master-room chat/ticket source days. No merge operation exists. Revisions
and their source-linked explanations commit with before/after receipts; source dates,
counts and legacy evidence weights are unchanged. Runtime pipeline behavior is unchanged.

The seven-belief, three-case authorized model evaluation produced four proposed changes
and three preservations on a copied database. This is exploratory model behavior, not
proof of correctness or production readiness. Integrity tests cover rollback, replay,
locks, stale context/evidence, citations, archive inspection and bounded investigation.
Personal source material and detailed results remain outside the repository. See
`belief_engine/review/README.md` for scope and explicit production-integration limits.


### MEM14/MEM15 projection follow-up (2026-09-20) — projection repaired; upstream gaps OPEN

The routine writer now receives belief keys, conditions, kind, confidence and observation
dates, with instructions to retain citations. Health formatting and full source-evidence
retrieval are unchanged. Weekly projection now retains all candidates with conditions,
confidence, evidence and the historical date range. This repairs qualifier loss and the
arbitrary first-three limit; weekly ingestion/reconciliation into beliefs remains open.

### MEM17. Contextual beliefs excluded and resolved guidance cached — FIXED (2026-09-20)

The routine stage admitted only certain kinds or routing tags, excluding potentially
relevant preferences in other domains. Its fingerprint ignored current status,
milestones and weekly context, so completed preparation could leave obsolete guidance.
_read_daily_context also ignored an explicitly empty authoritative expected_schedule,
retaining cancelled events from older daily context. Writer instructions and its output
schema conflicted over past/completed activity and did not preserve belief identifiers.

Repair: standard LLM selector reads the full active export; Python validates returned
identities without semantic classification. Writer uses original selected records,
qualifiers and citations, places preparation before events, and suppresses resolved asks.
The cache includes all contextual inputs, and an authoritative empty calendar clears the
old schedule. Prompt behavior lives in Jinja. Existing hourly eligibility remains;
ordinary chat performs no new model calls. A changed projection adds one selector call.

Verification: isolated unit checks cover context invalidation, unknown keys, cache reuse,
empty calendars and qualifiers. Synthetic model cases placed lesson preparation at 16:00
for a 17:00 event, moved it to 11:00 for noon, and suppressed another reminder after explicit
packing/delivery evidence. These examples are not a guarantee of model correctness.
See contextual_belief_projection_2026-09-20.md. No live resources or belief rows regenerated.


### MEM18. Candidate discovery has narrow coverage and inconsistent score contracts — OPEN (P2)

UpdateBeliefsStep.run concatenates the entire evidence batch into one embedding query,
then shows the updater at most eight hits. dedup_candidate searches only three neighbors
and stops at the first same verdict. Neither query embeds separate conditions or evidence.
BeliefStore.find_similar filters status after the limited Chroma query, so inactive hits
can consume its small candidate budget without refill. These are confirmed coverage
limitations; the number of missed duplicates in the live catalog has not been measured.

The weekly canonicalizer compares normalized-vector cosine >=0.80, plus shared stemmed
English words appearing in 2–12 beliefs with cosine >=0.50. Write-time dedup reuses the
0.80 constant against Chroma's 1/(1+distance), a different scoring contract unless the
configured distance happens to make it equivalent. Actual collection metric/recall was
not measured in this audit. The weekly pass excludes beliefs lacking embeddings.
Evidence: update_beliefs.py::_SIMILAR_K/_DEDUP_K/run/dedup_candidate;
canonicalize_belief_set.py::_embeddings_for/_keyword_pairs/_run_verifier_dedup_pass;
belief_chroma.py::search; belief_store.py::find_similar.

### MEM19. Pair decisions omit qualifiers and cache incomplete judgments — OPEN (P2)

belief_context_block supplies six recent evidence summaries, truncated to 160 characters,
plus dates/kind/domain/counts. It omits structured conditions, scope and original source
text; evidence lookup errors become an empty-history block. The updater's existing-belief
formatter omits these qualifiers too. A same verdict can consequently ignore conditions
held outside the statement. merge_belief does not pass conditions to upsert_belief, which
clears them on conflict: this extends MEM11 to confirmed merge-time qualifier loss.

Distinct-pair cache invalidation hashes only statement text, ignoring changes to evidence,
conditions, scope and verifier policy. An unusable verifier relation is coerced to different
and can be durably cached, suppressing a later valid review. Context-dependent distinctions
can therefore remain unreviewed after their basis changes. No live wrong merge established.
The verifier also lacks an explicit insufficient-context outcome; its fuller-version merge
instruction needs clarification against its specialises rule.
Evidence: canonicalize_belief_set.py::belief_context_block/verify_relation;
store/distinct_pairs.py; merge_verifier/prompts/system.j2; store/belief_store.py::merge_belief.

### MEM20. Weekly pair backstop can silently omit the remaining candidates — OPEN (P2)

_run_verifier_dedup_pass truncates sorted pairs to MAX_PAIRS=4000 before cached distinct
pairs are skipped. Unlike its 400-model-call limit, this truncation does not set truncated.
A sufficiently large candidate set can repeatedly revisit the same capped prefix and
stamp a full sweep complete without reaching the tail. Confirmed control flow; not measured
as an active problem at the current catalog size. Missing embeddings similarly are not
reported as incomplete coverage. No runtime repair performed during this audit.


### Incremental matching repair (2026-09-20)

MEM18/MEM20: replaced live embedding/keyword admission and capped weekly pair sweeps with
LLM catalog discovery, per-input page receipts, versioned pair queues and explicit pending
counts. No unchanged-night pair comparisons. Legacy helpers remain historical only.
MEM19/MEM12: reviewers receive full stored evidence, conditions and source dates after new
records are persisted; malformed answers remain pending, unresolved is explicit, and cache
versions include evidence/qualifiers/policy. Complete records replace 160-character/six-row
context limits; reevaluation's 50-row/200-character limits are removed as well.
MEM16: new atomic merges produce no supporting observation; complete before/after receipts
and redirects preserve evidence, which ordinary readers and decay now traverse through live
and archived predecessors. Exact and explicitly reviewed same-source observations count once.
Legacy bookkeeping no longer contributes weight. Existing historical mistakes are not reversed.
MEM11: merge conditions are explicit; updater no-change and absent conditions preserve the
stored qualifiers. Remaining confidence/half-life issues are unchanged.
MEM7: reevaluation now includes persisted contested records, including prior-run matching
conflicts. MEM13: pipeline propagates reported per-step errors and includes step results so
pending matching work is visible. MEM5: updater lock check now includes create-on-existing.

Five additive matching tables are initialized by production first use, not this code edit.
Tests use isolated databases/fake agents. Ordinary chat has no matching calls. Oversized model
input fails pending without truncation; multi-call investigation of a single oversized packet
and source documents absent from stored evidence remain explicit limitations. No live database
cleanup, restart, commit or push accompanies this implementation.


### MEM21. Full lineage repeated across candidate-discovery pages — FIXED locally (2026-09-20)

Fresh dry-run preparation counted 579 active beliefs, 8 catalog pages and 454 focal pages,
requiring 3,632 discovery calls before review because every focal contained all archived
evidence. Discovery does not decide merges; it now uses complete belief records, retaining
entire statements/conditions, while the decisive pair reviewer still reads full evidence.
Evidence-aware version invalidation remains intact. This repairs an implementation cost
problem discovered before spending model calls; no production belief rows were changed.


### Belief source accounting follow-up (2026-09-20)

MEM4: ordinary upsert/no-change rereads now derive counts and dates from original sources in the same transaction. Processing time no longer reconfirms claims. Overlapping ticket rollups remain OPEN.

MEM5: central upsert now honors owner locks. Suppression tombstones across archival remain OPEN.

MEM6: claim and complete evidence batch now commit atomically before indexing. Injected evidence failure rolls back both; index failure retains both and raises. A durable ordinary-write index repair outbox and historical provenance repair remain OPEN.

MEM11: omitted conditions are preserved; explicit valence governs confirmation; decay honors saved half-lives (-1 explicitly means durable, legacy NULL uses kind). Zero weight stays zero; undated/missing evidence produces unverified snapshots without deprecating the claim. Legacy missing policy/date information remains unknown.

MEM15: global collection now reconciles complete weekly candidates through the updater with zero independent support. Dayflow no longer injects candidates around reconciliation. Daily source refs are unchanged to avoid migration duplicates.

New confirmed extension to MEM4/MEM11: `flag_stale_chronic_beliefs` writes `decay_review` bookkeeping with a nonzero historical weight. It is now excluded from observations and decay alongside merge/deprecation receipts. Staleness is not new evidence about the user.

Verification details and remaining work: [belief_source_accounting_2026-09-20.md](belief_source_accounting_2026-09-20.md). Copy-only provenance audit: 131 active claims lack reachable original observations; none has a same-key archived candidate carrying original observations. No live data changed.


### Evidence interpretation follow-up (2026-09-20)

MEM2/MEM3: rolling ticket context now preserves complete dated records rather than deriving refusal/snooze/acceptance meanings from transport state. The updater explicitly assigns support/contradict/qualify to each cited source for the particular claim. Missing/invalid relationships fail the write instead of falling back to source sentiment. Deprecation attaches cited original evidence before the bookkeeping transition. Existing incorrectly assigned historical valences are not rewritten automatically.

MEM4: new rolling ticket windows carry source_type ticket_context and zero independent weight/count. They can inform the LLM alongside original daily observations without counting the same underlying events again each time a window changes. Existing legacy weighted rollups remain a provenance review concern; no speculative historical rewrite was performed.

Updater prompts now preserve uncertain duplicates and remove rules that inferred pacing preferences from expiration counts. Full replies and qualifiers are retained. 64 isolated tests passed, including negative evidence supporting a negative claim, invalid citation rejection, and changed ticket windows with no added support.


### MEM22. Discovery rejects valid supplied focal pairs and stalls on self-pairs — FIXED (P2)

The full copied-catalog evaluation produced 527 self-pairs and 147 pairs rejected by the original focal-to-catalog-only validator. The model can legitimately compare two supplied focal records. Self-pairs carry no work. Discovery now validates that both IDs were supplied, normalizes orientation when one is focal, drops self-pairs, and deduplicates exact pairs. Valid pairs within either supplied set are safe candidates; unknown IDs still fail. This changes identity validation only; the reviewer still decides semantic relationships. No live belief changes.

Discovery also produced 34 unknown IDs and five pairs without a focal record. An invalid page gets one bounded retry using short page-local labels, mapped back by Python. Invalid retry outputs still leave discovery pending. No semantic fuzzy ID repair is performed.


### MEM23. Model citation transcription errors stop an otherwise valid comparison queue — FIXED (P2)

The full copy-only evaluation exposed invalid evidence UUIDs in review results. The validator correctly refused them but had no bounded correction path. Review now validates source citations and observation-equivalence groups before application and retries once using short exact source labels. Python maps labels back to original IDs; it never uses fuzzy matching. Invalid retries leave the work pending. No evidence text is shortened.

### MEM24. A reviewer can mistake a compound superset for an equivalent belief — FIXED with conservative LLM merge check (P1)

In the copied catalog, a general research preference was proposed for absorption into a recipient-specific research-and-delivery rule. Shared source material and nonconflicting additions were being treated as equivalence. A separate standard merge_check agent now inspects both complete claims, sources, and proposed wording for changed applicability, added independent obligations, exceptions, and permissions. Only valid explicit approval permits a merge. A negative or invalid approval preserves both claims as unresolved, retaining the complete proposal, reasoning, and diagnostics. The check interprets context, including harmless paraphrases; Python enforces the result. This adds calls only for proposed merges in the background belief pipeline.

### MEM25. Oversized evidence investigations cannot progress without a single large prompt — FIXED with bounded continuation (P2)

Large packets now receive full-coverage source-page reading by a standard evidence_page agent, with exact fragment IDs and durable per-page receipts. All serialized source text is partitioned losslessly. The final reviewer and merge checker receive complete claims and every page finding. Missing coverage is refused. If the complete findings still exceed the final budget, the comparison stays pending without clipping. Cross-fragment uncertainty prevents a confident merge. Whole-packet review remains the path for ordinary-sized records.

Copy-only provenance investigation additionally found 81 of the 131 missing-source claims have potential original-source records explicitly named in old merge summaries. These are retrieval candidates, not automatically restored evidence; incorrect historical splits/merges require source-based review.


### MEM26. Superseding one clause can discard a compound belief's independent rule — FIXED with retirement check (P1)

Copy-only review found an old lighting record with separate on/off schedules. A dated off-time correction established replacement of that clause, but deprecating the whole old record would also remove the on-time clause. The independent final check now covers `supersedes` as well as `same`. It requires dated source support and preservation of independently valid clauses; partial replacement stays unresolved for a source-based split/revision. No live record was retired during evaluation.


#### MEM6 historical provenance investigation — copy-only review

The new opt-in provenance proposal service distinguishes historical retrieval leads
from source-to-claim attribution. Old compound predecessors can contain sources that
are irrelevant to a surviving split claim. In the frozen baseline, a browser-automation
news-site request appears among a timesheet-trigger predecessor's evidence; it does not
establish when a timesheet job may run. Some daily-insight summaries also repeat a broad
claim before a much narrower user quote. Recovery must assess the original source and
its interpretation limits instead of treating the repeated claim as fresh support.
The service reviews each full source against the full current claim, preserving partial
support as qualify and ambiguity as insufficient. It proposes selective original-ID
links only; no live source links, observation counts, weights or dates are changed.
Historical repair remains OPEN pending reviewed application and source-event accounting.

MEM23 extension: provenance search can also return out-of-page record IDs. The proposal
service validates exact supplied identities, retries once with concrete validation
errors, and rejects an invalid retry. Complete source coverage is required for source
attribution. Validated full-context receipts permit unchanged pages to skip model calls.
Nine isolated tests cover identity rejection, complete coverage, bounded repair,
source-content preservation, receipt integrity and context-based invalidation.

Provenance identity follow-up: some search outputs used an explicitly supplied belief key instead of its short ID. Exact unique keys now resolve to that record; duplicate historical keys remain invalid. This is identity lookup only, never semantic matching. Tests cover both unique and ambiguous keys.

MEM6 investigation limitation: source-review spot checks sometimes cited the predecessor
claim as proof of added clauses despite the prompt's warning. The copy-only evaluation
therefore independently checks proposed full support/contradiction using the complete
source without predecessor claim text. No links are applied automatically. Many current
statements also combine facts with derived assistant instructions; partial source support
is not evidence that those instructions are wrong. Keep their inferential status visible.
MEM14 historical-data extension: at least one stored manual-seed source excerpt is already
cut off mid-sentence. Passing its full stored contents cannot recover missing original text;
this remains a coverage limit rather than permission to invent the omitted source.


### MEM27. Copy-only provenance evaluator regrouped unchanged sources across restarts — FIXED in evaluation runner (P2)

The evaluation script iterated a set of candidate record IDs while building source pages.
Python hash ordering changed across processes, so identical source sets could form different
pages and miss the otherwise correct page cache. This affects the private evaluation runner,
not the nightly matching service. Candidate IDs and source IDs are now sorted. Completed
judgments were recovered by exact current-claim/source context into a per-source index,
retaining original full page receipts. Disagreements remain pending instead of selecting an
arbitrary result. Recovery made zero model calls. Future continuation must schedule only
missing indexed sources/checks, not rerun the complete archive.

The copied-data review was interrupted by API credit exhaustion: 20,159 of 20,762 source
relationships have a completed initial review; 121 of 131 claims have at least one relevant
reviewed source (often partial). Remaining: 603 initial reviews, 31 source-only checks and
155 conflicting judgments. No live evidence, weights, counts or dates were changed.


#### MEM6/MEM27 provenance continuation after credit top-up

The missing 603 source relationships and 31 source-only checks are now reviewed. The
copied baseline has 20,762/20,762 initial source assessments and relevant source proposals
for 122/131 claims (often partial). The 155 reviewer disagreements span four beliefs.
Repeating continuation schedules zero model jobs and preserves the review-index hash.
No live claim, evidence, confidence, count or date changed.

Additional source-review limitation (MEM6): a reviewed venue quote was described as a
grocery-list record, apparently importing predecessor context instead of reading the actual
source. Disagreement with another review preserved it for inspection. A source-only check
also flagged general proactive nudging as contradicting a narrower hydration cadence; the
quoted general rule does not establish that conflict. This is held for contextual review.
Positive-only source checks do not establish correctness of all negative or partial labels.
Review-policy finding: demanding separate evidence for an ordinary explicit-user-override
clause is overly literal; reasonable operational guidance must not be mistaken for an
invented factual preference. Five whole-belief cases are prepared for review, not application.

The 22:19:14 strategic_planner_wo output in logs/emi_logs_35600_agents.log abandoned

records the device goals dropped, not completed. Offline delay alone should not establish

DayflowScheduler._arm_work_node_wakes can arm overdue eligible nodes for now plus a small
delay, but skips abandoned work, so scheduler catch-up cannot rescue the dropped objective.

execution before suppressing a current device action. Preserve bounded retries.
