### MEM28. KG processing batches become irreversible context boundaries — OPEN (P1)

Inspection 2026-09-20: segment_messages.py::_load_day_to_segment loads only resolved, unsegmented messages from one local date. run_one_cycle permits segmentation at six ready messages even while resolution is incomplete, or at any positive count once the resolver is caught up. Catching up is not a conversation-completion signal. Previously segmented context is excluded on subsequent passes; _persist_windows always writes related_previous_window_ids=None. resolve_messages.py also restricts overlap to ten already-resolved messages within the same date. Consequently worker timing and midnight can separate a reply from its antecedent, with no continuation repair in this path. Intended: processing pages preserve access to prior context and provisional tails; semantic conversation boundaries are model judgments. Confirmed structural limitation, not a measured live error rate.

### MEM29. Boundary output is grouped without validating its semantic range contract — OPEN (P1)

segment_messages.py::_collapse_to_windows groups individual output entries by (start ID, end ID, should_process), but actual membership is only the IDs explicitly present in those entries. It does not validate complete one-time coverage, endpoint existence/order, or consistent membership across ranges. _persist_windows replaces proposed endpoints with first/last listed members. Omitted messages remain pending and may later be processed without their earlier context; duplicated message entries can fail the membership uniqueness constraint. should_process is stored as standalone, which critique_and_extract.py never consults. A requested drop/context-insufficient decision is therefore not enforced or routed for context repair. Intended: validate identity/coverage atomically, represent context sufficiency separately from extraction eligibility, and preserve explicit context links. Code-path finding; no malformed model response injected in this audit.

### MEM30. KG critique can discard useful contextual user statements — OPEN (P1)

knowledge_graph_add/window_critic_v2/prompts/system.j2 excludes user instructions even when they contain strong autobiographical information, and calls the assistant side irrelevant. The caller supplies assistant turns as a separate block without their original interleaving. This can prevent accurate interpretation of short replies and discard facts embedded in requests. critique_and_extract.py::run_one_cycle also skips the entire window when every user line merely contains the substring '(unknown)', even if those lines contain other clear facts. Intended: interpret full ordered exchanges, distinguish factual clauses from requests, and defer only unresolved claims rather than blanket-dropping the source. Prompt-policy mismatch and deterministic rejection risk confirmed; individual semantic mistakes remain unmeasured. Its malformed dict response path can also produce a permanent rejected extraction from missing/invalid extractable_lines instead of surfacing invalid output for retry.

Confirmed instance, 2026-10-05 (found while tracing a stale entity card): on 2025-11-01 the owner wrote "[the assistant] some
big changes. I now meet with [the friend] on mondays at 8pm. find the calendar event for [the friend] on thursdays and
delete that." Window `ddd2ab0a` was rejected: "All user lines are administrative scheduling instructions/status, not
extractable autobiographical claims" (its own summary: "move the friend's meeting from Thursday 8pm to Monday 8pm");
window `f70b32c7` (2025-11-06, "Delete reminders (scheduler) for [the friend] on Thursdays") likewise. The Thursday State
from 2025-01-02 stands; the friend's card still says Thursday (see "A friend's entity card says Thursday", 2026-10-05).

### MEM31. Daily insight raw-chat input omits assistant context — OPEN (P1)

ArchiveDailyTimelineStep.run selects only role=user rows for master_room and emits those raw messages into timeline_merged.json. ArchiveDailyInsightsStep passes that timeline to daily_timeline_insights without loading KG conversation windows or attaching matching assistant turns. Other normalized context/ticket entries may provide some context, but do not guarantee the exact question or proposal a short answer refers to. Thus fixing KG segmentation alone will not repair daily insight interpretation. The raw-chat query also silently caps at 10,000 rows without pagination; a larger day loses the remaining raw chats. Intended: preserve complete attributable exchanges and source identities for daily insights; page by identity without dropping source content. Inspection finding, no claim that the cap was reached live.

#### MEM14 / MEM10 follow-up: chat boundary audit (2026-09-20)

Boundary and critique persistence still truncate reasoning to 1,000/240 characters in segment_messages.py and critique_and_extract.py, contrary to the no-truncation requirement. ResolveMessagesStep's module docstring says ten new messages, while BATCH_NEW_SIZE is twenty. KG architecture eligibility still lists removed kg_maintenance_resolution and omits accepted empty room IDs. ArchiveDailyInsightsStep and ArchiveDailyTimelineStep still prepare behavioral task instructions as Python string literals rather than Jinja. MEM10's terminal extraction-error behavior remains present. SegmentMessagesStep additionally catches model exceptions and returns zero, hiding quota/provider failures from the runner's halt classification while pending_count can keep the work outstanding. These are recorded for later repair; this audit made no runtime changes or model evaluations.
#### MEM23 live extension: belief selection auto-disables two routines — OPEN (2026-09-21)

Process 35600 logs and resource_routine_status.json confirm belief_engine failed at update_beliefs at 00:31:55, 00:34:14, and 00:37:31 PDT, then auto-disabled. select_for_evidence (belief_engine/matching/service.py) rejects any returned belief_key outside the supplied catalog page with a generic ValueError. Unlike discover() in the same file, this selection path has no compact identity labels or bounded validation-repair retry. Valid pages are cached and retries advanced, but each attempt ultimately encountered another invalid selection. The logs do not identify the actual offending keys for this path; a typo versus an out-of-page reference cannot be distinguished from this error alone.

Dayflow's _select_beliefs (app/assistant/pipelines/dayflow/steps/dayflow_routine_stage.py) independently has the same fragile exact-key output contract and no validation-repair retry. Three consecutive failures at 05:00:51, 05:02:46, and 05:05:48 PDT disabled dayflow_pipeline. Logged unknown selections include home.nest.default_night_schedule_70f_9pm_75f_6am and general.work_hours.protected_window. These are absent from the supplied eligible catalog according to the validator, not proof of absence from all historical beliefs. The 05:05 selector request was logged at approximately 119,733 input tokens; prompt size is a cost/context concern, not an established cause of invalid output. Suggested repair: exact request-local ID handles, preserved complete context, bounded model correction on invalid selection, durable valid progress; never fuzzy-map semantic keys or silently discard potentially relevant selections.

Both routines use default max_failures=3, exponential backoff, disable_with_ticket, and auto_retry_after_seconds=0. The watchdog behaved as configured. The disabled dayflow_pipeline is the background context/guidance producer; dayflow_orchestrator_manager and dispatch were still active at 07:28. Daily insights completed at 00:06:57. Morning briefing was already disabled on September 13, not a new overnight shutdown.

Diagnostic follow-up: PipelineRoutineRunner reports only top-level failed_steps, while this Dayflow failure surfaces as generic 'pipeline status=error' in the status/ticket. Preserve the failed stage and underlying validation reason in user-facing routine diagnostics. Investigation only: no re-enable, code repair, database writes, or provider calls performed.
#### MEM23 selector repair — FIXED on disk; runtime recovery pending restart (2026-09-21)

Both evidence_match and dayflow_belief_selector now return request-local selection_id handles in belief_ids, mapped exactly to the supplied original records by belief_engine/matching/selection.py. No semantic key guessing or text truncation. Invalid schemas/IDs receive one correction request carrying the complete original input, prior response, validation error, and allowed IDs; repeated invalid results still fail explicitly. Provider errors propagate. Prompts and correction instructions live in each agent's Jinja templates; both config files declare selection_retry. Evidence-selection cache identity was versioned and includes the output schema; only valid canonical-key results are cached.

Verification: 19 focused tests passed, including both production callers, invalid-ID correction, persistent invalid response rejection, full qualifier preservation, and zero-call cache reuse. Isolated real-model checks through the configured provider succeeded: Dayflow selected 29 valid records from the complete 579-entry exported catalog; evidence matching selected 10 valid records from an 84-entry catalog page with copied original evidence. These checks prove the selection paths execute successfully, not correctness of every relevance judgment or completion of a full nightly pipeline. No live belief writes or routine execution occurred. Tests use isolated service registration rather than live bootstrap.

The running app process still has the old Python modules loaded. Leave the two routines disabled until restart loads the matching code/schema/prompts together; then enable them using RoutineManager's supported admin route and verify full runs. No restart, live routine toggle, commit, or push performed here. Generic Dayflow error-message propagation remains a separate OPEN diagnostic follow-up.
#### MEM25 live extension: updater reassembles paged selection into an oversized request — OPEN (P1, 2026-09-21)

After the MEM23 selector repair, live run d62cc274 reached belief_engine::belief_updater at 07:59:37 PDT in process 22080. The client estimated 910,365 input tokens and the provider rejected the request with context_length_exceeded at 07:59:43. update_beliefs.py::run serializes the entire EvidenceBundle plus encode(select_for_evidence(...)) into one updater call. select_for_evidence pages discovery, then returns full packet() records for every selected belief, including complete lineage/evidence. Discovery paging therefore does not bound the final updater prompt. Existing paged comparison review does not cover this production update path. The failed call occurred before this step's mutation loop; selection cache writes may already exist. Dayflow routine run 3d50a3d5 succeeded at 07:59:29, so this is a distinct downstream belief-update failure, not evidence that local selection IDs failed.

Required repair: bounded update investigations with complete source coverage, original evidence-reference mapping, preserved cross-page qualifiers/contradictions, and final whole-belief adjudication before mutation. Do not truncate evidence, drop selected beliefs, or naively perform destructive updates independently per page. Verify complete nightly flow on an isolated database copy, including updater, reconciliation and export, before claiming recovery. The preceding 19 tests and isolated selector evaluations did not cover updater prompt assembly at production scale. No runtime fix made in this investigation.

### MEM32. Dayflow writer failure can be reported as a successful step — FIXED (P2)

Read during the projection audit: DayFlowRoutineStep.run returns
StepResult(output={status:error,...}) when _call_agent returns no markdown. The common
step_runner.run_once only applies state_updates and records success for any non-raising
step, ignoring output.status. Thus a failed writer can advance step scheduling and appear
healthy without refreshed guidance. Intended: propagate an explicit stage failure.
This is an inspected contract defect, not evidence that the observed 07:59 successful
Dayflow run actually took this failure branch. Fixed: an empty/failed writer now raises before state updates. A regression covers the failure contract.

### Chat tab takeover fails to disconnect the displaced socket — FIXED (2026-09-21)

Observed in live PID5944 logs at 09:38:16 and 09:38:54: chat registration binds the
new socket, then app/socket_handlers.py calls socketio.disconnect(displaced).
The Flask-SocketIO instance has no such method, raising AttributeError and leaving
the old transport connected. The preceding socket_hijacked notification can succeed;
the warning's wording did not establish notification failure or duplicate delivery.
Fixed by calling socketio.server.disconnect with the displaced SID and request namespace.
The disconnect handler uses unbind_by_socket_id; bind already removed the displaced
SID's reverse mapping, so cleanup preserves the newly registered room owner.
Regression uses two real Flask-SocketIO test clients and the production handlers. It
reproduced the old connected socket before the fix, then checks disconnection,
takeover notification, delivery to the new client, repeated registration and final cleanup.
Validation: all 10 socket ownership tests passed. No live app restart or push performed.
The test run also exposed asynchronous logging writing to pytest capture after it closed
(ValueError in UnicodeStreamHandler.emit); this is a separate test teardown issue,
left open. A requests dependency-version warning also appeared; no HTTP failure was observed.


#### MEM8 controlled wrong-answer demonstration — 2026-09-21

Reproduced with fictional KG retrieval rows and the unmodified production global
handle_ask_kg evidence builder plus configured shared::ask_kg model (gpt-5.6-luna).
Ground truth: vacation state valid September 1–5, 2026; edge maintenance timestamp
September 21; question asks whether the vacation applies on September 21.
Current builder omits node validity dates. Real answer: "Yes. The evidence states
that Morgan Vale is on vacation and unavailable for work meetings. The record was
updated on September 21, 2026." Model confidence: 1.0.
Second call used the identical evidence plus the stored node start_date/end_date,
without changing the answering prompt. It correctly identified the expired vacation
and noted that no separate current unavailability was established (confidence 0.94).
This is a controlled synthetic reproduction, not a demonstrated wrong answer from
live personal KG records. Search/SQL retrieval used fixed fixture rows; payload
construction and model execution were real. Neither production code nor live data
was changed. Original payloads and responses are saved in the task workspace under
kg-validity-demo/. The paired calls demonstrate the failure mechanism, not a statistical
reliability estimate or proof that every expired fact produces a wrong answer.


#### MEM8 repair and verification — 2026-09-21

Both ask_kg builders now use a shared node projection including exact/prose validity
dates, date confidence, observation timestamps/count, valid_currently, validity_reason,
goal_status and claim confidence/tier. Edge maintenance timestamps and confidence are
explicit. The anchored base-node projection uses the same fields. Full descriptions
are preserved instead of the former 400-character truncation. No time-based filtering
was added: answering semantics are in shared/ask_kg Jinja prompts. A record edit is not
reconfirmation; missing dates do not establish current validity; an expired state does
not prove its opposite; decay closure is distinguished from a confirmed ending.
The static task instruction moved out of Python into the user Jinja template.
Eight regression cases cover both retrieval modes with expired, future, uncertain and
undated nodes, including complete descriptions and confidence/closure preservation.
All passed. The original real-model fictional vacation example was rerun through the
installed fixed builder and prompt: it now states that the record does not establish
current vacation/unavailability and explicitly rejects updated_at as proof of current
validity. This is a synthetic model check, not an exhaustive answer-quality guarantee.
No schema migration or KG content rewrite is needed; restart loads the Python changes.

no extra model calls or ordinary chat-path changes. Regression coverage checks
window/source/room/direction filtering, complete text, unavailable versus empty,
all three prompt views, and actual preparation through planning and wake paths.
Delivery receipts and linking scheduler fallback ticket replies remain future work.

`dayflow_orchestrator_scheduling_20260922_141634_eb0fd61d`:

`general.briefing_email.morning_delivery_once_daily_after_07_00`, which requests

briefing, with invented suggested priorities/times and a claim of no calendar/inbox

### TODO: Remove inline belief tags from agent-facing calendar guidance
Requested September 22. Remove [belief:...] tags from generated Dayflow calendar
logistics text and downstream agent prompts. Keep supporting belief references in
structured provenance metadata for inspection/hydration instead. The readable guidance
must carry the relevant meaning and qualifications without requiring a reference lookup.
This is separate from the historical-outage freshness defect. Status: TODO; not implemented.

### TODO: Distinguish belief ingestion from memory-management instructions
Requested September 22; design discussion deferred. Incoming chats, tickets and insights
may contain facts, behavior instructions, corrections, retractions or forgetting requests.
Do not automatically turn every such input into an active belief. Use LLM interpretation
of full context to determine the intended operation; Python executes validated changes.
Example: "X is wrong; do not record X" should retract/correct X and assess derived beliefs,
not create an active belief "User says X is wrong; never mention X" that resurfaces for months.
Consider explicit operations for correction, retraction, ending historical validity,
stopping reminders, and forgetting. Preserve the distinction between stopping contact
and denying the underlying fact. Prevent rejected source evidence from recreating a
retracted claim during later ingestion. Keep necessary correction/audit bookkeeping
outside normal retrieval and calendar projection; forgetting semantics and what minimal
bookkeeping may remain require further design. Preserve nuance and avoid incorrect merges.
Status: TODO / design pending; no implementation authorized by this note.

### belief_engine auto-disabled: evidence-page echo contract unbounded by fragment count — FIXED + VERIFIED LIVE
2026-09-25. Routine `belief_engine` auto-disabled at 08:04:28Z after 3 consecutive failures
(run_count 74). The user-facing ticket reported only the LAST error; the three runs were two
different defects. Runs ecb9d020 (00:30) and 30932e0a (00:36) failed at step `update_beliefs`;
run c32df285 (00:41) passed that step and failed at `canonicalize_belief_set`.

Canonicalize defect, measured against the live DB: the reviewed pair was
`routine.sleep.recover_tonight_not_day_nap` (a) and `health.sleep.recent_schedule_improving` (b).
b carries 41 lineage records and 158 evidence rows (163,766 chars); the combined review payload
is 168,708 chars, above `investigation.DIRECT_LIMIT` (160,000), so the pair is paged.
`fragments_for()` produces 203 fragments and `pages(..., max_chars=36000)` splits them into 6
pages of 30-38 fragments. `investigation.py:44` requires the returned `findings` set to match the
page's fragment ids exactly in count and membership, so the model must echo ~33 ids of the form
`b:evidence:17:0` verbatim with no omission, duplication or invention. The failing call's first
fragment logged at 01:03:56 is `b:lineage:26:0`, which is exactly the first fragment of page 2
(33 fragments) in the reconstruction — same page confirmed.

Root cause: `matching/context.pages()` bounds a page by CHARACTERS only, never by fragment count,
so the verbatim-echo burden is unbounded. A failed page is never cached (the
`belief_match_pages` insert at investigation.py:49 runs only after validation passes), so the
same page is re-attempted every run. This is a permanent wall, not a transient flake.

Contributing upstream cause: evidence bloat keeps packets above DIRECT_LIMIT so paging is
reached at all. Active beliefs with >20 evidence rows include
`routine.kids.wednesday_late_start_no_7am_wakeup` (119), `food.reminders.meals.selective_timing`
(75), `communication.tone.avoid_patronizing` (74). Relates to the uncleaned duplicate evidence
rows noted in the 2026-09-11 global-pass work.

Consequence: beliefs are frozen. `resources/kg_derived/resource_user_beliefs.json` last written
2026-09-24 01:22 and will not refresh while the routine is disabled. `belief_engine_export`
showing "(disabled)" in the log is NOT a second casualty — it is deliberately manual-only because
belief_engine exports inline.

No code changed. Suspected-fix direction only, not confirmed: cap fragments per page by count as
well as chars for the evidence-page path.

46, 55, 60, 62, 66; `_resolve_evidence_by_refs` logged five "evidence_ref N out of range (batch
size=1)" DEBUG lines and `_assessed_evidence` (update_beliefs.py:78) raised
`Invalid or repeated evidence relation` on `health.movement.hydration.late_evening_overdue`.

Two candidate code-side index mismatches were checked and are CLEAN, so this is model invention
rather than a numbering bug: `EvidenceBundle.as_block()` numbers items per batch via
`enumerate(self.items, 1)`, matching the validator's `len(items)`; and belief ids are UUIDs, not
small integers, so the refs are not ids copied out of the existing-beliefs block. The origin of
46/55/60/62/66 is unexplained — see the logging gap below.

The structural defect is the blast radius, not the bad row. The per-belief `except` at
update_beliefs.py:361 counts the error, then line 171 `if total['errors']: raise RuntimeError(...)`
aborts the entire global pass, and `total` is cumulative across batches. One malformed belief
output out of hundreds kills the run. Run 30932e0a had already written 1 belief before aborting,
so failed runs leave partial writes. Three aborts then auto-disable the routine.

Unresolved design decision, needs the owner: quarantine the offending belief and continue, or
retry that single belief, versus the current abort. CLAUDE.md's fail-loud rule argues against
swallowing; aborting ~600 beliefs of work over one row and disabling the routine is worse.

### Belief-engine agents log neither prompt nor model output — OPEN
2026-09-25. `belief_engine::belief_updater` and `belief_engine::evidence_page` have no
`prompt_debug` in their config.yaml and appear zero times in `emi_logs_*_agents.log*`. The one
subsystem whose failure mode is "the LLM broke a strict structured-output contract" records
neither side of the exchange, so the 2026-09-25 `evidence_refs` invention above could not be
explained after the fact. Consequence: these failures are undiagnosable retrospectively.

September 25 repair (update_beliefs blast radius only): a malformed belief output is now
tolerated per belief instead of aborting the global pass. `_run_batch` collects
`failed_beliefs` (key, action, error); `run()` drops the `if total['errors']: raise` and logs
one ERROR summary naming every skipped key; the step declares `incomplete_tolerated` so the
counts in `stats` stay truthful. `pipeline.py`'s gate — the SECOND abort, which fired on any
step reporting `stats.errors > 0` and would have made the first change a no-op — now honours
that explicit declaration only, records the step as `success_with_tolerated_failures`, carries
`tolerated_failures` on the run result, and logs at ERROR; a step that does not opt in still
aborts exactly as before. `routine_adapter` repeats the summary at ERROR and carries the field.
Skipped beliefs are re-attempted on the next run (evidence is re-collected each night).
Owner decision on 2026-09-25: keep the routine_manager 3-strike auto-disable as is, "since we
are in development and spotting bugs is important" — so routine_manager was NOT touched, and a
genuine hard failure still disables and tickets. Tests: test_belief_tolerated_failures.py (5),
including a regression guard that an undeclared error still aborts the pipeline.
NOT fixed: the canonicalize evidence-page defect above. It still aborts and still counts toward
the 3 strikes, so the routine will re-disable on its next three nights until the fragment-count
cap lands.

September 25 repair (evidence-page coverage): `matching/context.pages()` takes an optional
`max_items`, and the evidence-page call site passes `MAX_FRAGMENTS_PER_PAGE = 12`, so a page's
echo burden is bounded by fragment COUNT and not only by chars. Callers with no per-record
obligation pass nothing and keep the previous pure-char behaviour. Second, and the substantive
part: a page review now gets ONE bounded validation repair, the same contract
`matching/selection.select_records` already uses — the model is re-asked once with the exact
`missing_fragment_ids` / `unknown_fragment_ids` / `repeated_fragment_ids` plus the full
`required_fragment_ids`, via a new `page_retry` context item rendered in the evidence_page
user prompt. Full coverage is still required; it is asked for twice before the pair is refused.
`page_problem()` replaces the two anonymous raises, so the failure now NAMES the offending
fragment ids in the message and at ERROR/WARNING instead of only reporting that coverage broke.
A stored receipt that fails validation raises as corruption rather than being silently re-asked.
Note: page composition changed, so the ~376 existing `belief_match_pages` receipts have new
digests and are unreachable — one-time recompute, no correctness effect; the stale rows were
left in place rather than deleted.
Tests: test_belief_paged_investigation.py grew 5 (count cap splits where chars would not, the
cap holds at the call site, one dropped fragment is corrected rather than fatal, still refuses
after exactly one repair, empty findings follow the same path).

### Tolerated belief failures were invisible once they stopped failing the run — FIXED locally
2026-09-25, follow-on from the two entries above. Making a malformed belief row non-fatal meant
the run SUCCEEDS, so the routine manager's 3-strike auto-disable ticket — the only reason the
owner learned about any of this — can never fire for that class. Logs alone repeat the morning
briefing's failure mode. `belief_engine/pipeline/tolerated_failures.py` keeps a cross-run tally
in a new `belief_update_failures` table (added to `matching/history.SCHEMA`, create-if-missing
like its siblings): a belief absent from a run's failures is cleared, a belief present is
incremented, and at `THRESHOLD = 3` consecutive runs the adapter logs at ERROR and raises a
`dayflow_notify` ticket naming the keys. Deliberately NOT a ticket per occurrence: evidence is
re-collected nightly so a one-off model slip retries and heals itself, and a nightly ticket for
self-healing noise is how the briefing ticket got ignored. The tally is skipped when the update
step did not run, so an evidence-free night cannot clear a real streak. Owner kept the
3-strike auto-disable for genuine hard failures, so that path is untouched.
Tests: 5 in test_belief_tolerated_failures.py (one-off does not escalate, same belief escalates
at the threshold with its error, a clean run clears the streak, a different belief does not
inherit it, an unnamed key is never tracked).

### A belief and the work it caused could not see each other — FIXED locally
2026-09-25, from audit case sac_43ede13a8372. The belief
`routine.reminders.annual_physical.weekly_prompt` (active, high) says "the owner wants recurring
prompting ... approximately every week or so until he sets it up ... do not treat the request as
completed until he confirms it is scheduled." The work object it caused
(`work_0f19f0172d69`) encoded ONE reminder on October 1 with success criteria "the owner receives
one reminder on October 1", and a node payload with no loop/recurrence key. So the finalizer
would close the objective on a single delivery while the belief still said the need was open,
and nothing carried the outcome back to the belief either. The durable fallback would not catch
it: `resource_recurring_obligations.md` lists the physical at `next_due: TBD`, and that file's
own rule skips TBD items.

Root cause of the projection: the planner had NO doctrine on this. The entire guidance for the
field was `success_criteria: "How to know the objective is fully met."` — nothing said a
criterion must name the OUTCOME rather than the act, so the planner complied exactly.

Repair, two halves. (1) Doctrine: `strategic_planner_wo/agent_form.py` `success_criteria` now
says to name the outcome, that a delivery-shaped criterion closes while the need is open, and
that a source naming its own completion condition ("until he books it") carries that condition
in verbatim; a delivery is a valid criterion only when delivering is the whole point. The
recurrence then emerges from the existing engine — the finalizer judges the goal unmet, `failed`
routes to the architect, which picks the next nudge, and `_REPEAT_FAILURE_LIMIT` escalates to
ask_user. Deliberately NO recurrence grammar: the owner ruled out a weekly/daily/nth-Thursday
vocabulary.
(2) A bidirectional link, mirroring the concern lane exactly rather than inventing a second
pattern. `based_on: ["belief:<belief_key>"]` is lifted into `constraints.belief_refs` at creation
(work_persist), keyed by belief_key not row id so a merge that retires an id still resolves.
`work_objects/belief_outbox.py` (BeliefOutboxMixin + `work_belief_feedback` table, wired into
WorkStore beside ConcernOutboxMixin) commits a receipt inside the SAME transaction as the
terminal status change, carrying the objective, the success criteria the work closed against,
per-task finalizer verdicts and attributed user replies. `belief_engine/work_feedback.py` drains
post-commit from the three sites the concern lane uses (finalizer, planner prep recovery,
work_persist closure), asks the new agent what the outcome means, and applies it through
BeliefStore. The reverse link is the evidence row: `source_type='work_outcome'`,
`source_ref=<work_id>`, which is also the idempotency fence — a redelivered receipt is detected
before the model is consulted.

New agent `belief_engine::work_outcome` (config.yaml, agent_form.py, prompts/system.j2,
prompts/user.j2) decides per belief: `no_change` | `resolve` | `revise`, plus a valence and
reasoning. Its doctrine is the lesson from this case: a delivery is not an outcome; resolve only
on evidence the belief's own condition was met, normally the user's own words; an abandoned work
object is not evidence the belief is wrong; the user's words outweigh any machine field. It
DECIDES only — the control code writes, owner-locked beliefs are downgraded to evidence-only
there (as update_beliefs does), and deterministic validation refuses an unknown or omitted
belief_key, an invalid action/valence, or a revise with no statement.
Known limit: a belief_key retired by a merge is logged at ERROR and skipped rather than followed
to the surviving key, so the outbox cannot wedge on it. Merge-following is not implemented.
Tests: 17 in app/assistant/tests/dayflow/test_belief_outbox.py. Suites green: dayflow 673,
belief 120.

September 25 repair (task failure reporting): the reason is now captured BEFORE the closure that
erases it. `entry._after_drive` returns the failure reason, reading `failure_reason(store, work_id)`
ahead of its own `set_work_status(abandoned)` — the cascade abandons every still-startable node
and `failed` is startable, so reading afterwards found nothing and produced the generic
"no failed node recorded (stalled graph)". `start_task_run` / `resume_task_run` carry it out as
`failure_reason`, and both consumers (`routine_manager/runners/taskrunner/task_runner.py` and the
`run_task` tool) use that instead of re-deriving it too late. The terminal reason written to the
graph now carries the cause too, replacing the bare and untrue "run cancelled/failed at entry".
No compatibility path was left behind: both callers were updated.
Tests: 4 in test_task_failure_reason_survives_closure.py, including one that asserts reading the
graph afterwards is STILL lossy — the proof the fix had to move the read earlier rather than
reword the message.
NOTE: this repairs the REPORTING. The briefing's own defect (step_7 passing a dict to
write_text_file's `content`) is still unfixed, as is the dropped `artifact_1`.

### Morning briefing: artifact type and the dropped action-node artifact — FIXED locally
2026-09-25, the two substantive defects behind the 09-13 failures (the reporting half is the
entry.py repair above).

(1) **A structured fact handed to a text argument.** `${artifact_5}` resolved to a dict and went
to `write_text_file`'s `content`, which requires a str — the briefing was fully composed and
discarded on the last step, three runs running. A `${fact}` placeholder always sits inside a JSON
string in `args_json` (JSON cannot express an unquoted substitution), so `_substitute_args` now
serialises a non-str fact when the tool's OWN contract declares that argument `string`/`text`
(`_declared_text_args`). Type-directed, no tool names in the executor: `write_text_file.content`
is declared string so it is serialised, `save_daily_summary.summary_data` is declared object so
it is passed structurally, unchanged.

(2) **A provenance envelope recorded as the artifact.** `invoke_agent` returns
`data={'agent_name':…, 'agent_output':…}` and declares `data.agent_output` as its structured
output; `_write_output` blindly preferred `.data`, so the artifact was the wrapper — the briefing
file would have carried `agent_name` around the actual briefing. `_declared_payload_key` reads
the contract's single `data.<key>` object output and records that. A tool declaring no such
payload keeps `data` as-is. Note `save_daily_summary` already hand-unwrapped this envelope, which
is what identified the shape.

(3) **An action node's `produces` was dropped.** `result_recorder` writes a manager's answer with
no `data_id`, and only `tool_executor` honoured `produces` — so `${artifact_1}` (the CNN/BBC
scrape, an action node) never became a keyed fact and the compile step received the literal text
`${artifact_1}` instead of the headlines. Every briefing that got past the save step would have
had no news in it. `_write_output` is now `write_produced_output` (public, one shared writer) and
`task_runner._run_action_node` calls it with the manager's ToolResult.

(4) `write_text_file` now distinguishes a missing `content` from a wrong-typed one. Reporting a
dict as "Missing required argument: content" is what pointed the diagnosis away from the real
cause for months.

Tests: 9 in test_task_artifact_plumbing.py, written against the REAL shipped contracts so a
change to a declared argument type fails here rather than at 05:30 in a nightly run; includes the
end-to-end case proving an action node's artifact now reaches the next step's argument.
STILL NOT RE-ENABLED: `morning_briefing` remains `enabled: false` with `consecutive_failures: 3`,
and unlike belief_engine no recovery probe fires for it — the live log shows
"Routine skipped: morning_briefing (disabled)" every minute. It needs an explicit toggle.

### Belief engine audit 2026-09-26 — 30+ defects catalogued — OPEN
Four parallel code audits (forming / merging / decaying / contradictions), each reading its area
end to end, plus direct measurement against the live emi.db. Full writeup:
`docs/design/belief_engine_defects_2026-09-26.md`. Companion prose description of how the
subsystem works: `docs/architecture/BELIEF_ENGINE_WALKTHROUGH.md`. No code changed.

The three to fix first, all confirmed by reading the code directly:
1. The subconscious feedback extractor may emit 7 belief domains; only 2 exist
   (`meal`, `communication`). `upsert_belief` raises, it is caught at WARNING, and the source
   comment pod is marked processed anyway — the belief is lost and never retries. There are 20
   `user_comment` evidence rows in the whole database; this is the rarest evidence the system has.
2. A merge can erase the survivor's structured `conditions`: `matching/history.py:81-85` binds the
   column to the reviewer's `conditions_json`, and `match_review/agent_form.py:12` declares that
   field `str | None = None`, so omitting it writes NULL. Only 33 of 612 beliefs carry conditions
   at all, so this destroys the scarcest field in the store.
3. Ticket replies stopped producing weighted evidence on 2026-09-19 (`ticket_rejection` 137 rows /
   `ticket_acceptance` 49 rows both end that day; `ticket_context` replaced them from 09-20 at
   weight 0.0 and is in NON_OBSERVATION_SOURCES). Needs an owner ruling on whether the demotion was
   intended before anything is "fixed".

Also load-bearing: an evidence-free belief has NO exit route — only `faded` deprecates, and the two
last-confirmed-based retirement paths were unwired with `DecayStaleBeliefsStep`, so the 137
observation-free beliefs cannot age out; `unverified` is written as a band but is not in the
declared Band literal and holds 149 active beliefs; a user contradiction is recorded as evidence
and then ignored until a nightly recompute that a single comment's weight cannot trigger.
Legacy still resident: `belief_engine_v2/` at 46 MB with a 6,275-belief seed DB and zero importers;
`belief_distinct_pairs` with 9,150 rows, zero readers and zero writers, recreated on every install;
`DecayStaleBeliefsStep` retired from production but still executed by the sandbox, so sandbox
scenarios validate a model production no longer runs.

### Daily assessment reads a file nothing has written since 2026-03-08 — SUSPECTED DEAD INPUT
`app/assistant/pipelines/daily_insights/steps/archive_daily_assessment.py` lists

### A chat anecdote became a scheduled dayflow reminder, with no intake and no ask — DESIGN DECISION NEEDED
Traced 2026-09-27 (owner: "I was talking about a possibly poisoned rat, somehow this became a task on
dayflow orchestrator"). Chain, from `logs/emi_logs_45344.log.1`:
1. 15:09–15:11 chat in master_room about a dying rat on the lawn; the assistant's own reply suggested keeping
   the dogs away. chat_gate answered directly (no hand-off). The context engine's chat_scan said
   `should_activate=False` for the rat message (line 22080).
2. The daily-context generator recorded milestones "3:09 PM Saw a dying rat…", "3:10 PM Called
   animal control" and a day theme "A dying rat… remains a focused dog-safety concern".
3. 15:22:11 `dayflow_routine_writer` regenerated the routine document (delta gate on the changed
   daily context) and wrote "**Primary safety watch:** dying rat…", "8:00 PM … Because of the rat,
   plan for direct supervision", citing beliefs (line 88000, 88009, 88020 of the later render).
4. 15:23:27 `dayflow_orchestrator_manager` tick: `strategic_planner_wo` created work_fef9ed30c235
   "Notify the owner at 8:00 PM PDT … keep Clyde and Bonnie away from the front-lawn bushes and rat"
   with `source_intake: []` and rationale "It comes from today's routine safety guidance" (line
   29147–29168); persisted, architected into one node with a wake at 20:00 (line 29986).
Actual: an unrequested reminder is scheduled from the assistant's own chat advice, laundered through the
routine writer into "routine guidance" that the evaluator then treats as a source of work.
Intended (per CLAUDE.md): the evaluator converts INTAKE to work objects; routine-derived work is the
routine overlay for scheduled actions (AC at 21:00, lights, family-time notify). A "watch item" the
writer synthesised from chat is neither. Nothing in the chain violates its own prompt — the writer
was told to synthesise the day, the evaluator was told routine guidance may become work — so this
is a design decision for the owner: either the routine writer's flags/watch items are not a source
of new work (evaluator rule), or the writer keeps watch items to what the user asked for. Affected:
`agents/dayflow_routine_writer`, `agents/dayflow_orchestrator/strategic_planner_wo`.
**FIXED locally 2026-09-27 (owner: "do your other fixes also")** — evaluator rule added to
`strategic_planner_wo/prompts/system.j2` §1: the routine's clock-anchored items are work; its flags,
watch items and guidance are context; what the user asked for arrives as intake or a ticket. The
routine writer is unchanged. The 20:00 reminder for 2026-09-27 (work_fef9ed30c235) was left in the
work store. Hot-reloads (YAML/j2); no restart needed for this part.

### Context engine ran a 1.9-hour KG convergence and an emi_team "reasoning" run on "Was it poisoned you think?" — FIXED locally
Same day, same log. chat_scan activated on the follow-up "Was it poisoned you think?" with
seeds=['the owner'] (line 22654, 15:11:22). `context_activation` then took **6833 s** (kg_convergence
wave 1 activated 6,412 nodes, 0 convergence nodes — line 88076, 17:05:07), synthesised a situation
brief, and stage 4 `reasoning_agent` invoked **emi_team_manager** with the problem `The user said:
"Was it poisoned you think?"` (line 88169, 17:05:16). With no referent for "it", the team planned
"clarify what 'it' refers to, determine whether poisoning is suspected", queried the KG for
poisoning records, and produced a final answer suggesting poison-control and preserving evidence
(emi_logs_45344.log line 1614). Output went only to the context memo (blackboard) — no ticket, no
dayflow item — so the owner saw nothing, but the run cost two hours of a thread plus KG and LLM
work for a casual question whose subject was in the previous three chat lines. Three faults:
(a) chat_scan treats a follow-up as needing KG retrieval without the conversation (the rat is
chat context, not KG); (b) kg_convergence with seed = the primary user activates the whole graph
(6,412 nodes) with no budget that bounds wall time — the module's per-hop `max_nodes` applies from
hop 2 only; (c) the reasoning stage hands a bare user sentence to emi_team as a "problem", which
the planner dutifully treats as a task. Related memory: "chat_scan over-eager" TODO. Affected:
`app/assistant/context_engine/{chat_scan,context_activation,reasoning_agent}.py`,
`app/assistant/kg_core/kg_utils/kg_convergence.py`. Status: recorded, not changed.
**Root cause, owner's reading confirmed in code:** the room passes the whole recent history
(`room_session_manager.py:490-495`, `recent_chat_context=json.dumps(history)`), and
`run_context_activation_pipeline` hands it to **chat_scan only** (`pipeline.py:97-102`).
`run_context_activation` (`context_activation.py:188`) and `run_reasoning_agent`
(`reasoning_agent.py:50`, prompt line 26 `The user said: "{user_message}"`) take the bare
sentence; the dossier writer's inputs are `user_message, kg_briefing, seeds, primary_user,
visitor_label`. So the stage that could see "it" = the rat used the conversation only to decide
to activate, and every later stage had zero context and looked in the KG — which is written
nightly and cannot hold anything said today. Fix is plumbing first: carry `recent_chat_context`
into the brief, the dossier writer and the reasoning task; then chat_scan's rule ("a referent that
is in the conversation needs no retrieval"); then a wall-time budget on convergence.
**FIXED locally 2026-09-27, all three:** (1) `recent_chat_context` now flows
`pipeline.py` → `run_context_activation` → `dossier_writer` (new context item + prompt block, and
its grounding rule accepts the conversation as a source) and is stored on `SituationBrief` so
`run_reasoning_agent` puts it in the emi_team task ("The conversation above is what the message
refers to; the Knowledge Graph holds what was recorded before today"). (2) chat_scan system prompt:
a message whose subject is in the recent conversation is answered from the conversation; activate
for what the conversation cannot supply. (3) `WALL_BUDGET_S = 300` in `pipeline.py` (owner: "5 min
max wall time"): `multi_seed_activation`/`_bfs_from_seed` take a `deadline` (time.monotonic) and
return the partial walk at the deadline (WARNING with counts), wave 2 is skipped once past it, and
the pipeline saves the brief and stops before reasoning/memo (ERROR log) when activation alone
exhausted the budget. Python changes need an the assistant restart; the prompt/config parts hot-reload.
**Gap in the budget, found by reading 2026-09-30 (SUSPECTED, not measured; no run since the fix):**
the deadline stops the BFS walks, but after wave 1 `multi_seed_activation` builds a `ScoredNode` for
every activated node (`_build_scored_node`: `session.get` plus two `COUNT` queries for the degree, per
node) with no deadline check, so a walk stopped at 300 s with thousands of visited nodes can still run
long past the budget. The walk itself also issues per-neighbour ORM queries (node + two degree counts)
and, because chat_scan always seeds the primary user (degree > 1,150), covers most of the graph; a
set-based SQL two-hop shared-neighbour query answered the same question in 0.02 s (2026-09-29). All 50
saved situation briefs (master_room) took 3,900-6,800 s in activation; the newest is 2026-09-27.

### Belief intake (shadow) processed the day in progress on its first startup run — FIXED locally
`belief_engine/intake/routine_adapter.py` took the pipeline boundary day as its target. The routine
manager runs a missed daily slot at startup, so after the 2026-09-27 19:22 restart it ran the intake
for 2026-09-27 itself — against insights a daily_insights catch-up had written at 14:39 (the same
startup behaviour, after the 14:38 restart) — and marked the day done with one atom; the 01:00 run
would then have skipped the day and the evening's evidence would never have been ingested. Fixed:
the adapter's default target is yesterday's local calendar date (at the routine's own 01:00 slot that
is the day the nightly insights run just wrote; at a daytime catch-up it excludes the day in
progress). The premature 2026-09-27 rows (B534, one evidence row, one decision, the day row; no
revisions) were removed by a one-off script so tonight's run takes the full day. Needs the next the assistant
restart to load; tonight's 01:00 run yields the same day under either version.
Also seen: 2026-09-26 has no insights/timeline because the 00:05 daily_insights run hit OpenAI
"no credits remaining" (429) and the two retries hit the quota flag; the intake will never see that
day unless daily_insights is re-run for 2026-09-26.

### A daytime restart freezes that day's insights at a partial-day snapshot; the nightly run then does nothing — FIXED locally
Observed 2026-09-27/28. After the 14:38 restart the routine manager ran the missed daily slot of
`daily_insights_pipeline` immediately (14:38–14:39) for boundary day 2026-09-27, writing insights,
timeline and assessment for the day so far. At the real slot (2026-09-28 00:05:51) the same routine
"finished" in 0.03 s with status=success: the PipelineRunner found every step already recorded as
done for that date and skipped them all (`force=False`). So 2026-09-27's insights, timeline and
assessment are the 14:39 version for good — the evening (rat, dinner, dog walk) is absent from the
insights page, the old belief engine's 14-day read, and the belief intake (which processed the
partial day at 01:00: 1 insight, 1 atom). The previous night showed the other half of the problem:
2026-09-26's 00:05 run failed on OpenAI credits, and nothing re-ran it, so that day has no insights at
all. Actual: a day-boundary job runs at the wrong time and then refuses to run at the right time.
Intended: the 00:05 run produces the day's final artifacts. Affected: `routine_manager` (catch-up of
missed daily slots for day-boundary routines), `app/assistant/pipelines/step_runner.py` (skip-if-done
without regard to when the earlier run happened), every consumer of day_context. Recovery for the two
days: re-run `daily_insights_pipeline` for 2026-09-26 and 2026-09-27 with force, then reset the
belief-intake day rows for 2026-09-27 and re-run the intake.
**FIXED locally 2026-09-28 (owner: "recover the days, do the fix").** Root cause was neither the
catch-up nor the skip-if-done alone but that a late daily run let the pipeline pick *its own* day.
`RoutineManager._daily_slot_target_date` now passes every daily routine the boundary date of its
SLOT (00:05 → the day that just ended) as `target_date`, on time or late, so a 14:38 catch-up of the
00:05 slot re-runs the failed night for the right day and the real 00:05 run finds nothing done for
its day. Pipelines that ignore target_date are unaffected. Tests:
`non_agent_tests/test_routine_daily_slot_target_date.py`. Recovery ran the same morning:
daily_insights re-run with force for 2026-09-26 and 2026-09-27, intake rows for 2026-09-27 reset,
intake re-run through 2026-09-27. Needs an the assistant restart to load.

### Audit inbox ingest recorded the instruction paragraph as every case's resolution notes — FIXED locally
`app/assistant/system_audit/inbox.py` took notes from `text.find("## Resolution")`. Every case file's
"What I need from Claude" paragraph mentions "`## Resolution`" in running text before the real
section, so the stored `resolution.notes` of every dismissed/resolved case was that instruction
boilerplate, not the diagnosis. Fixed 2026-09-28: the heading is matched at the start of a line.
Past cases keep their wrong notes (the case files on disk hold the real text).

### situation_auditor opens cases on in-flight states the orchestrator closes on its own — OPEN (owner's call)
2026-09-28 triage of the 14 open cases: 10 were bound only to work objects the orchestrator had
already closed (abandoned/done) 1 minute to 9 hours after the case opened — the auditor observed a
state mid-flight (a notify past due during a restart, a failed lights run before the finalizer's
stop, an unanswered briefing). 2 were false positives against the owner's settled patterns (Friday
dog walk after Friday Night Meats; a domain-review reminder three weeks before auto-renewal). 1 was
already fixed at the source (4487db48). 1 is real and trivial (gettextwall.app review reminder
2027-09-01 falls after Cloudflare's ~2027-08-26 renewal attempt). Lifetime: 102 cases, 37 resolved,
63 dismissed. Options: (a) re-verify a finding after a hold period and auto-dismiss it as
self-resolved when every bound object is terminal; (b) give the auditor the owner's relevant beliefs
so settled patterns stop being flagged. Status: presented, no code.
**Update 2026-09-28 — the proposed fix fails its backtest; not shipped.** A 3-hour hold that
dismisses a finding once every bound work object is done/abandoned was built, unit-tested, then
backtested over all 100 closed auditor cases: it would have auto-dismissed 24 of the 37 RESOLVED
(real) cases while removing only 25 of the 63 dismissed ones. Variants (0.5 h / 1 h / 3 h; done-only
vs done|abandoned) all lose real cases at the same rate they remove noise. "The orchestrator closed
the object" does not predict "no defect" — defective objects are closed too, often because they
are defective. The hold was removed before it ran. The same test had been used in the morning's
triage; the ten "moot" dismissals were re-read: six are timing (restart, credits outage, the
auditor racing the finalizer), three content findings were false (the vet "today" is correct on
the notification day; the "cancelled workday" claim was unsupported), one is real — below.

### 09-24 morning briefing: two execution failures in the send path — OPEN (suspected, not re-verified)
From case sac_71728e7d369b (dismissed on the moot test, which the backtest above invalidated): the
first send task `work_emi_team_manager_4b11dc4b` aborted on `summary_pairs.summary must be a
non-empty string` (emi_team::summary produced an empty summary pair), and the verification retry
`work_emi_team_manager_b54b019` aborted after 8 cycles because `personal_admin_manager` was "not
allowed tool/node/exit action" — the work-context specialist is `work_personal_admin_manager`
(added 2026-09-22, entry above), so the planner named the non-work manager. Neither error string
appears in the logs since 2026-09-27; not re-checked against current prompts. Affected:
`agents/emi_team/summary` form validation; `work_emi_team::planner` specialist naming.
**Root cause found 2026-09-29 (the briefing again, same morning):** `work_personal_admin_manager` has
no tool wrapper — `app/assistant/lib/tools/` holds `work_emi_team_manager/`, `work_web_manager/` and
`personal_admin_manager/`, not `work_personal_admin_manager/`. The 2026-09-22 change ("Delegated work
personal-admin context — FIXED locally") added the manager config, the dayflow scope allow entry and
the prompt line "For personal administration within this work object, delegate to
work_personal_admin_manager", but not the wrapper, so the manager is never a tool: the
work_emi_team::planner's final allowed tools on 2026-09-29 07:00:25 contain `playwright_manager` and no
personal-admin manager at all. The worker asked find_tool five times, was told each time that Gmail
belongs to personal_admin_manager (which the work context cannot call either), and fell back to
`playwright_manager`, which opened mail.google.com in the browser session and searched Sent mail
(07:07–07:12). The page half-rendered, so nothing was sent. On 2026-09-24 the same gap showed as the
retry naming `personal_admin_manager` ("not allowed tool/node/exit action"). Every work-object task
that needs Gmail, Calendar, Tasks or Docs hits this. Fix: add the wrapper, mirroring
`lib/tools/work_web_manager/`. **FIXED locally 2026-09-29:** `lib/tools/work_personal_admin_manager/` added (wrapper, contract, forms, description; mirrors the personal_admin_manager wrapper, authority 90). Verified the registry loads it and the dayflow scope allows it for work_emi_team_manager; needs an app restart. Proof in use = the next work task that touches Gmail routes through it.

### A browser tool's answered error froze its whole work object and crashed every planning tick — FIXED locally 2026-09-29
Chain (morning briefing, work_ef82b7ea28f0): with no Gmail tool in work tasks (wrapper entry above)
the worker used the browser; `web_fill_ref` clicked the Gmail search box, then the browser server
ANSWERED "Ref e33 not found" to the type step. The wrapper reported that answer as
`error_code="mcp_call_failed"`, and `manager_runtime/execution.py` records `mcp_call_failed` as an
`unknown` external outcome — correctly, for its real meaning (no answer came back, e.g. a dropped
connection after a send). The unknown receipt blocks every later execution in the object (the
designed barrier); the architect's resolving node ("ask the owner whether the email went out") was blocked
by it too; and `work_node_dispatch_node`'s ExecutionBlocked branch returned without `last_agent`, so
the delegator routed the switchboard's output back into dispatch, which raised on the consumed pick —
every tick from 07:14 (4 failed ticks, "Dayflow autonomy is failing"). Fixes: (1) the 14 browser-tool
sites that report an ANSWERED MCP error now use `mcp_tool_error` (settled); the two true no-answer
sites (`mcp_tool_executor` exception path, `web_fill_xy` except path) keep `mcp_call_failed`
(unknown); `execution.py` and its tested rule are unchanged; test case added. (2) The hold branch sets
`last_agent`, so a hold ends the pass like a claim. (3) The stuck receipt was settled through
`resolve_execution_call` with its evidence (the tool's own error, and the run's final answer "No new
email was sent"); the next tick (08:01:22) claimed the blocked node. Tests: execution ownership 25/25,
dayflow 674/674. Still open (design): an ask whose purpose is to settle an unknown outcome sits behind
that same barrier; and the browser action tools declare `side_effects: read_only`.
Consequence verified 2026-09-29 (Muse comparison): the approval gate never reads `side_effects`
(`tool_registry.py` only parses it; `scope.tools.allow_external_side_effects` is built but no
dispatch path checks it). Approval is decided only by `approval_min_authority`
(`lib/tool_execution/tool_approval.py`). The browser action tools (web_click_ref_snapshot,
web_click_xy_snapshot, web_fill_ref, web_fill_xy, web_type_focused) set it to 70, so any room at
authority 70 or above — the dayflow room is 95 — can click, fill and submit web forms with no
approval ticket. This is how the 09-29 briefing worker could operate Gmail in the browser
unattended. OPEN (design): whether browser writes should require approval, or approval should key
off `side_effects`.

### Routine belief selector: request-local selection ids spelled like live belief ids — FIXED locally 2026-09-29
`belief_engine/matching/selection.select_records` labels each catalog record `selection_id = B<i>`
for the selector model and maps the returned ids back by that label. At the belief cutover the live
belief ids became `B<n>` (intake store), carried in the same records as `belief_key`, `short_id` and
`refines`. An echoed belief id such as `B45` would then pass validation (any `B<i>` below the page
size is a valid label) and select a DIFFERENT record, silently. Not observed in production (the
routine stage still read legacy dotted keys until the cutover); found while switching it. Fix:
selection ids are `S<i>`; both selector prompts (`dayflow_belief_selector`, `belief_engine::evidence_match`)
and three tests updated. `belief_engine/matching/service.py` builds its own `B<i>` labels for the
legacy discovery path, which reads `user_beliefs` only — left as is, noted here.

### Catalog tag lookup matched legacy UUID tag rows (SQLite LIKE is case-insensitive) — FIXED locally 2026-09-29
`catalog._entries` selected intake tags with `belief_id LIKE 'B%'`; SQLite `LIKE` ignores ASCII case,
so 42 legacy tag rows whose UUID begins with `b` matched too. Harmless in effect (UUID keys were
never looked up), found by checking. Now `GLOB 'B[0-9]*'`.

### test_agent_input_context_item::test_message_task_information_fields_reach_template fails — SUSPECTED STALE TEST (pre-existing)
The test sends `Message(information="Primary user: Sam")` to `context_engine::chat_scan` and expects
that text in the prompt. The template (`chat_scan/prompts/user.j2`, last changed in 7aacd502) renders
`Primary user: {{ primary_user }}` from its own context item, so the prompt reads `Primary user: `
(blank) and the assertion fails. Fails alone, not caused by the 2026-09-29 belief cutover. Either the
test should pass `primary_user`, or the template should keep reading `information` — owner's call
which convention chat_scan uses.

### Agent forms enforce some enumerations only in Python validators, outside the JSON schema — SUSPECTED DESIGN ISSUE (found 2026-09-29)
Server-side structured output (OpenAI's strict json_schema, and local constrained decoding) enforces
only what `model_json_schema()` declares. Some forms declare an enumerated field as `str` and check it
in a `@field_validator`, so no provider can enforce it and a well-formed reply can still fail
validation. Found in the local-model coverage run (qwen3.5:9b, 53 forms, 3 failures):
`work_finalizer` (`verdict must be one of ('achieved', 'achieved_plan_changes', 'retry', 'unrecoverable')`),
`work_repair` (retired; `disposition`), and `work_architect` (`duplicate_of` pairs a node with
itself — a cross-field rule, not expressible in a schema). Consequence: an invalid value costs a
retry or a failed call on any provider. Suggested: declare enumerations as `Literal[...]` so the
schema carries them; cross-field rules stay validators. Not changed.

### A work object reported accounting records it never wrote, and the finalizer judged it achieved — CONFIRMED 2026-09-29, OPEN
work_cc14b548da59 (Anthropic $200 Max receipt, 2026-09-29): the evaluator made a work object to
"record the payment in the accounting records … and add the subscription". EmiOS has no accounting
ledger or subscription store. The dispatch run's only tool calls were three `pod_fetch` calls of the
receipt email (emi_logs_44780_agents.log, 11:25–11:27 PDT); `work_personal_admin::planner` then
returned findings "Accounting transaction recorded — …" and "Subscription record updated — …" with no
tool behind them. `work_finalizer` judged the node `achieved` from those claims, the report node told
the owner "the $200 Anthropic Max payment was recorded … the active subscription was updated", and the
owner replied "ok great thanks". Actual vs intended: a finding claims a write no tool made; the
finalizer accepts worker claims without tool evidence for the claimed effect. Consequence: the owner
believes a record exists that does not. Contributing: the evaluator created work for a capability the
system lacks. Suspected fix areas (not decided): finalizer requires a tool result for each claimed
external effect; planners' findings are checked against the call log; the evaluator is told which
record stores exist. Found via audit case sac_6905f3c86649.

### Architect retypes source ids into task text and can corrupt them — CONFIRMED 2026-09-29, OPEN
Same work object: the originating intake cites `datapod:email:252502c3c5197e451dcd51ec`; the
architect's task text says `…51ed`. The worker fetched the right pod (it had the correct id elsewhere
in context), so no harm this time. An id copied by an LLM is a transcription, not a reference
(standing rule: ids + deterministic joins). Suspected fix: the architect names the source by a handle
the runtime resolves, or the runtime injects the intake's exact source ids into task context. Found via
audit case sac_6905f3c86649.

### Noticer never saw "I gave the dogs their flea medication"; the concern stayed live for two weeks — FIXED locally 2026-09-29 (items 1–3); digest self-feed OPEN
Owner said it twice in master_room: 2026-09-15 16:18Z ("I have given [the dogs] their flea medication
for this month (September 2026).") and 2026-09-28 22:01Z ("OK I HAVE GIVEN [THE DOGS] THEIR FLEA
MEDICATION!"). Concern f0c7b2f0 ("flea medication is due soon", anchored on the monthly calendar event,
first observed 2026-08-16, reinforced 47 times) stayed in `addressing`, and every daily digest from 09-18
through 09-29 listed it red. Its journal never mentions either statement; every entry since 09-20 says
administration is "not independently confirmed".
Why (context_builder.py, verified by rebuilding the sections at 2026-09-29 22:13Z):
1. `_build_recent_passing_mentions` is the only raw-chat lane: user messages from the last 4 days, 60–220
   chars, newest 12. The 09-28 statement is 55 chars and is dropped; its follow-up ("yes, i told them they
   were treats…") passes and is meaningless alone. A completion statement is exactly the short kind of
   message this filter removes. (09-15: likely crowded out of the 12; not re-verified.)
2. `_build_recent_chat_clusters` gives only each cluster's one-liner. The 09-28 cluster
   (datapod:chat_cluster:fb21e77f61d6656ddae92cae, "pet flea medication routine") holds the full exchange
   in its body; the noticer sees only the topic.
3. `_build_dayflow_recent` (48 h, 10 rows after filtering) carried no flea line.
Also found: the subconscious digest is itself clustered as chat — datapod:chat_cluster:398e59f40acfba92fc38c1f2
("active household concerns", 09-29) is the digest text, and one such cluster appears after each evening
digest — so the noticer reads its own "still tracking" list back as chat. Consequence: user statements
that settle a concern do not reach the noticer; nagging persists. Not fixed (diagnosis only).
**FIXED locally 2026-09-29:** every user message now lands verbatim in the brain inbox and is routed by
`subconscious::gate` against the open concerns; the noticer reads routed messages under their concern
(docs/design/brain_inbox_and_concern_store_2026-09-29.md). Live proof: the 09-28 message routed to the
flea concern and the next noticer tick resolved it. The digest-as-chat-cluster loop is still OPEN.
Work-object side, traced 2026-09-29 (CONFIRMED, OPEN, design item "closing concerns"): 46 work
objects cite concern f0c7b2f0; 45 were abandoned (40 of them in the 2026-08-31 01:30Z replan burst,
146 of 150 objects that hour dropped by the steward) and none delivered an outcome to it
(`work_outcomes` empty). The one flea work object that finished `done`, work_4e6853590e8c
(2026-09-20, from the owner's email "Confirmed plan for Bonnie and Clyde's September flea
medication", Banfield fallback), has `concern_refs: []`: the evaluator did not link the email to the
live concern, so its completion had no path to the register. The July–August "Notify … flea
medication" objects (done) also carry no concern_refs, so a ticket reply on them could not reach
their concerns either. Meanwhile the noticer journaled "administration is not independently
confirmed" on the concern daily for a week while it sat in `addressing`.

### test_job_thread_dispatch::test_do_work_claims_then_runs_on_job_thread is flaky — SUSPECTED (2026-09-29)
Failed once in the full dayflow suite and once in 3 isolated reruns (passed the other 2 and in both
earlier full runs today). Thread-timing test; the code under test was not touched by the change that
surfaced it. Not investigated.

### Entity detector does not know "Karjalohja" — CONFIRMED 2026-09-29, OPEN
`EntityCardInjector().detect_entities_in_text` returns [] for "Karjalohja", "the karjalohja summer house"
and a sentence containing it, although the KG holds `Karjalohja` (ed49d09c…) and `Karjalohja Summer House`
(16698e35…) with descriptions. The same call finds the owner's brother. Consequence: anything that expands a message
by detected entities (belief recall for chat, the planned brain context) silently misses places like this.
Suspected: the detector's catalog covers only some entity kinds or only entities with cards. Not investigated.
Owner, 2026-09-30: Karjalohja has no entity card, which is fine; the detector keys on cards. Entities can
be found directly in the KG by filtering to entity nodes and searching them, exactly or semantically,
which would find Karjalohja; important entities should still have cards. Planned: the brain's
association step searches entity nodes directly (not only carded entities) for the people and places a
matter names.
Also measured the same day: `multi_seed_activation` (the owner's brother + Karjalohja, 120 s deadline) ran 124 s,
activated 3,411 nodes and returned no convergence nodes, while a plain SQL two-hop shared-neighbor query
found the owner's father, the spouse and the owner in 0.02 s.

### Brain inbox attaches a stale, unrelated assistant message as "replying to" — CONFIRMED 2026-09-29, FIXED locally 2026-09-29
`brain_inbox._fetch_user_messages` sets `replying_to` to the latest assistant message in the same room
before the user message, with no age limit and no check that the message answers it. In the Slack room
between the owner and a friend, every message (the friend's included, across three days) was shown as
"replying to the assistant: Fair enough 😄 …", a days-old line; a dayflow delegation request was shown
replying to an unrelated declined ticket. The gate and the noticer read these as context. Found on the
/brain page. Planned fix (owner discussion 2026-09-29): render events as conversations — grouped by room,
time-ordered, local time with date headers, assistant turns inline, speakers labelled by role, and a
factual per-room description for the brain — instead of a per-event replying_to.
**FIXED locally 2026-09-29** as planned: `subconscious/conversations.py` renders events inside
their whole conversations (split at 30 minutes of silence) under each room's ROOM.md `description:`;
`replying_to` is no longer written or shown.

### Emails without action items never reach unified_log — CONFIRMED by reading 2026-09-29, FIXED locally 2026-09-29
`lib/core_tools/email_tool/utils/email_utils.py` (repo-ingest path, ~line 349): `unified_messages.append(...)`
is indented inside `if action_items:`, so an important email (importance >= 5) whose parser output has no
action items is stored in the event repository and minted as a pod, but no unified_log row is written.
Consumers of unified_log (the daily timeline and anything reading chat/email history there) miss those
emails. Found while tracing how email reaches dayflow; not measured how many emails this drops.
Also noted (design, not a bug): emails scored below 5 by email_parser are marked read and stored nowhere,
so no later reader — dayflow, pods, the planned brain — can ever see them; dayflow reads the event
repository for today's emails >= 5 and passes a 2,000-character body excerpt.
**FIXED locally 2026-09-29** in the move of emails to pods only: the unified_log append now runs for
every kept email. Dayflow and the UI email widget now read email pods (`pod_store/email_pods.py`); the
event repository no longer holds email.

### Email subjects are stored MIME-encoded (`=?utf-8?B?...?=`) — CONFIRMED 2026-09-29, FIXED locally 2026-09-29
`EmailProcessor.extract_metadata` (lib/core_tools/email_tool/utils/email_processor.py) returns
`email_message.get("Subject")` as is, with no RFC 2047 decoding (`email.header.decode_header`/`make_header`
or a parser with `policy.default`). Any subject containing non-ASCII characters (Finnish letters, emoji) is
stored as the encoded word. Live count: 201 email pods have `=?...?=` in their one_liner. Consequence:
email_parser, dayflow, the pod titles and the UI show unreadable subjects; the sender display name from
`parseaddr` is likely affected the same way (not measured). Already-stored pods would need a re-decode
of their stored subject once fixed. Found during the email move to pods; not fixed.
**FIXED locally 2026-09-29:** `EmailProcessor.extract_metadata` decodes the subject and the sender
display name with the stdlib RFC 5322 parser (`policy.default.header_fetch_parse`: unfolds, decodes encoded
words, an unknown charset yields a replacement character rather than an exception). The sender name was
encoded too (36 pods had only the sender encoded). Already-stored pods (201) and unified_log rows (522)
are left encoded by the owner's decision: old emails carry no value worth a data rewrite.

### Concern anchors are never set, so anchor-based suppression never fires — CONFIRMED 2026-09-29, duplicates ADDRESSED locally 2026-09-30
`persist._settled_anchors` suppresses a re-mint of a declined/chronic concern by `anchor`, but the
noticer leaves `anchor` null: all 60 most recent concerns in the `concerns` table have anchor NULL.
The same 60 hold near-duplicates minted over weeks: four makeup-Picture-Day concerns, three
Ask-A-Scientist-Night, two Club Drive, two ASB Card, two PTSA parent night, about six fatigue/sleep
variants. Consequence: duplicates and re-mints after a decline are not caught by code; only the
noticer's reading of the register prevents them. Not fixed (design discussion 2026-09-29).
2026-09-30: the concern door (`subconscious/concern_door.py`) now judges every new concern against
the open, recently resolved and dormant concerns and folds duplicates into the concern they match,
anchor or not. Anchors themselves are still rarely set; the settled-anchor rule remains as the
code-level check in front of the judge.

### Dayflow evaluator sees concern notes cut at 200 characters — CONFIRMED by reading 2026-09-29, OPEN
`pipelines/dayflow/utils/subconscious_projection._project_concern` truncates `notes` to 200 chars
(`_NOTES_MAX_CHARS`) with an ellipsis; the evaluator (`strategic_planner_wo`) renders that as the
concern's whole context. Work agents downstream (architect, worker, finalizer) see nothing of the
concern: `constraints.concern_refs` is used only to deliver outcomes back. Also truncates the weekly
schedule body at 4,000 chars. Violates the no-truncation rule; the missing concern context is the
open design item "context for concerns" (2026-09-29).

### Concern `escalation` and `addressable_by` are written but not read by current dayflow — CONFIRMED by reading 2026-09-29, OPEN
The noticer writes `escalation` (escalated_concerns, re_escalate) and `addressable_by` on concerns.
No current reader acts on `escalation`; `addressable_by` routing is described only in the retired
`strategic_planner` prompt. `strategic_planner_wo` shows every active concern without either field,
and `project_concerns` reads only `active`, so an `addressing` concern re-escalated to active is
indistinguishable from any other. Suspected dead output; not fixed.

### Calendar tool prints debug lists to stdout on every call — CONFIRMED 2026-09-30, OPEN
`lib/core_tools/calendar_tool/utils/google_calendar.py:377` does `print("Calendard ID DEBUG")` followed
by the calendar id list on every event fetch, so every noticer tick, brain run and calendar tool call
writes the household's calendar ids to stdout, outside the logger. Seen during the brain step-4 dry run.
Not fixed.

### A work object cites a concern that is not in the register — CONFIRMED 2026-09-30, OPEN
Checking every `constraints.concern_refs` against the concerns table (1,494 work objects, 103 refs): all
refs on active and done work resolve; one abandoned work object cites `concern:c4e8e1a7`, which matches no
concern. Suspected: the evaluator wrote a short id it was not shown, or the concern's id changed.
Consequence: that work's outcome could never reach a concern; dayflow now shows the ref as unresolved
(`concern_brief.briefs_for_refs`) instead of failing the render. Not investigated.

### Concern handoff item ids read as concern refs — CONFIRMED 2026-09-30, FIXED locally 2026-09-30
The first live handoffs (step 6) named intake items `concern:<concern id>:<brief basis>`. The steward
cited those ids in `based_on`, and `work_persist` lifts every `based_on` entry starting `concern:` into
`constraints.concern_refs`, so work_0472d99d4e5e and work_3150f6668f2d carried the item id as a second
"concern ref" beside the real one (added by `concern_refs_of`). That ref resolves to no concern:
concern outcome delivery would have raised on it at completion and retried every pass, and the agents'
brief view showed it unresolved. Fixed: items are `concern_handoff:<id>:<basis>`; the two work objects'
`concern_refs` were corrected through `revise_goal` (the stray entry removed). Needs a restart for the
new prefix; until then a new handoff would be named the old way.

### ask_user tickets do not name the work that asked — CONFIRMED 2026-09-30, FIXED locally 2026-09-30
`lib/tools/ask_user/ask_user.py::_ask_via_ticket` writes `trigger_context` with source, calling agent,
request id and room id only. When a dayflow worker (under `work_emi_team_manager`) asks, the ticket
carries no `work_node`, so the reply is not attributed to the work object in the ticket record and the
reading page (/read/<ticket_id>) cannot show the goal, concern or emails behind the question. Seen on
the four long ask_user tickets of 2026-09-30 ("I searched your … calendar and inbox and did not find…").
Dayflow's own ask/notify tickets (`create_dayflow_ticket`) do carry `work_node`. The work_execution_calls
receipts show those asks ran under work_818ab0956029's attempt, so the owner was known and dropped.
Fixed: the ticket carries `work_node` (`<work id>::<main node id>`) and `dispatch_epoch` from the
execution owner (`manager_runtime.execution.current_owner`) whenever the ask runs inside a work
attempt. Supersede and boot re-arm filter to dayflow ticket types, so they do not act on these.
Needs a restart.

### Email subjects reach the brain MIME-encoded — CONFIRMED 2026-09-30, OPEN
Of the 49 emails in brain_events over 2026-09-27..30, several subjects are raw RFC 2047 encoded-words
(`=?utf-8?B?UXVpY2s6IGRvZ3Mg...?=`), stored that way in the email pod's `subject` metadata and shown
verbatim by `email_pods.email_text` to the gate, the brain, the reading page and every other reader.
One is the owner's own email "Quick: dogs on Sep 29, 8:00–8:30?", which the gate saw only through its
body. A sender display name is affected the same way (`=?UTF-8?B?VGhlIE5ldyBZb3JrIFRpbWVz?=`).
Suspected cause: the fetch decodes some headers but not folded or non-ASCII ones (not yet read).
Consequence: readers lose the subject of exactly the non-ASCII mail. Not fixed.


### Concern status `addressing` meant "work finished" — CONFIRMED 2026-09-30, FIXED locally 2026-09-30
`persist.apply_work_outcome` moved a concern active→addressing when its work ENDED done, and nothing
changed the concern when work was created for it (only a journal line for brain handoffs,
`concern_handoff.record_intake_outcome`). So `addressing` held concerns whose work had stopped, while
concerns with work running stayed `active` (work stress sat `active` 15:22–17:38 on 2026-09-30 while
work_0472d99d4e5e ran, then went `addressing` when it finished). Finalizer judgments never reached
the concern, and the work's ending woke the noticer, not the brain: nobody resolved work stress after
the owner said "Nothing to be done now". Fixed (owner-approved): work is attached to the concern
(`attached_work`), receipts for attach / each judgment / ending (work_objects/concern_outbox.py);
`addressing` = attached work in progress; judgments and endings are brain inbox events; one-time
re-derivation at startup (persist.rederive_attached_work). Needs a restart.

### The noticer can still move a concern into or out of `addressing` — CONFIRMED by reading 2026-09-30, OPEN (design decision)
`persist._apply_noticer_output_locked` applies the noticer's `addressing_concerns` (active→addressing
with no work attached, e.g. the library fine on 2026-09-30 14:03: "the library-fine email was handled
by dayflow") and its re_escalate disposition (addressing→active while work may still be running).
Since 2026-09-30 `addressing` means attached work is in progress, set by code; these noticer paths
contradict it until the next attach or ending corrects the bucket. Not changed: the noticer's role is
being taken over by the brain; whether to remove these outputs is the owner's decision.

### The steward's abandon reasons are a fixed string, so dropped goals churn — CONFIRMED 2026-09-30, FIXED locally 2026-09-30
`work_persist.persist_steward_output` closes every `abandon_work_ids` entry with the same reason, "steward:
objective dropped (superseded, declined, or no longer relevant)"; the steward's output has no per-id
reason, and its actual judgment lives only in `evaluation_summary`, which is not written to the goal.
The RECENTLY DROPPED list the next pass reads therefore says nothing about why. Evidence 2026-09-30:
"Set the home Nest thermostat to 70°F … September 30 at 9:00 PM" was created and dropped four times
(01:07, 08:28, 10:38, 13:23 local; the last is work_ac12240af2ef), each with that string; the 13:23
reason, only in the summary, was "no linked Nest access". Consequence: a routine item is re-created and
re-dropped all day, and the owner cannot see why tonight's setpoint was cancelled. Fixed by the goal
ownership change (docs/design/dayflow_goal_ownership_2026-09-30.md): the steward has no abandon list; the
architect ends a goal with `end_goal`, whose reason is its epitaph. Needs a restart.

### One failed thermostat call is taken as "no Nest access" — CONFIRMED 2026-09-30, OPEN
The Sept 30 7:00 AM attempt to set the Nest to 75°F ended `unrecoverable`: the worker's call reported
