"no linked Google/Nest account or device access" and gave manual instructions. The Sept 29 9:00 PM
attempt set and verified the same Nest ("COOL, 70°F target, 78°F current"). The steward then abandoned
the Sept 30 9:00 PM setpoint because "prior execution confirmed there is no linked Nest access".
Confirmed from logs/emi_logs_51172.log and the work events: the 7:00 attempt WORKED — devices_manager's
nest_home_control returned "Nest applied: set_mode, set_target_temperature" and the worker recorded
success; the finalizer judged `retry` because the status read in the same call showed "target 74°F"
(the status before the change applied, or unit rounding: not established). The 7:02 retry called no tool:
work_emi_team::planner returned control at once and emi_team::final_answer (gpt-5-mini), with no tool
result, wrote "I don't have a linked Google/Nest account or device access"; the finalizer judged that
`unrecoverable` / `ask_user`, and the steward refused to recreate tonight's setpoint in every pass
after 13:23 ("prior execution established that device access is unavailable"). Three defects: a
finalizer that fails a confirmed action on a status read taken in the same call; a worker final answer
that reports a capability failure no tool returned; a finalizer that accepts it. Owner-requested
escalation 2026-09-30: work_02974a1106fd (tell the owner the report was false, ask whether to set 70°F at
9 PM). The defects are not fixed.
Cause of the 74°F, established 2026-09-30: it was a separate earlier call (`get_status: true`, a read
only, 07:00:33) before the applying call (07:00:40); the receipts showed neither arguments nor times.
Isolated replay of the 07:01 finalizer prompt: without times `retry` 4/5; with each receipt's local
time `achieved` 5/5. PARTLY FIXED locally 2026-09-30: receipts and every other time in dayflow and
brain prompts render in local time (`local_time` filter); the real render replays `achieved` 5/5.
Still OPEN: receipts lack the call's arguments; final_answer writes a failure no tool returned; the
finalizer accepts claims no receipt supports. FIXED 2026-10-01 (8358edba): on a retry the worker sees
the previous judgment (verdict, outcome, recommendation) and the main task's receipts from earlier
attempts; rendered for this task's attempt 2, the view shows "Nest applied: set_mode,
set_target_temperature" and "target 74°F" from attempt 1.

### A weekly habit lands in the day's routine on the wrong day — CONFIRMED 2026-09-30, FIXED locally 2026-09-30
Wednesday 2026-09-30 15:00 the owner got "Complete weekly timesheet" (work_0238877780b8), a Monday
routine. Chain: the dayflow_routine stage's belief selector chose B179 ("completes timesheets weekly …
usually aims to finish by 3–4 PM", no weekday) and not B195 ("weekly on Mondays … by 10:00 AM") or B62
("only one Monday 10:00 AM reminder"); the routine writer placed "Offer the fire-and-forget timesheet
command in the owner's usual 3–4 PM window" at 3:00 PM (resource_dayflow_routine.md, generated 14:14,
selection in resource_dayflow_routine_latest.json); the steward, whose prompt makes a clock-anchored
routine item real work, created the reminder. Consequence: a weekly or monthly habit whose day is not
in the selected beliefs is placed on whatever day the routine is written, and becomes a notification,
against the owner's stated reminder preference (B62). Also: the timesheet beliefs disagree on the day
(B195/B62 Monday, B455 Sunday). The selector read its catalog in pages of 48,000 characters in key
order, so B179 and B195 could be judged in different calls. Fixed: the beliefs were corrected on the
owner's words (B195, B455, B179 revised; B62, B92 retired; B541 added), and beliefs are grouped into
named topics (belief_engine/clusters.py) that the selector reads whole and the writer gets with each
selected belief; B179 and B195 share "Timesheet routines". Needs a restart.

### The belief intake files an owner's correction as "same", so the held belief is not corrected — CONFIRMED 2026-09-30, OPEN
Dry run of the owner's four timesheet statements through `belief_engine.intake.run.judge_atom` on a copy
of the belief tables: "Monthly timesheets are due usually by 5 PM and I like to get them in between 3–4 PM"
was judged `same` as B179 ("completes timesheets weekly … usually aims to finish by 3–4 PM"), and "Weekly
timesheets are due on Mondays" `same` as B195 ("… on Mondays, aims to submit them by 10:00 AM"). Only
`contradicts` leads to a revision, so both held statements would have stayed as they were, the owner's words
filed as supporting evidence; "end of day is usually fine" became a new belief beside B195's 10:00 AM.
Consequence: an owner correcting a belief through what they say does not change it; the /beliefs page's
correction path (revise + said evidence) is the only one that does. Applied that way on 2026-09-30:
B195, B455, B179 revised, B62 and B92 retired, B541 added. Not fixed in the intake.
Seen again 2026-10-02: the owner's "Trash day is wednesdays, unless there was holiday before it on same week,
then it moves to thursday" became the atom "trash day is Wednesday, moving to Thursday when a holiday falls
earlier in that same week", judged `same` as B330 ("puts the trash out on Wednesday nights"). The holiday rule
exists only as evidence on B330; no belief states it, so the calendar never reads it. A `same` verdict on an
atom that adds a condition or exception loses the new information.

### `test_message_task_information_fields_reach_template` fails on main — CONFIRMED 2026-09-30, OPEN (cause not investigated)
`app/assistant/tests/non_agent_tests/test_agent_input_context_item.py` expects "Primary user: Sam" in the
rendered prompt; it renders "Primary user: " (blank). Fails with and without the 2026-09-30 local-time
change (checked by stashing it). Whether the template, the context injector or the test fixture is wrong
is not established.

### Agent handoffs that lose the record — CONFIRMED by read-only inventory 2026-09-30, OPEN
Full map with file:line: `docs/design/handoff_inventory_2026-09-30.md` (+ its three detail files).
Intended: an agent that decides an action, verdict or end of work can see the record it rests on.
Actual, confirmed by reading (items re-read by hand marked so in the inventory):
- The node's result is `final_answer_answer` alone (`work_objects/result_recorder.py:31-41, 77`);
  sources, data list, what-was-done and the raw envelope are dropped.
- emi_team::final_answer cannot see the planner's findings or return_control text (`planner_result`
  is outside `HistoryFormatter`'s allowed set, `agent_runtime/services/history_formatter.py:13-19`).
- Work planner findings carry no `dispatch_epoch` (`WorkPlanner.py:123-131`), so `node_result`
  (`work_portfolio.py:57-60`) never includes them in the finalizer's "ITS FULL RESULT".
- `node_result` has one caller (the finalizer); every later agent sees only finalizer prose.
- `worker.j2` renders no finalizer judgment/failure count/epoch; `execution_state.j2` no receipts.
  FIXED 2026-10-01 (8358edba, `prior_attempt.j2`); provenance now labels evidence with its attempt.
- `architect_summary` is read by nothing, though prompts route three decisions into it; a declined
  steward end request is consumed and its answer lost.
- Receipts: no arguments, detail cut at 2000 chars, nested calls keyed to the main node, 20 per work
  object across all attempts.
- `hold_reason` not stored; `revise_objective.reason`, `wait_reason`, finalizer `abandon_node_ids`
  unread; steward RECENT OUTCOMES / RECENT NUDGES always empty.
Consequence: one misjudgment at final_answer or the finalizer is believed by every later agent (the
September 30 thermostat chain). PARTLY FIXED locally 2026-09-30 (trial, work_emi_team_manager
only): `WorkResultNode` replaces emi_team::final_answer there; the result is this attempt's planner
findings (tagged `finding_attempt`, deduped per attempt); an empty return goes back to the planner once,
then fails as "no result recorded". Other work managers still use a final-answer agent.

### Planner history never names the tool called — CONFIRMED 2026-09-30, FIXED locally 2026-10-01
`app/assistant/utils/history_formatting.py:171-180`: `_parse_tool_request` / `_parse_agent_request`
use raw strings with `\s`, which match a literal backslash, so neither pattern ever matches. The
planner's history labels each call with its result type and shows the whole "Calling tool ..."
sentence as arguments (probe run during the inventory). Intended: tool name and arguments separated.
Agent calls were never paired with their results either.
Fix (no regex; owner's rule): ToolCaller already holds the name and arguments, so it records them in
the request's metadata (`tool_name`/`agent_name`, `arguments`) and the formatter reads those; both
parse functions are deleted. A result's tool name now comes from its request, not its result_type.
Tests: `test_pipeline_contract.py` (both new tests fail on the old code).

### Smaller defects found by the handoff inventory — CONFIRMED by reading 2026-09-30, OPEN
- `app/assistant/agents/shared/work/worker.j2:9`: mojibake ("â€”") in the checklist header. FIXED
  2026-10-01 (8358edba).
- `context_sources.get_responded_tickets_categorized` swallows exceptions and returns empty, so the
  steward reads "no replies" on failure (fail-silent).
- `strategic_planner_wo_prep_node._abandoned_line` docstring says it shows the user's last reply; it
  renders no reply.
- SUSPECTED: work_emi_team::planner `system.j2:84-89` describes a `[User replied: ...]` content block
  that replies no longer produce.

### `test_mention_cross_room_addressing` fails only in a combined run — SUSPECTED test isolation, 2026-09-30, OPEN
`TestCrossRoomResolution` (4 tests) failed when run after the dayflow suite and related files, and passes
alone with and without the 2026-09-30 work-result change. Likely shared state leaking between tests; the
leaking test is not identified.

### Tests could open the live emi.db — CONFIRMED 2026-09-30, FIXED locally 2026-09-30
Intended: no test reads or writes the owner's databases. Actual: database choice was opt-in per test
module. `get_database_uri()` (app/models/base.py) returns emi.db unless USE_TEST_DB=true, and
`dayflow_work_db_path()` returns emi.db unless DAYFLOW_WORK_DB is set (it ignores USE_TEST_DB). A test
file run on its own, or a work-store test in any run, used the live file. Evidence: on 2026-09-30 an
isolated system_audit run emptied `system_audit_case`, created two active "Audit fixture WO" work
objects and inserted chat row `ul_evidence_1` in emi.db; test_oauth_token_refresh,
test_auth_bearer_materializer and the room harness smoke test opened emi.db directly on every run
(the two tool tests DELETEd their pod ids from it). Older abandoned test-titled work objects in emi.db
(August) suggest earlier leaks. Fix: the root conftest.py sets test-database defaults before any import
and wraps sqlite3.connect so any open of a live *.db (data dir or data/, not test_*/e2e_sandbox_*)
raises and fails the session even if swallowed (test_live_db_guard.py); the three offenders now use
their test databases. Full suite: 2887 passed, no live opens. Live-data cleanup of the fixture rows is
left to the owner (the auto-mode permission check refused the write).

### Routine belief selector writes belief numbers as selection ids; the routine fails — CONFIRMED 2026-10-01, FIXED locally 2026-10-01
`dayflow_routine` FAILED 2026-10-01 05:04 (run 8e17a24b): `belief_engine/matching/selection.py:29` "Unknown
selected belief IDs: ['S153', 'S286', 'S427']" after the one correction attempt. Pages hold ~100 beliefs
(S0-S106); B153, B286, B427 are real beliefs in that page's topic ("Sleep and nighttime routine"). The
selector chose the right beliefs and wrote each belief's number with the request-local S prefix. Cause:
`dayflow_belief_selector/prompts/user.j2` renders each record as raw JSON with three ids (`belief_key`,
`short_id`, `selection_id`). Most pages that night needed the retry. Proposed: one id per record — either
use the belief key itself as the selection id (validated against the page), or drop belief_key/short_id
from what the selector sees. `belief_engine/matching/service.py:99` uses the same select_records.
Owner's reading 2026-10-01: the instructions themselves contradicted — the form field was `belief_ids`
("The beliefs that…"), each record led with `belief_key` and a `short_id` holding the B value, `refines`
cross-referenced by B key, and one sentence said to return `selection_id` ("the supplied short
selection_id"). The belief engine's evidence_match had the same shape (records carried `id`, `belief_key`
and `selection_id`; form `belief_ids`). Fix: `select_records` uses each record's own belief_key as the only
id (no S labels); forms take `belief_keys` / `qualifier_keys`; prompts state one rule; the callers hide
short_id/cluster_id (routine) and the row id (evidence_match). Real selector on the failing 107-belief
page: valid keys first try.

### Context activation seeded from the owner's own node runs out its 300 s budget — CONFIRMED 2026-10-01, OPEN
2026-10-01 03:13 (emi_logs_39892.log.1): chat_scan seeds [the owner, a family member]; BFS from the owner's node
expanded 5035 nodes (10794 queued) in 5 minutes, the family member's seed got 0 expansions, wave 2 skipped, the brief was saved
without reasoning or memo (pipeline ERROR, 317.9 s). The owner's node is the graph's hub, so activation from
it is a whole-graph walk. Proposed: never seed the primary user (deterministic filter after chat_scan).
Related: project_context_engine_overeager_todo.

### Eight agent configs name model_tier 'fast', which model_tiers.yaml does not define — CONFIRMED 2026-10-01, FIXED locally 2026-10-02
`llm_factory.resolve_engine` warns "unknown tier 'fast'" and uses the configured engine as-is, so behavior
is the configured model; only the tier remap is lost. Configs: kg_date_gap_gate, kg_maintenance/
{evidence_digester, identity_sentence_writer, succession_judge}, subconscious/{answer_matcher, gate},
wiki_fact_answer_judge, wiki_fact_question_writer. Proposed: map them to an existing tier or define 'fast'.
Also `entity_card_critic` names model_tier 'standard', likewise undefined (found 2026-10-01).
Fixed 2026-10-02 with the tier switch below: every config names nano, mini or smart, and boot validation
(`agent_validator._check_llm_params_contract`) refuses a tier model_tiers.yaml does not define.

### OpenAI client could not run gpt-6 models and read preambles as the answer — CONFIRMED 2026-10-01, FIXED locally 2026-10-01
`app/services/llm_client.py`: `OpenAIModelCapabilityNormalizer.is_gpt5_family` matched only `gpt-5*`, so a
gpt-6 model was sent `temperature` and no reasoning effort; gpt-6-luna answers that with 400 "Unsupported
parameter: 'temperature'" (live probe 2026-10-01). Reasoning models can send a preamble as its own message
(`phase="commentary"`, Responses API since 2026-02-24) before the answer (`phase="final_answer"`).
`_extract_response_text` returned `output_text`, which joins every message, and `_parse_first_json_object`
skipped to the first `{` by regex, so a preamble containing a brace would be parsed as the answer. SDK 2.7.1's
`responses.parse` parsed every message, so a preamble failed the call (openai-python #3859, fixed in 3.14.1).
Fix: SDK 3.22.1; `is_reasoning_model` covers gpt-5 and gpt-6; the json_schema and prompt_json_validate
routes read only the final-answer message (or the one unlabelled message) and parse it strictly.
Tests: `test_openai_final_answer.py`; live: direct_parse and json_schema OK on gpt-6-luna and gpt-5.6-luna.
Not changed (owner to decide): `_parse_first_json_object` keeps its regex scan for OpenCodeLLM, whose schema
is only requested in the prompt; the `prompt_json_validate` route is selected by no config (unused).

### model_tier does not choose the model; the config's `engine` does — CONFIRMED 2026-10-01, FIXED locally 2026-10-02
`configs/model_tiers.yaml` says every agent using a tier "gets the new model automatically". It does not.
`agent_runtime/services/llm_client.py::call_structured_output` passes the agent's llm_params unchanged to
`LLMInterface.structured_output` → `OpenAILLM._structured_output_once`, which calls `send_params['engine']`.
The tier is read only by `LLMFactory.get_llm_interface` (per-provider singleton creation; its resolved
engine is discarded) and by provider fallback when the configured provider has no key. Evidence:
llm_call_log 14 d — `label` (engine gpt-5-mini, tier mini → resolves to gpt-5.6-luna) ran 7,715 calls on
gpt-5-mini; nano-tier configs with engine gpt-5.6-luna ran on gpt-5.6-luna, not gpt-5-nano. So 82 configs
still run gpt-5-mini, 7 run gpt-4o, 4 run gpt-4.1, whatever their tier says. Decision needed: make the tier
authoritative (and drop `engine` from configs) or drop `model_tier`. Also hardcoded models outside configs:
the image path forces `gpt-5.6-luna` (`agent_runtime/services/llm_client.py:303-304`), the fallback defaults
(same file, line 95), `OpenAILLM._init_once` default `gpt-4.1-mini`.
Fixed 2026-10-02 (owner: tier = model + effort, authoritative): a tier in `configs/model_tiers.yaml` names
the model per provider and the reasoning effort (nano none, mini low, smart medium; all gpt-6-luna on
OpenAI). `llm_factory.resolve_tier` resolves it on every call, including images and provider reroutes;
`engine` is removed from all 249 configs and refused at boot; the hard-coded `"medium"` effort, the image
path's model and the reroute defaults are gone; a reasoning model called without an effort raises.
`smart`: the 17 agents whose decisions are written down and believed (KG writes and merges, belief merges,
the dayflow steward, architect and finalizer); `nano`: dayflow switchboard and action_selector (12/12 and
3/3 agreement with no reasoning in the 2026-10-01 replay); everything else `mini`. Not changed:
`OpenAILLM._init_once`'s unused default engine, and the `DEFAULT_LLM_MODEL` environment variable (see below).

### llm_prices.json lists gpt-5.2 at the wrong price — CONFIRMED 2026-10-01, OPEN
`configs/llm_prices.json` has gpt-5.2 at $5.00 / $0.50 / $25.00 per 1M; OpenAI's pricing page (2026-10-01)
lists $1.75 / $0.175 / $14.00. No agent runs gpt-5.2, so no recorded cost is affected today.

### A ticket reply's first finalizer call is cancelled as "task abandoned or timed out" — SUSPECTED 2026-10-01, OPEN
logs/emi_logs_50068.log 09:43:13: the owner answered ticket dayflow_orchestrator_work_20261001_160019_e7950693
(work_a3ceced81bf0, re-armed after the 09:41 restart); a work_finalizer_node call raised ExecutionCancelled
"task abandoned or timed out" before the reply was recorded. The work session's own thread then recorded the
reply (09:43:34) and the finalizer judged it (09:43:36), so the judgment was not lost. Not established: which
path made the first call, and why the attempt it checked was revoked. Possibly the boot-recovery recorder
and the re-armed session both handling one reply.

### Every architect call received every answered ticket as a directive — CONFIRMED 2026-10-01, FIXED locally 2026-10-01
`work_architect_node._situational_context` rendered the tick's `recent_responded_tickets` (all answered
tickets, any goal or none) and active tickets under "RECENT TICKET RESPONSES (user directives — incorporate
these into the graph)", and passed that one block to every per-goal architect call; the system prompt called
those replies AUTHORITATIVE. 2026-10-01 17:02:56: work_fc1f128bf621 (approve, draft, send an email to a family member;
every step judged achieved) went stranded because its pruned approval step blocked the rollup; the
architect, given the owner's reply to the test goal work_a3ceced81bf0's question ("This was a debugging
mistake. This task should be aborted."), ended it `abandoned` "at the owner's explicit direction". The brain
journaled that attribution and the concern brief now quotes it as the owner's wish. Fix: the architect's
context carries no tickets (a goal's replies are recorded on its own asking step); the system prompt says so
and describes the portfolio as other goals' state, not instructions. Test: test_work_prompt_projections.
Related, FIXED 2026-10-01: the stranded prompt told the architect the goal "is not achieved" when only the
rollup did not fire (a pruned step). Owner 2026-10-01: the finalizer judges each completed node (this node
was worked on, this was its outcome); whether the goal is reached is the architect's judgment. The block now
says nothing is left to run, that worked steps show their finalized outcome and unrun ones why, and asks the
architect to end the goal as done when its outcome is reached (architect_task.j2; test in
test_work_prompt_projections). Also observed: the email sent was reworded from
the approved draft (the draft lived only in the ask_user arguments; the sender rewrote it); the owner judged
the result correct (signing as the assistant follows his standing rule), so not treated as a defect.

### The noticer keeps concerns in `addressing` with no work in progress — CONFIRMED 2026-10-01, OPEN
Contract (CLAUDE.md, concern outcome delivery): `addressing` means attached work is in progress; set on
attach, left when the last attached work ends (`persist.attach_work` / `apply_work_outcome`, both correct).
But every noticer pass emits `addressing_concerns`, and `persist.py:349` moves each named concern into
`addressing` regardless of attached work. 2026-10-01: four concerns sit in `addressing` with no live work —
fatigue/poor sleep (severity high; attached work done 2026-09-21; noticer: "already internally handled through
the prior wind-down intervention"), work stress (done; "does not want further support"), the Claude API
outage (abandoned; "the user said he would handle topping up"), a college-events concern (abandoned; "chose
to handle ... himself"). Consequence: a high-severity concern is presented as being handled while nothing is
in motion; "the owner has it in hand" and "settled" are recorded as work in progress. Proposed: concern
status follows attached work only; the noticer's addressing output is retired or carries no status change.
Also noted 2026-10-01: `concern_store.save_register` stamps every row's `updated_at` on each save, so the
column means "register last saved", not "this concern changed" (briefs key on their own basis hash; no cost).

### The routine writer never saw the qualifier beliefs — CONFIRMED 2026-10-01, FIXED locally 2026-10-01
Since the topics commit (aa2ce923) the belief selector names, for each selected belief, the beliefs of its
topic that state a condition on it (its day, a limit, an exception, a reminder wish), and the routine stage
passed them to `dayflow_routine_writer` as `related_beliefs_block`. The writer's config never declared that
item, and the context injector only renders declared items, so its prompt section was always skipped: four
writer prompts on 2026-10-01 had none, while the 22:48 routine had selected four qualifiers (B149, B211,
B205, B427). Fixed with the day pass move: the config declares it; in the first live replay the writer used
seven qualifiers (e.g. CPAP support limited to reminders, B427).

### The situation snapshot's "Today's Routine" was always empty — CONFIRMED 2026-10-01, FIXED locally 2026-10-01
`pipelines/dayflow/utils/situation_snapshot._build_routine` read `resources/resource_dayflow_routine.md`;
the routine was written to `resources/dayflow_pipeline_outputs/`, so the file never existed and the section
was silently dropped (its `except: pass` hid nothing, the path simply missed). Fixed: it renders today's plan
from `day_plan`.

### The old routine writer cited belief keys it was not given — OBSERVED 2026-10-01
The 22:48 routine (old markdown writer) cited B18, B156, B161 and B478, none of which were in its selected or
qualifier set. The day pass now refuses a plan citing a key it did not give the writer (`day_pass._validated`);
if that refusal recurs in practice, add one correction attempt as belief selection has.

### `DEFAULT_LLM_MODEL` no longer chooses a model — CONFIRMED 2026-10-02, OPEN (design decision)
Bootstrap sets it from `llm_classes_dict._PROVIDER_DEFAULT_MODEL` when it autodetects a provider, and
`LLMFactory.resolve_effective_params` used it as the engine when a provider was rerouted. With the tier
authoritative (2026-10-02) the reroute takes the tier's model on the new provider, so the variable now only
sets each provider class's unused construction default. Decide: remove it from bootstrap, `.env.example` and
`test_default_provider_autodetect`, or give it a meaning.

### ask_kg's prompt names a hard-coded model — CONFIRMED 2026-10-02, OPEN
`knowledge_graph_search.handle_ask_kg` sets `engine = "gpt-5.6-luna"` and passes it in the agent input;
`shared::ask_kg`'s user prompt renders it as "Engine: gpt-5.6-luna". It never chose the model (the agent's
config did), and after the 2026-10-02 switch it is false. It also appears in the tool result's `data`.
Three faults (owner, 2026-10-02): a model name has no place in the agent's prompt at all; it is chosen in
Python instead of by the tier; and it is false. Fix: remove `engine` from the tool, agent input, result data,
template and config. Queued as a todo.

### The action selector rewrites the node it picks, and the switchboard routes the rewrite — CONFIRMED 2026-10-02, FIXED locally 2026-10-02
Seija email, 2026-10-02 (work object for "Ensure Seija has the Friday Night Meats Zoom link"). The architect
wrote the send node with a conditional guard ("If [the owner] approved the exact draft, email Seija ... Do not send
without both [the owner]'s permission ... and approval of the final wording"). `action_selector` sees only the node
text and, through its form's `switchboard_task` / `switchboard_information` / `handoff_tf` fields (inherited
from the chat_gate pattern via `ChatTaskRouterNode`), restated the task as a question for the owner ("Ask
[the owner] whether he authorizes ..."). The switchboard routed on the restatement and chose
`create_dayflow_ticket`; the composer wrote a ticket saying the email was not sent. The send went out only on
the retry (epoch 2), which reached a worker. Consequence: the owner approved the email, then was asked again,
and the node's own goal never reached a handler on its first attempt. Proposed: the selector only picks;
`ActionSelectorRouterNode` stages the picked node from its record, as `WorkNodeWakeRouterNode` does.
Contributing (design, 2026-10-02 isolation runs, `scratch/replay/architect_isolation.py`): the standing
directions' email rules 1 and 3 split every outbound email into ask, draft, review and send; the steward
words goals conditionally ("if it has not already been handled"); and a node depending on an ask becomes
ready when the ask closes `achieved` even if the answer was no (`WorkObject.has_pending_revision` holds
dependents only for a finalizer instruction), which is what the architect's "If approved" guards hedge.
Checked 2026-10-02 and NOT a gap: the finalizer prompt makes an explicit denial `unrecoverable`/`stop`
(and the store forces it on a tool's approval denial), and all 33 recorded ask→act pairs since 2026-09-20
left the act unrun after a decline or an expired ask. The guards are redundant.
Also found: the router, a `ChatTaskRouterNode` subclass, posted the chat gate's "On it." acknowledgment to
the owner's master_room chat on every dayflow dispatch (log 2026-10-02 05:08:05, `[outbound] no reply_to —
defaulting to master_room`, len=6; 6 times in that process's log).
Fixed: the selector's form is `reasoning`, `acted_on_item_ids` (exactly one) and `no_op_tf`;
`ActionSelectorRouterNode` is a plain control node that loads the picked node and stages its own title and
goal with `node_dispatch.stage_for_switchboard`, shared with `WorkNodeWakeRouterNode`; the selector's
canonicalization moved to `node_dispatch.canonical_pick`; `flow_config.action_selector` is removed from both
dayflow managers. Test: `tests/dayflow/test_action_selector_router.py`.

### The steward re-created finished work from an already-handled ticket reply — CONFIRMED 2026-10-02, FIXED locally 2026-10-02
The steward's prompt listed every dayflow ticket reply from the last 12 hours as "TICKET REPLIES (user
directives …)", and its system prompt said "Ticket responses are direct instructions"
(`strategic_planner_wo_prep_node._build_situational_context`, `recent_ticket_replies`). The owner's 05:19
reply "Why was it not sent? please send it." was handled inside its own work object (the retry sent the
email at 05:21), but the steward re-read it as a fresh order and created five more Seija email work objects
between 06:19 and 07:46 (`work_8ea25896d6f9`, `257c394f140b`, `f437a90ff7ee`, `85c9bc921166`,
`1e7abd369acd`); each rationale cites that reply. No second email was sent only because each worker
searched the mail first. Same fault as the architect's (fixed 607119ea). Fixed: the reply list is
removed from the steward (`_load_responded_tickets`, `_annotate_work_refs` and
`context_sources.flatten_responded_tickets` removed with it); section 4 says a reply is its work node's
result, read in that work object's record and already acted on there. Replay of the four re-creating
calls with the new prompt (gpt-6-luna medium, 18 runs): no Seija work created.

### The day-plan writer puts 9 PM items in the 9 AM slot — CONFIRMED 2026-10-02, FIXED locally 2026-10-02
The 06:19 writer call (`brain_calls c7422997`) placed "set the AC to 70°F" (B198/B149, "9:00 PM") and
"last glass" (B146, "around 9 PM") in the 2026-10-02T09:00 slot; the steward created a 9:00 AM 70°F AC
work object and a 9:00 AM last-glass reminder from it (the AC one was ended by the steward at 07:59; the
reminder node was abandoned by the owner's directive at 08:22, before it fired). Replays of that prompt
misplace about 1 call in 10 at every setting tried, gpt-5.6-luna medium included. Ruled out (50–60 runs
each, no meaningful change): 24-hour slot keys vs keys equal to the 12-hour label; the system prompt's
24-hour examples; walking every slot vs returning lines with their own hour; B156's superseded
"around 9:00 AM" clause. Only 9 PM items move (8 PM, 10 PM never do), usually with text still saying
"At 9:00 PM". Untested: the placement rules "earliest useful moment" / "the hour slot where they first
matter". Harness: `scratch/replay/writer_ablation.py`, `slot_key_ab.py`.
Cause found (owner: "the writer never made a mistake in its months of operation", so look at what changed):
the 2026-10-01 day-pass change made the writer answer per slot (a form of 19 slot entries, each with its
lines) instead of one markdown document. Same 06:19 inputs, 60 replays per arm (`writer_bisect.py`): old
markdown writer 0 real misplacements; per-slot form 5 and 13 (with and without the concerns/conditions
blocks, which were ruled out: adding them to the old format gave 0). Fixed (owner's shape): the writer only
places beliefs, `(belief_key, time)` per time a belief applies today; code files each into its hour and
renders the belief's own statement. Replay of the new production prompt as of 06:19 (`new_writer_0619.py`):
B198 at 9 PM in 60 of 60; the one flag was B149's "75°F by the morning" half placed at 9 AM.

### The concern door's correction overturns a chained merge — CONFIRMED 2026-10-02, OPEN
`concern_door._problems` refuses `same_candidate` naming a candidate that was not decided `new`. At the
2026-10-02 17:35 day pass the door answered N1 (trash before bedtime) `same_open` C10 (the open concern
"reminders for low-frequency events such as the friend's Zoom, trash night, and Friday Night Meats") and N2
(same habit, its usual time) `same_candidate` N1. Both judgments are consistent: N2 joins whatever N1
joins. The correction text ("same_candidate must name a candidate decided new, N1 is not") gave the model
one way to comply, and it changed N1 to `new`: a separate standing concern `020060b6` for trash night was
created beside C10 (`brain_calls` 2026-10-03T00:35:50 and 00:35:53). The validation rule overturned a
judgment instead of resolving it. Consequence: a duplicate concern each time a batch holds two
candidates for one matter that already has an open concern. Whether trash night belongs in C10 (a
broader reminder preference) or its own standing concern is a separate judgment; the defect is that code
chose it. Not fixed (awaits the owner's go); the likely fix is to resolve `same_candidate` through its
target's decision in `plan_admission`.

### A brief that cites a miscopied ref is stuck until its concern changes — CONFIRMED 2026-10-02, FIXED locally 2026-10-02
Concern `2ab6df27` (Google Cloud payment declined) has had no current brief since 2026-10-01 21:49: the
brief writer cited `pod datapod:email:e88e363c16903945500f7f7` for the ref shown as
`…e88e363c16903945500f7f7f` (one character short); `concern_brief._problems` accepts only a shown ref
contained in the citation, the correction round repeated the miscopy, and `brief_error` holds the brief
until the record changes (`_failed_on_this_record`). Consequence: the concern planner waits for a
current brief, so the concern has no plan; the /mind card shows its older story with no decision.
Thirteen brief calls on 2026-10-01 for this concern. The gate and the door avoid this by labels (E1, C1)
mapped back by code; brief citations still made the model copy 24-character ids. Fixed (owner's go):
every source shown to the brief writer carries a label (S1, S2, ...) in its ref's place
(`concern_brief.label_sources`; the shared brain templates show a label where `source_labels` has one, so
the brain's own prompt is unchanged); the writer cites labels, code checks them and stores the refs they
stand for, so every reader of a brief still sees refs. A failed brief records the writer's revision
(`WRITER_REVISION`) and is tried again when it changes, without rewriting the briefs that succeeded
(raising WRITER_VERSION would rewrite every brief and plan each again).

### A work's judgment and its ending reach the brain as two matters — OBSERVED 2026-10-02, OPEN (suspected design gap)
When attached work finishes, the finalizer's judgment and the work's ending arrive as two `work` events
a minute apart (2026-10-02: `brain_events` 211 and 212 for `work_71c52f1ae5a2`; 209 and 210 for
`work_9ddbcd609b64`). The gate routes each when it arrives, so the brain reads each as its own matter,
noting the same fact twice on the concern, and the brief is written twice (00:23:33 and 00:24:28 UTC for
`e75a426c`). Visible on /mind as pairs of near-identical matters. Whether to hold work events until the
work ends, or group them by work id across wakes, is undecided.

### A standing concern's occurrence is handed over with the concern's rule, not what was placed at its time — CONFIRMED 2026-10-02, FIXED locally 2026-10-02
`concern_handoff.occurrence_meta` gives every occurrence the brief's single readiness task. At the
18:39 day pass, B140 ("sleep routine includes using a CPAP, adjusting the bedroom air conditioning, and
sometimes crating Bonnie and Clyde", placed 10:30 PM) was merged by the door into the AC concern
`750b0021` (rule: "Set the AC to 70°F at 9:00 PM"). At 18:45 the handoff
`concern_handoff:750b0021…:2026-10-02:10:30-pm` reached the steward as "At 10:30 PM today (2026-10-02):
Set the AC to 70°F at 9:00 PM." — self-contradictory. Consequence: work for the wrong act at the wrong
time whenever a concern holds beliefs about more than one act. The steward reviewed that item as
no_action (01:55 UTC: the 9 PM setting was already covered by active work). Fixed (owner's go): an
occurrence's summary is the statements of the beliefs placed at its time, read from today's plan
(`concern_handoff._placed_statements`), and its why carries the concern's rule as context. The door's
merge of a broad routine belief into a narrow concern is a separate, debatable judgment.

### The brain attributes a context turn's content to the event it decides, when a room's messages are split across matters — CONFIRMED 2026-10-02, PARTLY FIXED locally 2026-10-02
Event 206 (16:41, "yes, its they serve horses don't they", the owner's answer to the assistant's question
about which Bob's Burgers episode named Friday Night Meats) was routed by the gate to the Friday Night Meats
concern only (gate: "the owner confirms the Bob's Burgers reference"). The 16:43 messages ("Might not happen,
Seija is out Kayaking with friends") were routed to three concerns, so `brain_step.group_matters` (key: the
set of routed concerns) made them a separate matter (66). In matter 65 the brain saw the 16:43 turns as
unlabelled context and decided E1 with their content: "the owner says the gathering may not happen tonight
because Seija is kayaking", noting it on the concern (brain_calls 2026-10-02T23:52:07). Consequences: a
wrong reason recorded for the event; the same fact noted twice (matters 65 and 66); /mind told the owner
"You messaged the assistant asking whether they serve horses". Proposed (awaiting the owner): the brain prompt says
each labelled event is decided on its own words, unlabelled turns being context only; and/or one matter per
room per gate pass. Fixed (owner's go): the brain's prompt says each event is decided on its own words,
read in its conversation, and an unlabelled line is decided as its own event in its own matter; the story
agent now reads each matter's events in their conversations (`brain_stories._conversation`). Open: one
matter per room per gate pass (not chosen).

### The brain does not learn whose school an email is about: knowledge-graph lookup is exact-name only — CONFIRMED 2026-10-02, FIXED locally 2026-10-02 (a, c)
The 2026-10-02 14:14 Woodbridge High announcement (brain event 186) became three concerns about "the
student" (senior trip, YoungArts, Homecoming); /mind told the owner "the student" throughout. The brain's
knowledge-graph context (`kg_links.find_entities`, exact label or alias, whole words) found "Google", "Sam"
(a name in the Homecoming court list) and "Game" ("Game of the Week"), all noise, and not the school: the
email says "@ WHS" and "Woodbridge students", while the node is labelled "Woodbridge High School" with no
aliases and an empty description (a duplicate "Woodbridge High" node also exists). Even matched, their son would
not appear: "their son attends Woodbridge High School" is in their son's description and their link, and
`kg_links.shared` shows links only between two or more entities found. Options put to the owner: show the
household's ties to each entity found; add aliases and merge the duplicate; retrieve knowledge-graph facts
by meaning alongside names. Single-word generic labels ("Game") also match capitalized common words.
Fixed (owner chose a and c): every entity found carries its ties to the household's people
(`kg_links.household_ties`: the school's node now brings "their son's school is Woodbridge High School.");
the brain and the brief writer also get the knowledge graph's link sentences closest in meaning to the
events (`kg_links.facts_by_meaning`, each text embedded whole: an offline probe on this email ranked
their son's school facts 1st to 5th of 13,790). Open: the school node's missing aliases and its duplicate
"Woodbridge High" (data); generic single-word labels still match.

### The belief selector is not told the date or the weekday: trash night on a Friday — CONFIRMED 2026-10-02, FIXED locally 2026-10-02
On Friday 2026-10-02 the owner was asked about trash night. The day pass (04:50 UTC) selected B157 ("trash
taken out before bedtime") and B267 ("usually around 10 PM") and not B330 ("puts the trash out on Wednesday
nights"), though B330 was on the same selector page in the same topic ("Household trash"). The selector's
prompt carried no date and no weekday ("Friday" appeared only inside "Friday Night Meats"), so a belief's
day could not decide its topic. Regression: until 2026-10-02 the daily context held the hour grid, whose
slot keys carried the date (never the weekday); the placements rewrite (032802bc) replaced the grid with a
schedule list and the date went with it. Fixed: the selector is told "Today is <weekday> <date>, <time>"
(`day_pass._select_beliefs(today=...)`), as the writer is.

### An occurrence after midnight was judged passed at the start of the day — CONFIRMED 2026-10-02, FIXED locally 2026-10-02
`concern_handoff._occurrence_passed` put every clock time on the plan's date, so "12:30 AM" (after
midnight, still the same plan day, which runs to the 5 AM boundary) was read as that morning and never
handed over. Found by the occurrence test at 22:39. Fixed: times are placed inside the day's window
(`pipelines/context.day_window_local`); one before the boundary hour is on the next calendar date.

### The planner's tool results are recorded twice per wake — OBSERVED 2026-10-02, OPEN
`brain_steps` shows each `plan` tool result twice (e.g. two `todo_tasks` "Retrieved 4 task(s)" at
01:45:15). `concern_planner._invoke` records every `tool_result` message on the manager's blackboard; the
blackboard apparently holds two per tool call. Cause not yet read.

### B198 said the opposite of the owner: a misjudged contradiction, revised into a dated flip — CONFIRMED 2026-10-03, FIXED (belief retired; revision prompt fixed)
B198's origin (2026-04-07, the owner): "the nest cannot be configured on automated schedule… you just need
to issue the command to do it every day", and "There is no other scheduler than you." On 2026-06-13 the
owner asked "why is the AC set to reminder instead of just doing it?" and answered a "recurring nightly
automation for the Nest" ticket with "This should be recurring every night." Belief intake judged that a
contradiction (it is the same position: the assistant does it each time, no reminder, no device schedule) and
the revision rewrote B198 as "Until June 13 … did not want Nest actions placed on an automated schedule …
since June 13 … wants the AC set to 70°F automatically every night … as a recurring task". Downstream: the AC
concern's brief asked whether "the recurring automation is configured", the steward made "Configure the Nest
thermostat … each night" (work_fd405350a76f, abandoned), the worker asked the owner how. Work-outcome
feedback added five entries saying a night's AC run "does not establish that a recurring nightly task is in
place". Fixed: B198 retired at the owner's word; `belief_engine::belief_revise` now writes the present state
alone, the history staying in the evidence (it had been told "one dated clause says what held before and until
when", 4487db48). Open: why intake judged "do it, don't remind me" a contradiction of "you are the only
scheduler"; 12 other active beliefs still carry history clauses (B1, B35, B64, B73, B78, B98, B99, B129, B149,
B161, B193, B440), each to be checked for the same misreading before rewriting.
Update 2026-10-03: the intake no longer revises a belief on a contradiction. The new belief is held `pending`
with the beliefs it contradicts, and the owner keeps the new one, the old one, or both in the morning review
(/beliefs/review; belief_engine/intake/run.judge_atom, IntakeStore.hold / resolve_contradiction). The
belief_revise agent has no caller left. Work-outcome feedback (belief_engine/work_feedback.py) can still revise a
belief on its own.

### The live belief catalog has no decay — CONFIRMED 2026-10-03, OPEN (design decision)
Decay (temporary beliefs deprecated after 30 days unconfirmed, chronic ones contested after 180) lived in the
legacy belief_engine pipeline, retired at the 2026-09-29 cutover (routine `belief_engine`, "RETIRED"). The
intake store that replaced it holds no strength or decay ("There is no confidence band: an intake belief is a
view of its evidence", belief_engine/intake/catalog.py); nothing replaced the step. Separately, work-outcome
feedback records "support" evidence on each run of a belief's act, so a decay keyed on last confirmation would
not have retired B198 either.

### The day pass read retired beliefs: the catalog export is rewritten only by some writers — CONFIRMED 2026-10-03, OPEN (export refreshed by hand)
The day pass, the health and entertainment stages and the routes read the belief catalog from the export
`resources/kg_derived/resource_user_beliefs.json`, not from the intake store. The export is rewritten by the
/beliefs route after its writes (routes/beliefs.py), by the intake and pipeline routine adapters, and by
belief_tag_v1. A write straight through `IntakeStore` (retire, merge, revise) leaves it as it was. On
2026-10-03 B198 and 167 other beliefs were retired and 11 duplicates merged through the store: the store held
370 active beliefs, the export, generated at 01:00, still 549, B198 among them, so every day pass placed B198
("sets the AC to 70°F automatically… as a recurring task") again. Found while replaying old calendars through
the new day pass. The export was regenerated by hand (`python -m belief_engine.export.export_beliefs`, 370).
Open: the store's writes do not keep the export current; a reader that needs the current catalog reads a copy
that is as old as its last writer's export.

### Intake and daily insights drop what an owner's reply refers to — CONFIRMED 2026-10-03, OPEN
The owner answered a dayflow question ("…Friday Night Meats runs until 10:00 PM. Would you like to keep the walk
at 9:00 PM…") with "On Fridays if there is a meeting (there is no meeting every time) I just walk the dogs whenever
the meeting ends." The evidence row keeps the question (`in_reply_to`), but the belief intake's atom says "On
Fridays when the owner has a meeting…" and the insights page says "When a Friday meeting is happening…": which meeting
(Friday Night Meats, not the Friday 9:15 standup) is dropped. The atom was judged `same` as B10, whose February
wording names Friday Night Meats, so the catalog kept the meaning by luck; as a new belief it would have been
ambiguous. Intended: an atom made from a reply states what the reply refers to, read from the message it answers.
Affected: the intake atomizer and the daily insights writer (prompts not yet read for this). Seen on the insights
page by the owner.

### The brain made a concern out of dayflow's own work in progress and handed it back — CONFIRMED 2026-10-04, OPEN
The calendar placed the morning briefing (B94); dayflow made work_9b2c0e852a91. Its first judgment was `retry`
(no national or politics item yet). That judgment reached the brain inbox as a work event with no concern
named. The gate routed it `new_matter`, the brain recorded it `tracked_as_new`, and the door created the concern
"Complete and deliver the owner's October 3 morning briefing" (66321843, 14:37 UTC). Its planner chose
`act_now` ("delivery is still outstanding") and handed it to dayflow at 15:14, while the original work was
already delivering. The steward reviewed the handoff `no_action` ("Do not duplicate delivery"), so nothing was
sent twice. Only the steward prevented a duplicate; the door, brief, planner and steward each spent a call on
it. Intended: an outcome of work dayflow still owns is a report on that work, not a new matter. Suspected
cause (gate prompt not yet read): since beliefs stopped being concerns (2026-10-03), calendar work cites no
concern, so its judgments and endings reach the gate with no owner. On 2026-10-03 the gate also routed four
other calendar outcomes `new_matter` (phone-charge reminder, snack reminder, 9 PM dog-walk heads-up); the brain
judged each not worth tracking.
Update 2026-10-04: built, uncommitted (docs/design/work_progress_for_outside_readers_2026-10-04.md).
Every work report and concern record now says where the work stands, including the steps the assistant still
does in it. Replayed on this case: the gate routes the retry `none` 5/5, and the concern planner decides
`no_action` 17/20 (6/20 before). The ownership of work no concern holds is not built.

### A finalizer's "do not retry this attempt" became the concern's rule never to ask again — CONFIRMED 2026-10-04, OPEN (design decision)
Concern d9b3f7a2 (a son's October 5 college-planning events: a parent workshop overlapping a quiz). Dayflow
asked the owner once (work_bccf649f6e15); the ticket expired unanswered; the finalizer judged it
`unrecoverable`, `stop`. The brain wrote into the concern's journal: "Do not send a reminder or recreate the
prompt through another channel." At 21:36 the disposition read "retain the concern only until the event passes,
without further contact", and the plan holds until 2026-10-06 00:00, after the events. So one missed ticket
means the owner is never asked before the date. Intended (to be decided): a finalizer's `stop` ends that
attempt; whether and how to ask again is the concern's decision, made from its deadline.

Update 2026-10-04: diagnosed and changed, uncommitted. The finalizer's account was right ("expired unanswered
… does not establish that he received it"). Its verdict, recommendation and next step, which are instructions
to the architect, reached the brain through the past-work view (`shared/brain/past_work.j2` rendered
`finalizer_summary.j2`), which carried RECOMMENDS: "do not send a reminder or recreate the prompt". The brain
copied that into the concern's journal and the brief filed it under "the owner's wishes". Changed: readers outside
dayflow (past work, concern records, work reports, the concern story) show the finalizer's account without the
verdict or next step. The concern decider sees the concern's severity, and weighs an unanswered question by
importance and date. A stop inside a piece of work ends that work; only the owner's words settle whether to ask
again. Chain replay (`scratch/replay/account_only_chain.py`, 10 each): minor concern let go 9/10, owner asked:
asked again 7/10, severity high: asked again 10/10 (before: 0/10 asked in every case). Still open: the architect's
ending reason repeats the stop in prose, and the brain's note on the ending carries it 5/5; the one-hour ask
window (`ASK_WINDOW_HOURS = 1`).
### Work-outcome feedback counts the assistant's own acknowledged reminder as support for the belief behind it — CONFIRMED 2026-10-04, OPEN
`belief_engine/work_feedback.py` wrote 24 evidence rows from work outcomes on 2026-10-03. Rows marked `support`
include B143 ("routinely handles the nighttime walk") from an abandoned dog-walk heads-up, with this text: "does
not establish that the owner handles it". There are also B26 and B135 from an 8 PM heads-up the owner acknowledged
with OK. A reminder the belief caused, then acknowledged, is not independent evidence that the belief holds.
Consequence: once strength bands are built (belief_maintenance_2026-10-03.md, section 1), every belief placed
on the calendar would strengthen itself each day. Intended: only what the outcome shows about the world (the
owner's words, an observed act) is evidence.

### Brain runs failed for three hours: the routine writer's deleted weekly template was still loaded — CONFIRMED 2026-10-04, FIXED by restart
From 23:55 to 02:38 UTC (2026-10-03, local 16:55–19:38), 19 brain runs failed with "TemplateNotFound:
dayflow_routine_writer/prompts/weekly.j2". The template was deleted in the working tree while the running
Python still rendered it. The exception raised in the day pass, after the gate, brain step, briefs, planner
and handoffs of each run had committed. The day pass, its occurrence handoffs and the stories did not run in
that window, and the run summaries were lost. The restart at 02:50 loaded the code without the template, and
runs are clean since. This is the known half-applied deploy (templates hot-reload, Python does not).

### read_work_object cuts every record at 1,500 characters and reports a failed load as "not found" — CONFIRMED 2026-10-04, OPEN
`app/assistant/lib/tools/read_work_object/read_work_object.py`: `_render` slices the goal and every node's
content to `_MAX_NODE_CHARS = 1500`, so a long finding reaches the agent cut off with no mark. Any exception
from `store.load` returns None, which the tool reports as "work object not found", so a store error reads
as a wrong id. Found while adding the work's progress to the tool (2026-10-04); not changed then.
Intended: whole records (the no-slicing rule), and a load failure that fails loud.

### The chat gate ran to Gemini's output ceiling: a 188-second reply — CONFIRMED 2026-10-04, OPEN
The owner wrote "I scheduled my physical for october 13 8.40AM" at 07:24:03 local; the reply came at 07:27:13.
The whole delay was one call: `master_room::chat_gate` on `gemini-3-flash-preview` with Gemini's default
thinking (the gate sets no thinking budget). It returned 63,003 output tokens (the ceiling is about 65.5k) in
188 s, for a one-line routing decision, and was logged `ok` ($0.19). The client's degenerate-response retry
(empty, MAX_TOKENS truncation, unparseable) did not fire, probably because the JSON still parsed. The same
happened to the chat gate on 2026-08-21 (63,010 tokens, 184 s) and to situation_auditor three times on
2026-08-07 (about 63k each). Since 2026-09-20 it is 1 of 123 Gemini calls (`llm_call_log`). Options, not
decided: a thinking budget for the gate (replay first), a short gate timeout with retry, treating output at
the ceiling as degenerate, or moving the gate to gpt-6-luna (owner decision: the gate stays Gemini).

### An internal "already in flight" marker was sent to the owner as the chat reply — OBSERVED 2026-10-04, OPEN
To "I will start the timesheets at 5PM or so" (07:21:35 local) the master room replied, verbatim:
"[THIS TASK IS ALREADY IN FLIGHT - DO NOT RE-ATTEMPT]
Task: [the owner] starting timesheets at 5 PM"
(unified_log_2026, 07:22:20). A marker meant for agents reached the owner as the assistant's message. The gate's own
answer (from the agents log) was a normal reply, "Copy that. 5 PM-ish it is…", with `track_tf` true; the
tracking path's internal note replaced it (suspected in `master_room_chat_task_router_node`; not yet read).
Update 2026-10-04 07:45 local: it happened again on gpt-6-luna (the chat gate's trial model), so it is not a
Gemini quirk. To "yes add to calendar" the room sent three messages within nine seconds: the marker
"[THIS TASK IS ALREADY IN FLIGHT - DO NOT RE-ATTEMPT] Task: Add [the owner]'s physical appointment to the
calendar ... Args: (none)", then a raw result in UTC ("Calendar set: ... from 2026-10-13T15:40:00Z to
2026-10-13T16:40:00Z"), then the formatted reply ("on the calendar for October 13, 2026, from 8:40 AM to
9:40 AM"). Every handoff turn seen so far leaks the marker; the raw UTC line breaks the local-times rule.

### The todo tools never said whose list they write to: a done todo became a three-question spiral — CONFIRMED 2026-10-04, FIXED (needs a restart)
work_3707bf2dbe2f ("Set a ToDo for [the owner] to get a head start on his weekly timesheets", made by the steward
from the approved calendar belief B455, "sometimes likes to get a head start on his timesheets on Sunday"). Step 1
found and reused the todo the owner already had. The worker then reported "the task record does not indicate an
assignment to [the owner]", the finalizer judged the step unrecoverable, and the work asked the owner which task system
to use, checked whether "the Tasks list" is his Google todo app, and readied a ticket asking him to authorize Google
Tasks. The todo tools write to TASKS_GOOGLE_ACCOUNT_ID (default google_user_primary, the owner's own account), but their
descriptions said only "Use this tool to create a todo task." Fixed: the create/get/update/delete descriptions
(prompt and contract) say the lists are the user's own Google Tasks, the to-do app on their phone, and that a task
created there is the user's, with no assigning step. The owner answered "I'll handle it myself".
Underlying (open, design): the steward invents an act for every approved calendar item. The same belief also
produced a 5 PM heads-up, while the owner had told chat "I'll start at 5". See the direction memory: one decider of what
work exists (the brain), and what the assistant does with each calendar item decided in the morning review.

### Work made from a concern handoff lost its concern: cited the item, carried no concern_refs — OBSERVED 2026-10-04, OPEN
Concern 985a64d7 ("Recharge the Front Door Ring Video Doorbell 3 battery", opened by the brain from Ring's email at
03:25 local). Its first handoff became work_8c0a28de107d (9 AM notice), linked by concern_refs as designed. After
the plan changed, the second handoff (concern_handoff:985a64d7…:2749ae7e9818) became work_0299ad85cbe5 ("Check the
battery status … whether it has been recharged", 8:30 PM). The steward cited the handoff in based_on, but the work
object has concern_refs [] and source_intake []. work_persist takes concern_refs from `concern:` citations plus
work_intake.concern_refs_of(source_records(admitted_artifacts, based_on)); suspected: the handoff item was not
among that tick's admitted artifacts, so no source record resolved and the concern was dropped silently. Its
outcome reaches the concern only if the gate matches it by meaning. Intended: work made from a handoff item always
carries that item's concern, and a citation that resolves to nothing fails loud. Not yet read in code.

### The concern journal reaches agents in UTC: the planner called a 9 AM notice "now-past" at 3:31 AM — CONFIRMED 2026-10-04, FIXED (needs a restart)
Ring concern 985a64d7. At 03:31 local the concern planner handed the concern to dayflow a second time to "correct
the now-past 9:00 AM notification scheduled in work_8c0a28de107d" ("The scheduled notification time has passed").
The notice was five and a half hours away; the steward checked and reviewed the handoff no_action. The planner reads
the concern's journal raw (concern_planner.concern_text appends `reinforcement_notes`), and every journal line is
stamped in UTC ("[2026-10-04T10:25:40.778630+00:00] …"), while the prompt's Current Time is local. The 2026-09-30
local-times fix (every time an agent sees is local) did not cover the journal. The brain's concern view
(shared/brain/concern.j2) renders the journal too. Intended: journal stamps shown local, like every other time.
Fixed 2026-10-04 (uncommitted, needs a restart): `persist.journal_for_prompt` shows each stamped entry through the
shared `local_time_text` (stamps parsed with `parse_iso_utc`); the brain's concern view, the concern door, the
concern planner and the noticer read the journal through it. Storage stays UTC.

### A work object with no tasks is "created" again every tick, and the architect's decision to end it is dropped — CONFIRMED 2026-10-04, FIXED (needs a restart)
work_5af7a42b2e83 ("Give [the owner] a brief heads-up at 2:00 PM on October 2 …", created 2026-10-02) has only its goal
node. `work_architect_node._undecomposed_goals` treats every active work object without a main task as freshly
created ("recover initial decomposition from durable empty graphs after a lost tick"), so each planning tick sends
it to the architect's create branch. The architect answers, correctly, `end_goal: abandoned` ("its useful moment
has passed"), but the create branch calls `apply_architect_dag(store, work_id, nodes, ...)` with the nodes only;
`end_goal` and `revise_objective` are passed only by the re-plan branch. The log shows "ended: None" each time, and
the goal is asked again about ten minutes later. Architect calls per day: 49 (09-30), 56 (10-01), then 122, 141,
142 (10-02 to 10-04); about 40 of today's were this one goal ($0.14 today in all). The loop also fills the
architect's 50 recorded calls (brain_calls), pushing out the records replays need. Intended: the create branch
honours end_goal and revise_objective as the re-plan branch does, and delivers the ending.
Fixed 2026-10-04 (needs a restart): the create branch in `work_architect_node` passes end_goal and
revise_objective to `apply_architect_dag` and delivers the ending (test_architect_ends_a_moot_goal.py).

### test_no_signal_when_nothing_else_ready fails in full-suite runs and passes alone — OBSERVED 2026-10-03 and 10-04, OPEN
`app/assistant/tests/dayflow/test_job_thread_dispatch.py::TestOneDispatchPerTick::test_no_signal_when_nothing_else_ready`
failed in the full suite on 2026-10-03 and again on 2026-10-04 (`signals == ['work_…::n1']`, the step just
dispatched reported as more work ready), and passes when its file runs alone (24/24). The dayflow conftest wipes
the work store before and after each test, so leftover work objects are not the cause; the test may race with
something left running by an earlier test (a thread or a timer). Not investigated.


### The calendar tool shows UTC times to every agent that reads it — CONFIRMED 2026-10-04, FIXED (needs a restart)
`get_calendar_events` (core_tools/calendar_tool.py) wrote each event line as `- <title> @ 2026-10-12T07:00:00+00:00`
and its header as `from 2026-10-05T05:39:52Z to …`: the stored UTC instant. Its text is what the brain step's
30-day calendar (`brain_step._calendar`), the noticer's three calendar windows (`context_builder._fetch_calendar_text`)
and every chat or worker call of the tool read. All-day events (holidays) showed as 07:00 UTC, the instant of their
local midnight. Found while building the brain's situation (2026-10-04). Intended: agents read local time; storage
and the API stay UTC. Fixed 2026-10-04 (needs a restart): the header and each line go through the shared
`local_time_text`; an all-day event shows its local day, "(all day)". The anchors the noticer adds by title
(`_with_calendar_anchors`) are unaffected.

### Calendar-made work that cites only `belief:<key>`, or nothing, is not recognized as calendar work — CONFIRMED 2026-10-04, FIXED (part), OPEN (part)
`work_links.active_work` marked work `from_calendar` only when an intake item of source type `belief` was in its

timesheets heads-up, 2026-10-04) carries `belief_refs: ['B455']` and no source, so the shadow day decision would
have been shown it as non-calendar work. Fixed 2026-10-04: `work_links.from_calendar` also counts `belief_refs`,
one definition read by `active_work` and the brain's situation. Open: work_3615ecbedf79 (the 9 PM dog-walk reminder,
2026-10-04) cites nothing at all; work made that way cannot be told apart, which is the owner-of-record gap the
brain-delegates design closes (every work object made from a list entry carries its id).
