### Tool cards cut every tool's description in the planners' prompts — CONFIRMED 2026-10-04, FIXED (needs a restart)
`ToolRegistry._format_tool_card_with_hints` (the tool list every planner, switchboard and router reads) and
`_format_compact_tool_card` showed a tool's description only to its first sentence or line, then capped it at 140
characters (200 for a curated planner_description), and each input's hint to its first sentence, capped at 120.
The switchboard read work_emi_team_manager as "…carries out a piece of work end to end and reports what it" (its
description wraps after "it"), without the sentence that says where work ends. On 2026-10-04 at 10:30 PM the dayflow
switchboard sent "Remind [the owner] at 10:30 PM … to make a bathroom visit before bed" to the work team ("a reminder
record … which a tool must create"); the work team, which cannot ticket, scheduled a fixed reminder and reported
delivery unconfirmed. 1 of 32 reminder-type nodes in the 50 recorded switchboard calls; replayed 10 times at effort
none, 10/10 ticket, at low 9/10. Intended (owner: "no fucking truncating things in prompts"): every word of a
description reaches the prompt; a description too long is rewritten at its source. Fixed 2026-10-04: both cards use
the whole planner_description or description and the whole input description, whitespace collapsed to one line.
All 171 tools' cards: 43,931 characters before, about 94,800 after (each agent sees only its own tools).
Same incident, FIXED 2026-10-04 (the prompt is live at once; the tool list and descriptions load at boot): the
ticket tool's description said what it is made of, not what it is for; the work team's described the boundary as
"reach the user and wait for their answer" (a reminder waits for none); the switchboard prompt taught "Set a
reminder … → work" and was shown every tool (`allowed_tools: all`) though it chooses between two. Now (owner's
rule): a step that notifies, reminds, tells, nudges or asks the owner goes to the ticket; every other step to the
work team, an email or message to anyone and a todo or calendar event included. The switchboard sees only those two
tools; the ticket tool says it is how the assistant reaches the user, the work team's says what the assistant does.
Replayed on the 50 recorded switchboard calls, three times: only the bathroom step changes route (to the ticket);
the bathroom step 60/60 ticket. Without "a todo or a calendar event" the timesheets todo step (2026-10-04 06:13)
went to the ticket.

### The day pass placed the monthly timesheets on the 3rd business day; its own output came back as fact — CONFIRMED 2026-10-05, FIXED (needs a restart)
`subconscious/day_pass.run_day_pass` (the brain's day pass; writer `dayflow_routine_writer`, tier mini) placed the
monthly timesheet beliefs (B179 "due by 5 PM on the first business day of each month", B457, B574) on Monday,
October 5, 2026, the 3rd business day, in 25 of 30 replays of today's 6 recorded calls (B288, the clock-in reminder,
in 3 of 30). Causes found by reading the whole rendered prompt and the writer's reasons:
1. No date facts: the header named only the weekday and the date; the rule named only weekdays. FIXED: `_day_facts`
   (day of the month, business day n of m counting Monday to Friday, the first and last business days, nth weekday),
   rendered in the writer's user.j2, and a system rule for a belief tied to a day of the month or business day.
2. The header named the clock's weekday beside the boundary day's date (before the boundary hour: "Monday,
   2026-10-04"). FIXED: the day is the planned day; the clock is shown apart.
3. Noise lines in beliefs.j2 (kind, tags, observation count, an always-empty conditions line). FIXED: removed; the
   first-seen and last-confirmed dates kept as readable text.
   With 1-3: 13 of 30.
4. The writer lists a belief it judged not to apply: its reason reads "business day 3, so this does not apply" with
   the belief placed "all day". A not_today list beside the placements did not stop it (13 of 30). FIXED: one
   judgment per approved belief (agent_form Judgment: belief_key, why, applies_today, times); code places those that
   apply (`day_pass._placements`) and asks once for a correction when an approved belief is judged twice or not at
   all. 6 of 30 with the day theme, all but one in the call whose theme is wrong (item 5).
5. A loop: the daily_context_tracker reads the day plan (`resource_dayflow_routine`, rendered under "Routine
   (day-specific)") and writes the day theme, which it carries forward all day as its previous context; the writer
   reads the day theme. After the writer placed the timesheets, the theme read "monthly timesheets are due by 5 PM
   on this first business day of the month", and the writer's reasons cite that sentence over the computed facts.
   The same theme reaches every reader of the daily context (the brain's situation, the architect, the steward).
   Candidate 4 without the day theme: 1 of 30 (B457, "flagged near the first business day", judged near). FIXED
   (owner: both): the tracker no longer reads the day plan, and the writer no longer reads the day theme (nor does
   the plan's input fingerprint). With everything, the shipped files, today's 6 calls x 5 with the correction round:
   no monthly item in 30, B288 once; 29 valid, 1 still missing B8 (the last belief listed) after its correction,
   which raises and keeps the previous plan. The model tends to stop before the end of the list (B8, B61, B614 in
   5 of 30 first answers): OBSERVED, not fixed.
Consequence: the plan carried the monthly timesheets most of the day; with the calendar lane live, day_tasks would
have decided on them, and until fix 1 of the same day a placement that dropped out of a re-run cancelled its entry
and its work (`brain_list.apply_calendar`, FIXED 2026-10-05: only a decision ends an entry).
Replays: scratch/replay/writer_day_facts.py, writer_not_today.py, writer_judged.py, writer_final.py.
Live after the restart (2026-10-05 12:32 PM): the first answer judged 43 of 45 (B556, B576 left out), the
correction 44 (B61 left out), so the day pass raised and the 11:32 plan stayed. FIXED (owner: "break it into
batches of 10"): `day_pass.BATCH` = 10 approved beliefs per writer call, each batch with its own correction.
Today's live inputs, 5 day passes per arm, each call with its correction: all 45 at low effort, 2 of 5 first
answers incomplete (corrected); all 45 at medium, 0 of 5 incomplete but B457 ("flagged near the first business
day") applied in 3 of 5; batches of 10 at low, 0 of 25 incomplete and B457 0 of 5; tokens and seconds about
equal. Owner: keep batches (scratch/replay/writer_batches.py, writer_effort.py).
6. The loop also reached today's record: the evolving calendar held "Monthly timesheets 3:00 PM - 5:00 PM",
   source "inferred", no update time, added while the tracker read the plan and carried forward from its previous
   output; the writer then judged B574, B396 and B274 to apply "because monthly timesheets are scheduled today"
   (5 of 5 in every arm). FIXED in the tracker's prompt (live at once): an inferred item is what telemetry shows
   has happened or is happening; a future item is never inferred; an inferred item with no telemetry behind it
   is removed even when the previous context carries it.
7. A day pass that raises stops the whole brain run (`brain_wake.run_brain`: day_tasks and the stories after it
   did not run at 12:33 PM); it is retried at most hourly. OPEN: a design decision (whether the rest of the brain
   runs on the last good plan, the failure logged as an ERROR).
8. The item outlived fix 6 by changing its source. At 3:00 PM the steward's work reminded the owner "Please address
   your monthly timesheet before the 5:00 PM deadline today" (ticket ..._220008_c0b2f190, made from the false item);
   the owner answered "OK"; the tracker then kept "Monthly timesheets 3:00 PM - 5:00 PM", status completed, source
   that ticket, and a "Monthly timesheets" milestone ("Reported"), and wrote the day theme "[the owner] completed monthly
   timesheets and is working late, displacing scheduled family time". So (a) an acknowledgment was read as the event
   happening (Acknowledge is receipt only), and (b) a reminder made from a false item became that item's evidence.
   Removed from resource_expected_calendar(.json, _markdown.md) and the generator output (item, milestone; day theme
   cleared for the generator to rewrite) at the owner's request, 2026-10-05 ~7:20 PM. "Family time 6-8 PM" is
   recorded "cancelled": real (the owner cancelled it in a ticket reply, working late), kept. OPEN: (a) and (b);
   the design fix is the tracker's provenance checked by id in code (design note section 6, parked TODO).

### A friend's entity card says "Thursday evening Zoom"; the call has been on Mondays since 2025-11 — CONFIRMED 2026-10-05, PART FIXED (cards, critic), OPEN (KG supersession, data)
Seen in the daily context tracker's prompt (ENTITY CONTEXT, level 0): "[the friend] is [the owner]'s old friend from Berkeley and has
a regular Thursday evening Zoom call with him." Calendar, B331 and B521 say Monday. Card `entity_card_v2` for node
`958f0d98`, last built 2026-09-22 11:31 UTC, after the Monday State `52b7714e` existed; its level_0 and summary lead
with Thursday, while its notes carry the Monday start. Investigation (read-only; card text and window verdict
re-checked in the database; the rest from the investigator's reading of the code, not re-verified):
1. The owner's change was never ingested (MEM30, confirmed instance above).
2. The KG does not supersede a recurring arrangement: State `b7a9fc10` ("regular zoom call every Thursday at
   8pm", chat 2025-01-02) has no end date; `proposal_promoter.py` conflict detection covers five single-target
   predicates and habits are "deliberately NOT covered"; the Monday facts were minted as separate nodes (Events
   `2787ac68`, `817a8fcb`, `4ec6b3fd`, `72d57947`; States `30857837`, `52b7714e`) and never compared with it.
3. The card pipeline carries no time: the renderer and summary writer see no observation dates and no rule that a
   newer statement of the same recurring arrangement replaces an older one; level_0 is the summary's first sentence.
   The card's NOW filter drops every Event, so the four Monday Events never reach it.
4. Suspected: dropped card tags fall through to general_facts (`entity_cards_v2/builder.py` ~L1046-1063, against
   its docstring ~L984-989 and 12_ENTITY_CARDS.md), so rejected facts recirculate each build; Thursday reached
   general_facts as three near-duplicates.
Consequence: every agent with entity injection reads Thursday; belief recall (`belief_engine/intake/recall.py`
`expand_query`) appends level-0 summaries to its queries. Beliefs never feed the KG or cards.
Also found by the same investigation (suspected, unverified): `builder._is_user_self_entity` raises TypeError
(`ResourceManager.get_resource()` called with two arguments) on every build and returns False, logged at DEBUG only;
`entity_scan_keys` in agent configs has no production caller (entity detection scans the whole rendered prompt);
since 2026-06-27 "[the owner]" mentions bind to a Disambiguation marker (`2d67adda`, 381 edges) and the [the owner] entity
`2e83cbe1` gets no new edges.
Fixed 2026-10-05: (a) each card fact carries the first and last date its source messages were written
(`kg_node_evidence.message_timestamp`), shown to the renderer, distiller and summary writer, each told that the most
recently said fact about the same ongoing arrangement is current; `CARD_BUILDER_VERSION` v2 so every dropped fact is
reconsidered once. (b) A fact whose card tags were all dropped is skipped, not rerouted to general_facts (confirmed by
reading `_tags_from_persisted`). (c) `_is_user_self_entity` reads the canonical primary user name and raises when it
is missing (confirmed: `get_resource` is keyword-only). Three dry builds of the friend's card on the live KG: level 0 and
summary say Monday 3 of 3; a "Thursday at 8 p.m." bullet stays in current_connections 3 of 3, because the renderer
compares facts within one section and the Thursday State is still open in the KG (item 2, OPEN). (d) The window
critic's request rule: a request asserting a durable new or changed fact is claim-bearing for that fact; a relay of a
request passed to the team asserts nothing. The owner's move-to-Mondays window: kept 10 of 10 (old rule 7 of 10); the
relayed "delete the Thursday reminders" line 0 of 10; 60 sampled windows, 5 to 9 verdict differences per run, mostly
the critic's own run-to-run noise. Replays: scratch/replay/card_dry_build.py, critic_requests.py.

### Node.first_observed is the legacy import's date for imported claims — CONFIRMED 2026-10-05, OPEN (data)
`first_observed` is documented as "when the claim was first observed in chat — NOT the same as created_at", but for
nodes minted by the 2026-05 legacy chat import it is the import date: the friend's "Thursday" State reads 2026-05-12
though its only source message is 2025-01-02. 2,206 of 7,884 nodes with dated evidence have a first_observed more than
30 days after their earliest `kg_node_evidence.message_timestamp`. Consequence: every reader of first_observed (the
timeline, decay, cards before 2026-10-05) sees old claims as recent, so "newer wins" by first_observed picks the wrong
one. Cards now read the evidence dates. A backfill of first_observed from the earliest evidence date changes stored
data: awaits the owner.
Measured 2026-10-05 before the backfill (read-only, with step_state_decay's own rule): 2,860 nodes have a
first_observed more than a day after their earliest source message; correcting them would make decay close 163 open
States at once, all TTL class long_term, many of them durable facts ("[his wife] is [the owner]'s first true love", "Nightrain
is a Guns N' Roses song", his political leanings). Their long_term TTLs are wrong, consistent with the TTL estimator
being given the proposal's sentence instead of the node's own (see "State/Event matching cannot see a changed
arrangement", item 3); the import date hides them today. Backfill HELD: fix the TTL estimator's input, re-estimate the
TTLs, then backfill.

### A failed node_merger call minted a second owner Entity; a Disambiguation marker has captured every later mention of the owner since 2026-06-27 — CONFIRMED 2026-10-05, ROOT FIXED (needs a restart), OPEN (data, lock rules, marker lifecycle)
Actual: on 2026-06-27 at 02:30 PDT, during a network outage, `node_merger` failed three times ("Connection error")
while confirming the owner's label (`_needs_person_confirm` routes every mention of the owner through it).
`_call_node_merger_for_state_match` returned None on the exception, which means "create": a second owner Entity
(`fec5a709`) was minted, and `_mint_disambiguation_on_label_collision`, whose docstring assumes the merger "DECLINED",
minted Disambiguation `2d67adda` at the label. `_resolve_entity_like` binds a Disambiguation first, beating the exact
label, so every later mention of the owner parks on it: 381 edges (Jun 20, Jul 121, Aug 51, Sep 167, Oct 22) while the
real owner node `2e83cbe1` (user-locked since 2026-05-10) has had no new edge since 2026-06-26. The owner's wife (`ec7a408f`,
114 edges) and son (`7b9c838e`, 45) have the same markers; their twins were merged 2026-09-15, the markers remain.
Re-checked in the database and the agents log; the rest from an investigator's reading, not re-verified.
Why nothing drains them: no code retires a marker (the resolver's docstring expects "Disambiguation deleted"); the
temporal drain returns early when fewer than two same-label nodes exist (the wife and son markers) and skips undated and locked
eras (the owner); `kg_repoint_edge` and `kg_merge_nodes` refuse a user-locked endpoint, though the promoter itself
writes onto locked nodes; finding `8a598a15` sits at investigated/needs_user_review since 2026-08-25; verdict
`a2ebd0f4` (2026-10-05) records the two owner Entities as DISTINCT with the memo "same person, separate person
mentions", which now blocks their merge and is injected into node_merger calls.
Consequence: the owner's (and his wife's and son's) facts since June are missing from their wiki pages, cards and the
brain's household ties (`subconscious/kg_links.py` considers Entity nodes only); the friend's Monday State never met
the Thursday State in the merger (participant Jaccard 1/3 with the marker, see below).
ROOT FIXED 2026-10-05: every promoter model step fails loud (`_call_node_merger_for_state_match`, `_estimate_state_ttl`,
`_canonicalize_sentence`, `_semantic_entity_candidates`): a failure or malformed answer raises and the proposal stays
pending; only a real decline creates. A Property label collision mints no marker. Tests:
tests/kg/test_promoter_fails_loud.py. OPEN: the data repair (move parked edges and evidence to the real nodes, merge
the owner twin, supersede verdict `a2ebd0f4`, delete the markers), what a user lock forbids (adding facts to a node or
only changing existing ones), and a marker lifecycle (retire a marker when its label is back to one referent).

### State/Event matching cannot see a changed arrangement — CONFIRMED 2026-10-05, OPEN (design)
1. The merger's candidate filters keep supersessions away from it: the hub-weighted overlap filter (≥ 0.1, each shared
   participant weighs 1/degree) drops every owner-plus-person State whose other person has more than ten edges (the
   friend: degree 28, weight 0.036); `_get_participant_kg_ids` counts only entity-like neighbours, so a candidate's
   fingerprint omits a Disambiguation participant while a new proposal's includes it.
2. node_merger decides only merge or create; nothing closes an older State because a newer statement replaced it
   (`_is_durable_conflict` covers five single-target predicates; habits deliberately not).
3. The TTL estimator is given the proposal's representative sentence and group labels, not the node's own sentence
   (`_prepare_proposal_plan`): the Thursday State's TTL reasoning is about "an old friendship". Suspected on three nodes.
4. `step_state_decay` prose names its anchor "created_at" while the date used is first_observed.
5. Comment says the label similarity threshold is 0.7; `_LABEL_SIMILARITY_THRESHOLD` is 0.6.
A design for arrangement supersession (a candidate scan by shared participant and said date, a judge, and closing the
older State with `kg_close_state` + `succeeded_by` on acceptance) awaits the owner.


### The KG continued past failed calls: fallbacks, skip-and-continue, outages recorded as verdicts — CONFIRMED 2026-10-05, FIXED (needs a restart), OPEN (part)
Owner, 2026-10-05: "If any call fails in KG, we should treat that as a stop processing." A read-only survey found the
KG did the opposite in about forty places. Fixed the same day (commits e02a2efb, 93606bd2, 1db7892a, b2c4836f,
d97efb93, c2837018, 8a868a77, db2eaccd): a failed LLM, embedding, Chroma or DB call raises and the run stops, its
remaining work pending; a well-formed but incomplete answer gets one targeted correction, then raises; queue loops count
failures per item in the KG failure ledger (kg/failure_ledger.py), which sets an item aside after three failed runs
with a `processing_failure` finding born escalated. Among the defects fixed (each confirmed by reading the code):
- the window critic accepted every user line on a failure, so an outage extracted and promoted windows it never judged;
- a fact_extractor failure wrote a permanent verdict='error' row: 17 windows (2026-05, all "LLM quota exhausted") were
  never extracted (repair awaits the owner);
- the promoter's similarity filter degraded to label-only on an embedding/Chroma failure (twins minted), its run loop
  skipped failed proposals and continued;
- the duplicate executor merged by plain field copy when the merger LLM call failed;
- the investigator counted outages as failed attempts and escalated real findings on the third; an aborted executor run
  was recorded as "planner declined" (escalated) or "executed" after partial mutations; an aborted investigation's audit
  was saved as its report (ManagerInvoker returns `manager_aborted` instead of raising);
- the duplicate cluster drain turned nodes the agent left out into permanent "distinct" verdicts, and a missing merge
  winner dismissed its pairs as distinct;
- the importance rater wrote 5.0 for every edge it left out (never re-rated) and turned a real score of 0 into 5.0;
- section tagging wrote the `_processed` sentinel for nodes the tagger omitted;
- the duplicate scan stamped nodes scanned after a failed Chroma lookup;
- entity cards were built from an unfiltered pool when the prefilter failed, kept an uncapped section when the
  distiller failed, and took level_0 from the wiki description when the summary failed, each stamped fresh; a new card
  stayed active when its critic failed;
- identity sentences stamped a node "undefinable" after a failed read; the resolver stored raw text as resolved; the
  segmenter repeated a failed paid call every 5 seconds; the KG runner retried every sqlite3.OperationalError forever;
- the weekly maintenance pipeline continued after a failed step.
Escalated findings already written by the old outage path cannot be told apart from real declines (80 read "Executor
ran but applied no mutations — planner declined"); left for the owner's review as they are.
OPEN (found by the same work, not fixed):
1. app/routes/kg_maintenance.py `resolve_with_prose` (~381-404): when resolve_cluster_with_prose crashes or returns
   error/no_report (kg_resolution/resolve_with_prose.py ~184-196 ignores manager_aborted), the route still marks the
   lead and every sibling executed. FIXED 2026-10-05 (owner's go): the resolver raises on a missing lead, a failed or
   aborted manager run (investigator's invoke_manager) and a run with no report; the route answers 500 and closes
   nothing; a partial resolution closes nothing beyond what the manager resolved; only a completed one closes the lead
   and cascades. Tests: tests/kg/test_resolve_with_prose_closes_only_completed.py. Still open in that file: Other swallows in that route file: fill_dates revision-log insert and set_status
   fallback (~287, 302-307), the operator-notes save in apply (~763-766); `api_run` skips a step "execute_findings" that
   does not exist.
2. chroma_embedding_manager delete helpers (e.g. delete_edge_embedding) log and swallow.
3. The importance rater skips an edge whose endpoint node is missing (`_build_edge_block` returns None): it stays NULL
   and is re-selected every run. Suspected; a data-integrity skip, not a failed call.
4. Batch-level ledger counting: a failed rater/tagger call counts against every item in the batch, so three outage
   nights set aside a whole batch (up to 50 edges or 20 nodes), each with its own finding. Triage batches, goal outcome
   detection and edge-canon batches have no per-item ledger: an always-failing batch blocks its routine (the routine
   auto-disables with a ticket after three failures).
5. A partially applied executor run is released to `investigated` and re-run on a partly mutated graph; the drift guard
   checks only labels and dates. An investigator report with take_action=False and missing verdict fields still
   escalates instead of getting one correction (a correction re-runs the ~4.5-minute manager).
6. The duplicate cluster drain can still leave merged nodes with pending findings if it fails between the merge commit
   and closing the findings (set_status and record_distinct_pairs_bulk open their own sessions).
7. entity_cards_v2/builder.py comments still say the prefilter "mirrors description_creator._prefilter_edges" (deleted);
   `_judge_new_card` cuts each section to 400 characters for the critic's brief and its EVIDENCE block is placeholder
   text; a critic failure deletes the unjudged new card (a rebuild next run) because an inactive card cannot be told
   from a vetoed one without a critic_verdict column (a schema change, not made).
8. Window context passed to node_merger is cut to 600 characters (`_resolved_window_text` max_chars), against the
   no-truncation rule.

### Dayflow: an architect re-plan proposing an existing dependency loses the whole re-plan — OBSERVED 2026-10-05, FIXED 2026-10-05
2026-10-05 16:20, work_062b58b07e65 (a school sign-up made from the brain's list): the architect's re-plan included a
dependency that already existed; `work_objects/store.py` `_op_add_edge` raised "add_edge: self or duplicate
dependency", the whole batch was refused, work_architect_node logged ERROR and the tick continued without the re-plan.
Not yet diagnosed: whether apply_architect_dag should drop an existing edge deterministically (it de-duplicates nodes)
or give the architect a correction.
Diagnosis (2026-10-05): the architect prompt tells it to reuse existing node_ids in depends_on and to output "the DAG";
apply_architect_dag skipped a restated node (idempotent on node_id) but queued add_edge for every depends_on of every
spec, including edges the graph already had and one stated twice. Fix: an edge the graph has, or one already queued in
this plan, is skipped like a restated node; new edges on existing nodes are still added
(tests/dayflow/test_atomic_architect_revision.py::test_a_replan_restating_existing_dependencies_adds_only_the_new_ones).
Found while diagnosing, all FIXED 2026-10-05:
1. The architect's structured answer was not logged anywhere (its config logged system + user prompts, not
   results), so the exact re-plan that failed could not be read; only the error was. `prompt_debug.results: true`.
2. SUSPECTED, not observed: a restated existing node with a wake field queued defer_node, which moves a `dispatched`
   node to `waiting`, and an in-flight ask restated with a wake_ref lost its user_reply wake — the architect parking
   in-motion work, against its prompt's "in-motion nodes are the runtime's". apply_architect_dag no longer applies a
   wake to a dispatched node (WARNING when it differs).
3. Any refused revision (a cycle, an invalid dependency, a refused prune, an invalid duplicate pair) lost the whole
   create or re-plan with an ERROR the architect never saw. It now gets one correction round with the refusal
   (shared/work/architect_refused.j2, work_architect_node._apply_with_correction); a second refusal raises as before.

### Brain gate prompt still names the noticer as the reader — OBSERVED 2026-10-05, OPEN (suspected stale text)
`agents/subconscious/gate/prompts/system.j2` says "You route; the noticer judges. It reads every event you pass on" and
"costs the noticer one line". Since 2026-09-30 the brain step (`subconscious/brain_step.py`) reads what the gate passes
on; the noticer no longer does. The routing rule itself is right; only the name is stale. Found while adding camera
events to the gate. `tests/non_agent_tests/test_brain_gate.py` pins the sentence ("You route; the noticer judges."),
so a fix changes the test too.

### 05a reference says dayflow short_ids wrap past 10,000; a test pins that they do not — OBSERVED 2026-10-05, OPEN (doc)
`docs/architecture/05a_DAYFLOW_ORCHESTRATOR_REFERENCE.md` (run_dayflow_ingestion) says "`short_id`s continue from the
highest existing one and wrap to 1 past 10,000". `ingestion.run_dayflow_ingestion` continues from max+1 with no wrap,

### Chat-attached images are never described: the captioner thread is never started — CONFIRMED 2026-10-05, FIXED 2026-10-05
`app/routes/process_request.py` mints the image pod, then starts `image_pod_enrichment.enrich_image_pod` with
`start_monitored_thread(owner=..., name=..., target=..., kwargs=...)`. `runtime_registry.start_monitored_thread`
requires keyword-only `daemon`, so the call raises TypeError; the surrounding `except` logs a WARNING and the chat turn
goes on. Log, 2026-09-24 22:47-22:48: "failed to spawn image-pod enrichment: start_monitored_thread() missing 1 required
keyword-only argument: 'daemon'". Consequence: all 25 chat-attachment image pods (2026-04-29..2026-09-25) are still
`vision_extraction_status: pending` with an empty body and a one_liner "[image: png, 58kb, 500x248]"; every reader
(brain, chat history, pod_search) sees an id with no content. The captioner itself works: run by hand on
datapod:image:770c2245… it described the screenshot correctly. Also pending without a describer: 3 agent_camera_snapshot
and 3 manual_mint pods. `enrich_image_pod` itself returns error dicts instead of raising, cuts chat lines to 240
characters and the one_liner to 160, and reads at most 6 messages from 10 minutes.
Fix (2026-10-05): ingest_image_file takes the source's description (the camera passes its analyzer's) and queues
every other image for the captioner as it is minted (`image_pod_enrichment.queue_description`, two-worker monitored
pool); the chat route records the message and room on the pod and starts nothing itself. describe_image_pod raises
on a missing pod or file or an empty caption; it reads the room's conversation in the 30 minutes up to the image,
uncut. The brain sees a chat image as its description (`with_images`). The 31 pending pods were described the same
day. Tests: tests/non_agent_tests/test_image_pod_description.py.

### Brain → dayflow contract: external review findings, verified against the code — 2026-10-05, 1-4, 6, 7 FIXED 2026-10-05; 5, 8, 9 OPEN
An external read-only review of the brain/dayflow cutover (2026-10-05) raised these; each was checked against the code
the same day. "Confirmed" means the code path does what is described; none was observed causing harm yet.
1. CONFIRMED. Resolving a concern does not end its list entries or their work. persist.apply_brain_matter moves the
   concern to `resolved`; concern_planner.stale reads only active/addressing, so nothing cancels its entries, and
   brain_list_intake ends work only for a cancelled or not-needed entry. The brain can consider a matter settled
   while dayflow still holds an active goal for it.
2. CONFIRMED. A re-plan does not see what the work was made from. The shell's brief (entry, concern or belief in full,
   sources word for word) is `constraints.rationale`, read by the architect only at create; shells carry
   `source_intake: []`; work_context.work_data (the re-plan's graph view) has no rationale. A concern-lane re-plan
   sees the concern briefs (concern_context); a calendar-lane re-plan sees no belief at all, only the objective.
3. CONFIRMED. brain_list_intake._decide revises work only when `objective(entry)` (what + when) changed; a change of
   the assistant's part (or why) alone is recorded delivered with action none.
4. CONFIRMED. A brain revision's re-plan request is not durable: revise_goal is committed and the delivery recorded,
   but the re-plan trigger is only `licensed` on the tick's blackboard (replan_work_ids). An architect failure, a
   restart, or the per-tick cap (_MAX_REPLANS_PER_TICK = 3) leaves a new objective over the old graph with nothing
   pending. (The end instruction is durable: constraints.architect_instructions.)
5. CONFIRMED. One list, not one decision context: day_tasks sees every open concern's entries (with_concerns); a
   concern's decider sees only that concern's entries and may modify only those (concern_planner.write_plan/problems).
6. CONFIRMED. Decision recorded before its list changes, in separate commits: day_tasks writes brain_day_decisions
   (the fingerprint) and then brain_list.apply_calendar; concern_planner stores the plan (set_concern_plan) and then
   brain_list.apply_concern. A failure between them leaves the next pass treating the decision as current.
7. CONFIRMED. day_tasks still runs as shadow: situation.gather(calendar_work=False) leaves calendar-made work out, the
   module docstring says SHADOW ONLY, its trace step says "decided (shadow)" — while the calendar lane is live from
   2026-10-06.
8. PLAUSIBLE, not yet traced: a changed placement is not a labelled fact a modification can cite (new_since accepts
   M/W/R/C/E/B labels), so a moved placement may not be able to justify changing its entry.
9. BY DESIGN, needs a recovery path: a failed brain matter is not retried (its events stay in the inbox); a failed
   concern plan waits for the concern to change.
Fixes (2026-10-05, one commit):
- 1: brain_list.cancel_for_closed_concerns runs on every brain pass after the brain step: an open entry whose concern
  is resolved, dormant or gone is cancelled with the concern's resolution, and the intake ends its work like any
  cancelled entry's. Covers the brain step and the noticer (still enabled). First pass cancels 4 live entries, none
  with active work.
- 2: every re-plan shows the architect what the goal was made from (constraints.rationale, architect_task.j2).
- 3: _decide revises active work on any delivered change (the list records a change only when the entry changed).
- 4: a revision commits revise_goal and an `instruct_goal` kind `replan` in one batch; the architect is woken by it
  (constraints.architect_instructions), licensed as the brain's directive, and consumes it in its own revision; the
  blackboard hand-off is gone.
- 6: day_tasks applies the list changes before recording the run; set_concern_plan applies them (before_store) under
  the lock before saving the plan, and a failure there stores no plan and is planned again next pass.
- 7: on a live calendar day the situation includes calendar-made work; shadow wording kept only for steward days.

### Calendar intake: a belief dropping out of the day plan stops every planning tick — FOUND 2026-10-05, FIXED 2026-10-05
Found preparing the calendar lane's first day (2026-10-06). brain_list_intake.brief read a calendar entry's belief from
the CURRENT day plan and raised KeyError when it was not placed; the day pass re-places the day as its inputs change
and a belief drops out and returns (the clock-in reminder was in 2 of 9 runs on 2026-10-05); and the intake node raises
every failure, so one undeliverable entry would have failed every planning tick until the belief returned. Fixed:
the brief reads the placement as the run that decided the entry saw it (day_tasks.decided_placement, from
brain_day_decisions; all 26 of 2026-10-05's entries resolve); and one entry's failure is logged ERROR and left
undelivered for the next tick while the other entries go on (reading the list still raises).

### concern_tasks never saw the concern's entries: {{ information }} undeclared — CONFIRMED 2026-10-05, FIXED 2026-10-05
subconscious::concern_tasks/prompts/user.j2 renders `{{ information or "(nothing yet)" }}` (the concern's current
entries on the brain's list, passed by concern_planner._invoke as the Message's information), but its config.yaml did
not declare `information`, so the prompt builder left it empty. The boot validator logged it ("Template will render
with an empty/Undefined value") and nobody read the warning. Consequence: the decider was told the concern has no
entries and could not modify or cancel one by id (7 concern entries exist; 1 was modified, probably from an id in the
planner's messages). Fixed by declaring it (config loads at boot: restart). New test
tests/agent_tests/test_prompt_variables_declared.py fails on any prompt variable no config declares.

### The day's tasks called the model every brain pass to decide nothing — FOUND 2026-10-05, FIXED 2026-10-05
Live log after the evening restart: day_tasks ran at 18:37, 18:48, 18:58, 19:02, "17 placement(s) offered, 0 decided"
each time. Its fingerprint is meant to change only when what it reads changes; two inputs changed on their own.
(1) situation.calendar_ahead is brain_step._calendar(now), whose text opens "Found N calendar events from <now> to
<now + 30 days>", a new string every minute; (2) situation.health is the health inference's prose, reworded on every
inference run. Fixed: the calendar window starts at the day's boundary, and health is left out of the fingerprint
(owner: no field list of the inference's output; a change in health alone waits for the next other change). Captured live twice, 10 minutes
apart, before and after: before, calendar_ahead and health differed; after, only real changes (a placement passed, a
chat message, a reminder sent).

### At midnight the expected calendar holds the next day's events under the current day's date — OBSERVED 2026-10-06 00:43, FIXED 2026-10-07
resource_expected_calendar.json at 2026-10-06 12:41 AM local: `date: 2026-10-05`, source daily_context, and only two
entries, "Take out the dogs 8:00-8:30 AM" and "Work Hours 9:00 AM-4:30 PM", both "upcoming", both google_calendar —
the 6th's events. The day boundary is 05:00 (configs/dayflow_pipeline.json), so the 5th's record (its history: walks,
standup, lunch, dinner, family time) was replaced by the next calendar day's events under the 5th's date. Suspected:
the tracker's rebuild reads the Google calendar for the local calendar date while the record is keyed by the boundary
date. Consequence seen at once: the steward read "today's schedule" and at 00:43 created work_72304b263984 (8:00 AM
dog-outing heads-up) and work_f50144b083bf (9:00 AM work-hours heads-up) for the 6th, with no source intake.
Not diagnosed: daily_context_generator_stage's day-boundary rebuild path.
Addendum 2026-10-07 (the day's tasks, steward retired): situation.calendar_today reads resource_expected_calendar, so
between midnight and 05:00 the day's tasks decides the boundary day (the 6th) from the next calendar day's schedule;
situation.gather's weekday (now_local) also says the next day. At 01:01 it made four own entries under day 2026-10-06
for the 7th's events (8:00 dog outing, 8:30 kids' late start, 9:00 work hours, 9:15 standup); the calendar lane made work
of them (work_3e7043c7eeac abandoned by the architect at creation, the 6th's 8:00 being past; work_f7155fb89851,
work_f3e3e7db087f, work_49d2ac5c9cd5 planned for the 7th). 01:10 it added day:2026-10-06:27bc4647 "dog outing at 8:00 AM
on Wednesday, October 7" ("the existing entry concerns the prior day") -> work_e5c3d50cc65a, and at 02:46 cancelled
the first. The 7th's day's tasks (05:04) sees only the 7th's entries, so these heads-ups run once, from entries filed
under the wrong day. Same root as the B488 addendum below: the expected calendar (and anything keyed by the calendar
date) is not the boundary day between midnight and 05:00. OPEN.
Diagnosed and FIXED 2026-10-07. The Oct 6 snapshot at 04:52 (resource_expected_calendar.json, date 2026-10-06,
source daily_context) held only the 7th's four events, stamped `2026-10-06T15:00Z` (the 6th at 8 AM). Three causes:
(1) daily_context_generator_stage gave the tracker the next 12 hours of events (context_sources.
get_calendar_events_upcoming_for_daily_context), shown without a date when on the current calendar date, and the
calendar date's weekday, so at 1 AM the next morning read as today; (2) expected_calendar_utils._parse_local_clock put
every clock time on the boundary date, so the 7th's "08:00 AM" became the 6th's; (3) get_calendar_events_structured_
for_day picked events by calendar date, not the day's window. Fixed: the tracker's upcoming events stop at the day's end
(day_window_local) and its day_of_week is the boundary day's; a clock time before the boundary hour is on the next
calendar date; the structured calendar is the events overlapping the day's window. Tests:
non_agent_tests/test_day_boundary_calendar.py (fail on the old code). Not changed: situation.gather's weekday is now's
(shown beside "Now"), and work titled by the calendar date (the B488 addendum) stays OPEN.

### The steward turns calendar events into heads-ups, a path no brain lane covers — OBSERVED 2026-10-06 00:43, OPEN (design)
The two goals above cite "today's schedule" (calendar events in resource_expected_calendar) and the owner's standing
preference to be notified of schedule context changes; neither cites a belief or a list entry. The calendar lane moves
BELIEF placements to the brain (day_tasks decides placements of the day plan); calendar events themselves have no
decider on the brain's side. So (a) after the calendar lane goes live the steward and the brain can both act on the
same moment (a calendar event and a belief placed at its time, e.g. the 8 AM dog walk; a scheduler reminder sent
"take the dogs out" on 2026-10-05 as well), and (b) retiring the steward (brain_lanes steward.mode) would end these
heads-ups with nothing in their place. Needs the owner's decision on who decides calendar-event heads-ups.
Addendum 01:04: the steward also made belief work for the 6th — work_94dda59bb4ce (AC COOL 75°F at 6:00 AM, B156) and
work_39bd6cf0b6f4 (household lights off at 10:00 AM, B211/B478), no source intake. Their source is the day plan the
steward reads in its own prompt (rendered "### 6:00 AM — ... [belief:B156]"): at 01:00 the 5th's plan's morning
placements read as upcoming. A second route around the calendar lane: the lane stops placements reaching the steward
as intake (belief_occurrences), not the steward acting on the plan in its context. After 05:00 the brain places and
decides the same beliefs for the 6th; the architect's portfolio rule may end the BRAIN's copies as duplicates of the
steward's. Left running overnight (harm: a double AC set or lights-off); owner to decide on waking.

### node_merger copies a candidate's UUID and garbles it — OBSERVED 2026-10-06 02:31, FIXED 2026-10-08
proposal_promoter run 5e50962c: "node_merger chose to merge '[the son]' into 'd62686dd-3881-4da9-8dff-83a54db80c7d',
which is not a candidate". The graph's only node of that label is d62686dd-3881-4da9-8dff-83b28ba80c7d: same prefix,
garbled tail. The merger chose the right node and mis-copied its id; the promoter's candidate check refused it, the
run stopped (KG fail-stop), the failure ledger counted 1 of 3. Consequence: the proposal fails on each retry with the
same risk and is set aside after 3 runs; no wrong merge happened. Fix direction (not done): request-local candidate
labels mapped back by code (the gate's E1/C1 pattern), so the model never copies a UUID.
Diagnosed 2026-10-08: not a random slip but a splice. Each candidate carried `node_id` and, beside it,
`source_window_id`; for the son's node (d62686dd-…-83b28ba80c7d) the merger answered his id with his source window's
tail (window 6efed387-…-83a54db94a2a): 2026-10-06 `…83a54db80c7d`, 2026-10-08 `…83a54db94a2a` four times (two
proposals, the same splice each time). Before c29ca9ca (2026-10-05) an invalid id was a logged warning and the proposal
went on as "no match" (once in four months of logs, 2026-06-28); fail-loud made it stop the run. On 2026-10-08
02:30-02:42 proposal 2737503f failed 3 runs and was set aside, then proposal 8da5dfaa failed: 4 consecutive routine
failures auto-disabled proposal_promoter (on_error.max_failures 4 outlasts one bad item, not two). Fixed: the merger
sees candidates as N1, N2... with no node id, no source window id (code never read them; the new node's context
dropped it too) and answers `merged_candidate`; code maps the label back; prior-distinct notes name the other
candidate by label (they cited `id[:8]`). Also removed on this path: the candidate description cut `[:500]`, and the
600/800-char caps on window text (`_resolved_window_text`, `_node_window_text`, `_proposal_window_text`, the
identity-sentence caller). Test: tests/kg/test_promoter_fails_loud.py. The three set-aside proposals (2737503f,
8da5dfaa, and 61fed917 of 2026-10-06) stay skipped unless their ledger rows are cleared.
KG data check 2026-10-08: no stored reference anywhere matches a live node/edge id at its start or end except one —
`c801fadf-fc3a-4b5d4fb0f9e1` (live c801fadf-fc3a-4fbc-9702-4b5d4fb0f9e1 with its middle dropped), written by
kg_maintenance::duplicate_detector into finding 2353f34a (2026-05-26, dismissed) and copied by
duplicate_cluster_resolver into verdict 7ac2b174 (2026-06-10, "distinct", a no-op against a node that does not
exist): the same id-copying weakness in the maintenance agents. Not cleaned up (a data change).

### One bad proposal disabled the promoter: the ledger and the routine both count to 3 — 2026-10-06, FIXED 2026-10-06
02:31-02:36: proposal 61fed917 (the node_merger UUID garble above) failed three runs in a row; the failure ledger set
it aside on the third (finding da3a4206), but that run still failed, and the routine manager disables a routine after
on_error.max_failures (default 3) consecutive failed runs: proposal_promoter was disabled the run before the one that
would have skipped the item. The KG fail-stop entry had flagged the risk (open item 4). Fixed: the promoter re-enabled
through /api/routines/<id>/toggle (clears the auto-disable and the failure count); every routine whose code uses the
ledger (13: derived from the handlers' imports and the pipelines' packages) has on_error.max_failures 4, above
SET_ASIDE_AFTER = 3; tests/kg/test_ledger_routines_outlast_the_ledger.py derives the set from the code and fails when a
ledger routine's limit is not above the ledger's. Still open: retries 60 s apart let a short outage spend all three
of an item's runs within minutes (the ledger's "outage sets items aside" item).

### An outage counted as the item's and the routine's failure — 2026-10-06, FIXED 2026-10-06
Owner: "most likely reason for failure is credit outage, second most likely is api outage ... if its the first its not
very likely to be resolved before the morning. the second one could be minutes to hours." A credit outage arms the
quota breaker (every LLM call raises QuotaExhaustedError without touching the network, one trial call per 15 min); an
API outage raises connection, timeout or 5xx errors after the client's retries. Either way every KG item failed, the
failure ledger counted it against the item, and at a minute's routine retry every item of a queue was set aside within
three minutes while its routine auto-disabled. Fixed: llm_resilience.is_outage (by type and status along the cause
chain, never message digits: work errors carry UUIDs); the ledger logs an outage and does not count it; the routine
manager keeps consecutive_outages beside consecutive_failures, an outage sets the backoff (60 s doubling to 1 h) and never
counts toward auto-disable; the KG pipeline's halt says whether it was an outage. A genuinely bad item still counts.
Tests: tests/kg/test_outage_is_not_the_items_fault.py.

### The KG pipeline's halt was never cleared: every run after one reported "halted" — FOUND 2026-10-06, FIXED 2026-10-06
KGPipeline builds its KGPipelineRunner once; the pipeline is registered once per process; run() cleared the stop flag
and the stats but not _halt_reason/_halt_step. After one halt every later kg_pipeline run returned status "halted"
(contract "error") until a restart, toward the routine's auto-disable. Found while adding the halt's outage flag. Fixed:
run() resets the halt; pinned in the outage test file.

### The concern decider re-created a question it had cancelled, about an expired, trivial reminder — 2026-10-06, FIXED
2026-10-05 21:13 the concern decider cancelled con:48a45f7750 ("ask whether the October 5 7:50 AM dog-walk reminder
was received"; "the morning has passed ... does not merit another ask"). 2026-10-06 08:15 it created con:e53f33688a,
the same question ("the appropriate one-time follow-up"), and dayflow planned it for 16:35. The decider is shown only
open entries (brain_list.entries excludes cancelled), so it could not see its own decision. The concern itself
("Confirm [the owner] received the October 5 morning dog-walk reminder") existed only to confirm a past reminder. Owner:
"Decider should understand what things are trivial and if a reminder is missed it certainly should not be carried
beyond hours for which is was scheduled and certainly not to next day." Fixed: shared/brain/time_bound.j2 (a time-bound
reminder or check ends with its hours; a trivial matter is let go) in the brain, the concern decider and the day's
tasks; the concern decider sees its cancelled entries with why, as a record it cannot change; the concern resolved at
the owner's direction (its entry and work end through the closed-concern sweep). Test:
test_concern_planner::test_the_decider_sees_what_it_cancelled_and_why.
Addendum 2026-10-06 15:06 (same root): the brain decided B488 (lights off once the owner has gone to bed) not_needed
for the 6th: "W25 already turned off all household lights on October 6 after sustained post-bedtime inactivity". W25
(work_1526553ed39d) ran at 02:11, the night of the 5th by the 05:00 boundary, but was titled "...on October 6, 2026"
by the calendar date. Consequence: the 6th's bedtime lights-off is skipped unless something new changes the entry.
Work and items made between midnight and 05:00 carry the calendar date, not the boundary day. OPEN.

### The concern planner asks Gmail for the thread of a chat message — OBSERVED 2026-10-06 16:36, OPEN
concern_planner_manager (subconscious::concern_planner) called get_email_thread(seed_message_id=
'd6e3899a-51aa-4465-a4b4-ab6e38fdfd24'): a master_room chat message id (the concern's evidence ref
message:d6e3899a-...), not a Gmail id; Gmail answered HTTP 400 and the tool returned the error to the planner, which
went on. Harmless (one wasted call, an ERROR line), but the planner cannot tell a chat ref from an email ref. Fix
direction (not done): the email tools refuse a non-Gmail id with a plain error before calling the API, and the

### Ended daily and weekly reminders fire forever: the wall-clock cron drops end_date — CONFIRMED 2026-10-07, FIXED 2026-10-07
timing_engine.schedule_event arms a day- or week-aligned interval event as a ready-made CronTrigger
(`_wall_clock_cron`, added 2026-09-12 for DST) and passes `end_date=` and `jitter=` beside it to add_job.
APScheduler 3.11.1 `BaseScheduler._create_trigger` returns a trigger instance as-is and ignores the other trigger
arguments, and the CronTrigger is built without start_date or end_date. Intended: the event stops at its end_date
and does not fire before its start_date. Evidence (time_events in emi.db; scheduler_reminder rows in
unified_log_2026, since 2026-09-01): South Lake registration (end 2025-08-13) 27 sends, last 2026-10-07 08:00;
Woodbridge data confirmation (end 2025-08-11) 24, last 2026-10-06; omeprazole (end 2025-08-21) 25, last 2026-10-07;
"Read probable probability for martin" (end 2026-01-05) 16, last 2026-10-06. The oldest South Lake send kept is
2026-09-13, the day after the change. Consequence: the owner gets four expired reminders a day; the day's tasks read
the South Lake one in the situation's reminder history and on 2026-10-07 08:09 made an own entry
(day:2026-10-07:5923a561, a ToDo for the year-old registration) -> work_1dfaa12a3350. A daily or weekly event with a
future start_date would also fire before it. Sub-daily and other intervals keep the interval trigger and are not
affected (Irvine monitor, wind-down monitor: 0 sends). Fix direction: build the CronTrigger with the event's
start_date, end_date and jitter.
Fixed: `_wall_clock_cron` builds the CronTrigger with the event's start_date, end_date and jitter, and add_job no
longer passes them beside it. On a real BackgroundScheduler an ended event is added with no next run time (parked);
a live one keeps its next run. Tests: test_scheduler_dst_drift.py (three new, fail on the old code). Takes effect on
restart; the ended events' rows stay in time_events (deleting them is a data change, not done).

### Yesterday's doctor appointment appeared on today's computed calendar — CONFIRMED 2026-10-08, PARTLY FIXED 2026-10-08
resource_expected_calendar (2026-10-08): "[the daughter] doctor appointment 10:30 AM", source
`concern:[the daughter] is sick and needs medical assessment after missing school`, start_utc 2026-10-08T17:30Z; the appointment
was 2026-10-07. Traced (agents log, daily_context_tracker runs): first in the 08:50 run's output; its input's
current_daily_context.active_concerns_this_week carried the concern's `notes` verbatim, "She has a doctor appointment at
10:30 today." (written 2026-10-07, undated); the 08:44 run's input did not. Causes: (1) the concern's `notes` hold a
relative date ("today") with no date, stale the next day (its brief is dated correctly); (2)
pipelines/dayflow/utils/subconscious_projection._project_concern hands the tracker the raw `notes` (also cut at
_NOTES_MAX_CHARS, against the no-cut rule), not the dated brief, and projects only the `active` bucket, so a concern
flips in and out of the tracker's view as attached work starts and ends (this one returned to active ~08:47); (3) the
tracker may add schedule items from concerns as bare clock times, which normalize_expected_schedule_to_utc dates to
the boundary day. Fix options (owner to decide): project the brief whole instead of notes; a brain rule that stored
notes carry dates, never today/tomorrow; the tracker adds a concern item only when the concern dates it to today.
Owner, 2026-10-08: "all messages that are carried need to carry timestamps (local for the models, utc for db)"; "we
could just have the notes recorded in order of timestamp ... then it has clear history and can probably figure out what
happened". The brain had the outcome on record at 2026-10-07 11:31 local (event 534, matter 227: "attended"), but could
only append to the journal (actions note/resolve; nothing revises the raising note), and readers saw the raising note
undated. Fixed: persist.history_for_prompt — the raising note stamped with first_observed, then the journal, oldest
first, local times, whole — read by the brain and the brief writer (shared/brain/concern.j2), the gate, the concern door,
the concern planner, and the daily-context projection (which feeds the tracker, the chat gate and the retired steward's
prompt; its 200-character notes cut is gone). Test: test_journal_local_time.py. (a) FIXED 2026-10-08 (owner: "Do we lose context if we don't keep all notes? i think we do. so we have to keep
em."): the journal cap, the evidence cap and the chronic-dormancy evidence cut are removed; entries deleted before
are not recoverable. Was: the journal is capped
at _JOURNAL_KEEP = 10 and older entries are DELETED ("(2 earlier notes archived)" archives nothing): for this concern
the deleted entries include the 2026-10-07 11:31 "attended" note, the very proof of the outcome; evidence is likewise
kept to the newest 4; (b) "USER RESPONSE" journal entries carry raw JSON with UTC responded_at into prompts;
(c) schedule lines in the day's tasks' situation still show no source or recorded time.

### UI mute is advertised to the architect but no worker dayflow reaches can do it — CONFIRMED 2026-10-08, FIXED 2026-10-08
Fixed: set_ui_mute is in devices_manager's tools and scope contract, and its descriptions (manager config, tool
contract planner_description) name it, so the work team routes a mute to the devices team. Architect prompt: end_goal
allowed on a new goal (the code already applied it; the form said RE-PLAN ONLY); a goal about the assistant's own conduct
is honoured where it applies and otherwise ended; wake_ref covers any non-clock trigger; notification-release v1.1
(the architect writes when it is meant to go out, the state mover releases it).
The architect's capability text (standing directives) says "device_control: ... Nest thermostat, lights, Ring cameras,
and UI mute/unmute". The tool exists (lib/tools/set_ui_mute) and is listed in the dayflow managers' tool scope
(dayflow_dispatch/wake/orchestrator configs), but the switchboard may call only create_dayflow_ticket and
work_emi_team_manager; the work team sent "Mute UI during focus" (work_8868d00a7f6c, 2026-10-08 ~09:47) to
work_personal_admin_manager, whose finalizer recorded "no available tool can change or verify the UI notification
setting", and the devices team's toolset is fixed to nest / lights / ring. Consequence: a legitimate mute is planned and
always fails; workers improvised a reminder instead. Fix options (owner): give set_ui_mute to the devices team (matches
the device_control wording), or let the switchboard call it directly. Found by a context-free read of the architect
prompt (2026-10-08), which also flagged: "full control" vs "the state mover decides" in notification-release; wake_ref
defined as outside-only vs internal triggers; end_goal "RE-PLAN ONLY" vs "a new goal is planned or ended"; standing
directives written for other roles reaching the architect (escalation policy, cron reminders, "routine doc", a
worker's lookup procedure; two sections duplicated); a ~96k-character user prompt (own work object printed twice,
RECENTLY COMPLETED undated with titles cut mid-word, current time only at the end).
Input fixes 2026-10-08: titles were cut at write time (work_persist and brain_list_intake stored objective[:80]); both
now store the whole objective, and recently completed/abandoned show the goal's full content with its local end time; a
re-plan's portfolio shows the goal's own work object once (a pointer line), read fresh from the store. The current
time stays at the tail of the architect's user prompt by design (approved cache-shape fix 68719a16; pinned by
test_cache_shape_templates.py).
Directives split 2026-10-08 (owner approved; "more context is always better, but wrong context is worse"):
resource_orchestrator_user_prefs (+ its personal overlay) is split into resource_assistant_capabilities (architect, day's
tasks, concern planner), resource_owner_standing_directives (day's tasks, concern planner), resource_work_shaping and
resource_worth_doing (architect only: a worker handed a task does not re-ask whether it is worth doing),
resource_task_approach (work_emi_team and work_personal_admin planners, which read none of it before), and
resource_owner_escalation (read by no agent yet: owner to decide). Dropped: the two "routine doc nudge" lines (the
routine doc is now the brain's day plan; the day's tasks decide what becomes work) and the action selector's copy
(nothing in it applied to picking the next step). The old combined file stays for the retired steward's prompts.
UI follow-up 2026-10-08: the three edit surfaces still pointed at the old file (the /personalize/directives editor,
the /skills/dayflow editor, and the resource editor's resource_orchestrator_user_prefs entry), so the owner could only
edit what the retired steward reads. Fixed locally: the directives editor works over the six pieces (allowlisted piece
id; it writes only that piece's `_personal.md` overlay), /skills/dayflow redirects there, and the resource editor lists
the six shared templates. CONFIRMED and FIXED 2026-10-08: `situation_snapshot._build_user_directives` (the hourly situation auditor's
snapshot) read only the old base file `resource_orchestrator_user_prefs.md` (no personal overlay, so the owner's own
lines never reached the auditor) and swallowed any read error with `except Exception: pass`; it now reads
resource_owner_standing_directives through the resource manager (overlay applied), and a failure raises. `bootstrap` still seeds the old file from
its `.example`; harmless while the steward reads it.

### The brain cited an unrelated concern to satisfy "used", attaching unrelated work — CONFIRMED 2026-10-08, FIXED 2026-10-08
Event 784 (work_ae704d73aed1 ended: Nest OAuth no longer needed, Nest set COOL 70°F) was routed by the gate as a
new_matter "not matching any listed open concern". Matter 263: the brain decided it `used` (it was informative), and
since a used event must be cited by a concern update, cited it on the only open concern, "[the owner]'s pickup of [the
son] from BAS tonight", with the note "it does not establish that [the owner] picked [the son] up"; code attached the
cited work to that concern (attach on cite). The same pressure showed in two refused answers that morning ("E1 is
decided used but no concern update cites it"), whose correction text offered only citing. Fixed: the third decision is
`needs_nothing` (changes no open concern and raises nothing new; the reason records what it established), the prompt
says to cite an event only on a concern it bears on, never to say it does not apply, and the correction offers "or
decide needs_nothing if it changes none". The 35 stored brain_matters labelled not_worth_tracking were relabelled
(brain_calls/brain_steps keep the model's original words). The wrong attachment, its evidence item and its two journal
lines were removed from concern faabcc6c with a dated CORRECTION line (owner's direction). Same day: a concern about a
moment now closes once the moment has passed (shared/brain/time_bound.j2, 12b9784b).
The rule alone did not close the pickup concern: the brain reads a concern only when an event bears on it, and the
10-minute heartbeat runs the gate and brain step on pending events only. Fixed: brain_inbox.add_day_ended_events, on
every brain pass, hands each open concern with horizon `today` whose day (by first_observed and the day boundary) has
ended to the brain as a `time` event routed to it by code, once per concern per day; the brain resolves it under the
rule or keeps it with a note on what lies ahead. OPEN (owner, 2026-10-08): with notes and evidence now kept whole, very
long concerns will need a compaction step (an agent that summarizes, building on the brief; never a cut).

### The day's tasks loops on an entry whose work ended: refused every pass, tonight's reminder never made — CONFIRMED 2026-10-08, PARTLY FIXED 2026-10-08
Chain (cal:2026-10-08:B571, phone charged before the evening walk): entry created 05:02 local, work_4b090fe060db made
05:12; the architect abandoned it at 05:13 as "already achieved" by work_f6deef0d2431, which was the 10-07 reminder
(calendar-made objectives carry no date: `brain_list_intake.objective` is `what (when: 9:00 PM)`, so two days' identical
reminders read as one). At 06:12 the day's tasks re-decided the entry needed, rightly ("W29 records that reminder for
Wednesday's walk, not tonight's"); intake recorded `work_ended_not_reopened` (`brain_list_intake._decide`:157-160, by
design: a modified entry whose work ended is never reopened). The entry now says needed and no work exists: no reminder
tonight. From then on every run tried to set it not_needed citing the abandonment (W37), which predates the 06:12 entry,
and `problems` (day_tasks.py:275-283) refused it, twice, so the run raised: 40 refusals and 11 failed brain passes on
10-08. A failed run records no fingerprint (day_tasks.py:398), so the next pass runs again. Consequences: a reminder
lost; each failure discards the whole pass's day planning; one model call wasted per pass. Fix needs: dated objectives
for day-bound work; a path from "needed, work ended by mistake" back to work; and the validator not to wedge on an entry
whose own work changed state.
FIXED 2026-10-08 (owner: "you have a gate keeper not letting a decision through"): the newer-fact gate is removed
(day_tasks.problems / own_problems, the `new_since` field and its prompt rule); the decider reads each listed entry
with its reason ("Why: ...") and its work's ending, and its decision goes through. Intake makes new work for an entry
still decided needed after its work ended (`_decide` returns create; `work_ended_not_reopened` retired). OPEN: dated
objectives (the date sweep, H1-H5).
