annotation, the Google event was live until the work deleted it; the tracker also invented "Drive [daughter] to BAS".

### Day-bound text carried without its date — SWEPT 2026-10-08, PARTLY FIXED 2026-10-08
Owner's rule: "all messages that are carried need to carry timestamps (local for the models, utc for db)". A read-only
sweep found where time text travels undated or relative. FIXED 2026-10-08 (owner's go on 1-4):
(H1) the work objective (`brain_list_intake.objective`: title, goal, revise) now names a calendar entry's day and
when every entry was decided: "... (for Thu 10-08; when: 9:00 PM; decided Thu 10-08 06:12 AM)"; it was
"(when: 9:00 PM)" and the architect ended tonight's reminder as achieved by yesterday's. (H2) the architect's brief
(shared/work/brain_entry_brief.j2) dates the when and names the calendar's day instead of "today's calendar".
(H3) every schedule header shows the schedule's date (architect, state_mover, action selector, intake triage, cron
tickets, chat gate, entertainment, health, the brain's situation, the retired steward), and every time
context_sources hands a model is dated ("Thu 10-08 12:07 AM": presence since, last away, activity segments, completed
and upcoming events, ticket responses and snoozes); at 05:13 the tracker read last night's undated activity as the
day lived and marked 8 AM-5 PM "completed". (H4) a work report (shared/work/work_report.j2, stored as concern evidence)
says when it was judged or ended, and each owner reply says when it was given.
OPEN: (H5) relative words written into list entries, node titles and concern titles ("today", "tonight") on one day
and read on another (19 nodes, 15 concern titles since 10-06); concern briefs shown without when written; time-only
chat lines in dayflow (state_mover_prep_node, item_display, intake triage, switchboard, post-room finalize); pending
tickets without creation time; pod evidence without its date; raw UTC in the work_outcome prompt. Stored text written
before the fix stays undated.

### Ids, labels and relations mixed into text across brain and dayflow — INVENTORIED 2026-10-08, OPEN (design)
Full inventory and the design that answers it: docs/design/objects_and_projections_2026-10-08.md. Concrete defects
found on the way:
- day_tasks validates an own entry's `cites` and then drops it (day_tasks.py, `own_changes` keeps no cites): the
  relation to what prompted the entry is lost.
- The finalizer's `abandon_node_ids` is a dead field: `_apply` keeps only outcome, recommendation and
  question_for_user (work_finalizer_node.py:175).
- Architect directives name earlier nodes by slug in prose (system.j2:161-163 asks for it); some do not match the
  stored id ("ask_nest_approval" vs `ask_nest_approval--02974a`).
- state_mover matches copied composite task ids as exact strings (state_mover_persist_node.py:61-63, 92): a miscopied
  id silently falls through to promotion instead of the hold. action_selector picks are matched by substring
  (node_dispatch.canonical_pick).
- The noticer copies raw concern ids: 2 of 29 distinct ids it emitted were not in the register (a miscopied uuid
  `b7f1d8c4...` for `b7f1d8c2...`; a concern title), logged and skipped.
- `bind_ticket` stores the ticket id in `wake_ref`, the field otherwise holding a prose wake condition
  (work_objects/store.py:502-514).
- The architect's ending of work_4b090fe060db named the work it thought covered it by title only; nothing could
  check it, and the decider later trusted it over its own reason (tonight's reminder lost).

### Clearing the day's list left its work running; the decider took that work as coverage — 2026-10-08, CONFIRMED (one-off, procedure)
The owner's "start the day fresh" (24 entries cancelled 18:21 by brain_list.cancel) ran while the entries' work was
still active: intake's end instructions wait for the next tick and the architect. At 19:10 the day's tasks saw the
placements as new and their old work as active, and decided four not needed because that work "is already
scheduled" (relations: B146 and B608 relied on work_bcaf83649f54, B153 on work_ccde86384766, B558 and B3 on
work_8d9eed685ee2: each its own entry's work); intake then ended that work. The 9:30, 10:00 and 10:30 PM reminders
were lost. Also B571 again decided not needed relying on work_4b090fe060db, whose architect ending claims "already
achieved" (the open module 3 fix: an ending names the work it means as a relation). Procedure for any clearing:
end the entries' work first, then cancel the entries. The relations made the cause readable in one query.

### After midnight the old day's "all day" placements are offered and repurposed for the next day — OBSERVED 2026-10-09, FIXED 2026-10-09
Between midnight and the 05:00 boundary the day's tasks still decides the old day. At 04:47 the day pass re-placed
Thursday's plan with B162 (work companionship) and B614 (funny facts) at "all day"; `offered` drops only clock
times more than an hour past (belief_occurrences.passed returns False for "all day"), so they were offered, and the
decider, reading Friday's calendar, re-decided Thursday's entries for Friday ("Friday's 9:15 AM standup marks a new
workday"): work "(for Thu 10-08; when: 10:00 AM ...)" for Friday's workday. It also filed Friday's standup and
Friday Night Meats reminders under Thursday. Risk: duplicates when Friday's day's tasks starts at 05:00 (the
architect's covered_by caught one pair overnight). Proposed fix: an "all day" placement is past once the calendar
date has moved beyond its day. Owner, 2026-10-09: low stakes before 05:00; fix if duplicates show at the rollover.
It mattered after all: the repurposed work belonged to Thursday's entry, which Friday's decider could not reach (the
re-time entry below). FIXED: belief_occurrences.passed treats an all-day placement as past once the local date is
beyond its day.

### The brain raises concerns from reports on its own list's work — OBSERVED 2026-10-09, FIXED 2026-10-09 (prompt + context)
Work made from the brain's list for a day (calendar or day entries) carries no concern, so its reports reach the
brain as new matters. On 10-09 morning it raised "Morning air conditioner set to COOL at 75°F" from the routine that
had succeeded (matter 331) and "Review and send the Friday Night Meats Zoom-link email" from work still waiting for
the owner's approval (matter 330; the work already does exactly that). Fixed (owner's option A): a work group's
heading says when the work was made from the brain's own list, with the entry and its day
(conversations._work_origin, seen by the gate and the brain), and the brain's rule says such a report is the outcome
of a decision already made: a note on a concern it bears on, or nothing; a new concern only when it reveals
something new (a failure, a problem, the owner's new words); a step the work itself still carries is not tracked
again. Option B (the gate routing such reports to their entry) not done. The two concerns raised that morning stay
until the owner decides. Still OPEN (separate): presence and day-ended events noted on concerns as "does not
establish", where needs_nothing fits.

### The day's tasks asked a worker to re-time another work's step — OBSERVED 2026-10-09, FIXED 2026-10-09
09:56 local: the day's tasks wanted the 10:00 companionship check-in (inside work_d8cfbcc5ce9a, made overnight from
Thursday's entry cal:2026-10-08:B162) moved to 10:20, after the 9:15-10:15 standup. It cannot change another
day's entry and has no way to revise existing work, so it re-decided today's cal:2026-10-09:B162 as needed: "Move
the 10:00 AM work-companionship check-in to 10:20 AM". Intake made new work (work_76afa54cc1b1) whose task asked a
worker to locate and reschedule the check-in; the check-in is a dayflow work step, not a calendar event or reminder,
and no worker tool reaches it, so the worker searched calendar, scheduler and pods and asked the owner where it was
stored. 10:18 the day's tasks set the entry back to not needed and intake ended the work; the dispatched worker
finished anyway (abandonment revokes writes, it does not stop a running thread). The 10:00 check-in went out after
the standup regardless. Causes: the list can only say what is needed, so "change that work" becomes new work about
dayflow's own graph; the owning entry is yesterday's (no owner after its day; the overnight repurposing,
2026-10-09 entry above). The right path is a re-plan instruction to the owning work. Owner, 2026-10-09: the day's
tasks is meant to be mechanical: "If this agent wants to change its recommendations it should rewrite the task, then
the task will have a delta and the orchestrator can see what work object is affected". FIXED: the decider is also
given every earlier day's open entry whose work is still in motion (brain_list.open_before, listed among its own
entries with their day), and told that changing work in motion means modifying the entry that owns it; intake then
revises that work and leaves the architect a re-plan instruction. With the all-day fix, work is no longer made from
an old day's entry after midnight.

### The chat gate's prompt still offered a retired dayflow delegation — OBSERVED 2026-10-09, FIXED 2026-10-09
`agents/master_room/chat_gate/prompts/system.j2` lists "3) Delegate to dayflow orchestrator for deferred,
conditional, or monitoring tasks" and its in-flight guard speaks of "dayflow delegations". Since 2026-09-30 nothing
behind the gate writes to dayflow: track_tf only acknowledges, and the brain reads the same message and keeps it as a
concern (master_room_chat_task_router_node.py docstring). The prompt describes a path that does not exist, so the
model is told something false about itself. Also unstated: a request to change future behaviour ("don't send the
encouragements for two hours") belongs to track_tf, never a switchboard handoff, which has no tool that reaches
scheduled work. Consequence: suspected misrouting of such requests; not yet observed (track_tf fired 0 times in the
last three app runs' logs). Not fixed: part of the hub-and-spoke design (owner, 2026-10-09: the brain is the only
maker of work; quick acts like lights, research or calendar adds must stay immediate from chat). FIXED: the prompt's three options are a reply, a handoff for one act done now that
finishes, and track_tf for what changes later, which names stopping, pausing or changing something the
assistant does on its own and is never a handoff; a message holding both hands off the act and leaves the
rest to the brain. The dayflow-delegation guard line is gone.

### The deciders did not see what an entry's work was waiting for — FOUND 2026-10-09 (by reading), FIXED 2026-10-09 (fac5b8fc)
Suspected, not observed: a work step waiting on an outside event ("until the contractor's reply arrives") is
released by dayflow's state mover when the email comes in. The same email also reaches the brain, which notes it on
the concern, and the concern planner then decides that concern's entries again. The planner (and the day's tasks)
saw an entry's work only as its status, its ending and the results of finished steps (work_links.work_view,
shared/brain/entry_work.j2), not its steps to come or what they wait for. So the awaited email could read as new and
become a second entry: duplicate work, caught at best by the architect's covered_by when it lays out the new goal.
The brain step already saw it (active work with its progress); the planner and the day's tasks did not. Second
route: work from one of the day's own entries with no concern behind it; the email routes as a new matter, and a new
concern's planner adds an entry. Fix (owner, 2026-10-09): work_view carries the progress, entry_work.j2 shows it for
active work, and the brain, concern_tasks and day_tasks prompts say an arrival that work is waiting for is that
work's to act on: a note at most, an entry changed only when it changes the outcome wanted. Not checked whether this
already happened in past entries.

### The concern view prints part of a concern's id in prose — OBSERVED 2026-10-09, OPEN
`agents/shared/work/concern_brief.j2` renders each concern as "- concern {{ c.concern_id[:8] }}: <title>", and its
known facts end with their source refs (`datapod:email:...`, `work:work_...:000-...`). Every reader of the concern view
gets ids as content: the concern planner, dayflow's agents, and since 2026-10-09 the list decider, which reads every
open concern in full (seen in a read-only dry run of its prompt on live data). Against the objects-and-projections
rule (docs/design/objects_and_projections_2026-10-08.md: ids identify objects and are never content); the brain step's
own concern view (shared/brain/concern.j2) shows labels instead. Not fixed: the view is shared by many readers, each
needing its own labels.

### The gate dropped a task the owner gave the assistant — OBSERVED 2026-10-09, FIXED 2026-10-09 (prompt)
13:28 local the owner said in chat: "Today only i want to get a notification every time i get a new email." The chat
gate acknowledged it as track_tf ("the brain reads the user's message itself and tracks it"), but at 13:30 the brain's
gate routed the message none: "a one-day notification preference, which does not bear on an open concern or raise a
separate matter worth tracking" (brain_events 3283). The brain reads only events routed to a concern or new_matter,
so no concern, entry or work was made, and the owner had been told it would be handled. Cause: the gate's "none"
covers "instructions about the conversation itself", and nothing told it that a task the owner gives the assistant is
the brain's to decide; tasks like this one used to be picked up by dayflow (the steward and architect) reading chat.
Owner, 2026-10-09: tasks the owner assigns that change the assistant's behaviour, or that need more than a simple
request the helpers executed at once, and that no agent has shown it picked up, must reach the brain. FIXED (prompt):
the gate routes such a task to a concern or new_matter unless the conversation shows it carried out, and an
acknowledgment that it will be handled is a promise, not a pickup. Not done: routing a track_tf acknowledgment to the
brain by code, without the gate. Event 3283 stays routed none.

### Dayflow's email items named the sender without the address, so a wait on a sender never matched — OBSERVED 2026-10-09, FIXED 2026-10-09
Work work_9edbf70ea8ed (the owner's "today only, notify me of every new email from the owner's own address") parked a
step on "An additional email from the owner's own address arrives". At 14:02 local the owner sent a test email from
that address; dayflow took it in at 14:03 (dayflow_email:google_user_primary:1a122793f45bf46e), and the state mover saw
it as "sender: <the owner's name> / subject: Test email" and answered "No awaited event clearly arrived". The item carries
only the display name: input_message_builder._build_email_message sets `email_sender` and the summary from
`email_data["sender"]`, while the email record also holds `sender_email`; brain_inbox shows both
(email_pods.email_sender_line, "Name <address>"). Consequence: any wait or reader keyed on an address cannot match.
The email scored importance 5, under the brain's 6, so the brain never saw it either. Proposed fix: build the item's
sender with email_pods.email_sender_line. Items already written keep the name only.
FIXED: _build_email_message names the sender with email_pods.email_sender_line ("Name <address>"), which
reaches the item's summary, email_sender and the state mover's intake source. Still a limit (by design, not
changed): dayflow reads only emails scored importance 5 or more (email_pods.recent_emails), the brain 6 or more;
a request about every email from a sender cannot see a lower-scored one.

### The day's record (shadow) crashes on an all-day event — OBSERVED 2026-10-09, OPEN
14:37 local the day_record agent answered a new event with "start": "All day"; day_record.apply ->
local_clock_utc -> day_pass._clock_hour_minute returned None and the run failed ("cannot unpack non-iterable NoneType
object"), logged by brain_wake as the shadow's failure. `problems` does not check that start and end are clock times,
and the schema has no way to say all day. The run is recorded only after apply, so the same inputs are decided again
on every brain pass (a smart-tier call each time) until the answer happens to avoid it. Shadow only: it feeds nothing.
Proposed: the answer check names a start or end that is not a clock time (one correction round), and the schema gets
an all-day flag. Owner, 2026-10-09 earlier: no record fixes for now; this one is a crash and a repeated cost.

### Ticket replies reached no decider — FOUND 2026-10-09, FIXED 2026-10-09
The brain's inbox had no ticket source. Replies to dayflow's own tickets returned to the task that asked and reached
the brain in its work report, but replies to every other ticket (this month: 168 scheduled reminders, 15 notices, 8
questions, 8 audit notices) were read only by the old daily context tracker, as context. On 10-03 the owner answered a
question ticket: "This is not something you can set a recurring schedule for. You do this every night."; no decider
read it. FIXED: brain_inbox.ingest_tickets takes every answered ticket except dayflow_orchestrator's (the ticket's
words and the owner's reply, at the reply's time), the gate routes it like chat (a direction is the brain's to decide;
a bare "Done" or "OK" goes none), and an answered ticket wakes the brain.

### The brain filled concerns with notes that recorded nothing — OBSERVED 2026-10-09, FIXED 2026-10-09 (prompts)
Every return to the computer (1:08 PM, 5:37 PM, and 9:22 PM after 3 h 16 min away at a family visit) was routed to the
fatigue and poor-sleep concern, and the brain noted each: "does not establish when or how well he slept ... the
October 13 reminder remains scheduled". Every "OK" to an hourly encouragement check-in was routed to the work-stress
concern and noted "does not show whether his stress changed". The 6:06 PM departure was routed none, so the evening
(the movie at a friend's house, which the owner had mentioned) never reached the brain as what the absence was.
Consequence: the concerns' records fill with lines that say nothing, burying what they do know. FIXED (prompts): the
brain writes no note that only says an event establishes nothing, and an event that establishes nothing needs
nothing; an absence is read against the day (what was planned in that window is a sign, not a fact), and an absence
in the day or evening says nothing about sleep; the gate routes a day or evening absence to a sleep concern only at
night, and a routine check-in only acknowledged goes none. The notes already written stay in the records.

### The noticer forced the same "keep_active" paragraph onto concerns every day, once onto the wrong one — FOUND 2026-10-09, FIXED 2026-10-09
compute_pressure flags a concern with 8+ notes since its last disposition, and the daily noticer must give each
flagged concern a disposition. keep_active is meant to reset the count, but the check counts `reinforcement_count` or,
when absent, the journal's lines, while the reset stores `int(reinforcement_count or 0)`: 0 for every concern the
brain raised (the noticer alone sets reinforcement_count). Such a concern is flagged again every day. Live on
2026-10-09: the library fine (19 lines), both Heroku concerns, AI Studio and the testing-day concern, each with a
daily keep_active paragraph. Separately the noticer writes raw concern ids in its answer: on 10-06 it filed the AI
Studio disposition under the library fine's id. Underneath both: the noticer is a second decider and writer of
concerns beside the brain. Proposed (docs/design/concerns_as_state_2026-10-09.md, rule 2): it stops writing to
concerns; its pattern findings reach the brain as events; the forced-disposition machinery goes.
Fixed 2026-10-09 (owner: "noticer is supposed to look at long term things. it should just raise issues that can then
become concerns."): the noticer's answer is now `issues` (title, kind, subject, what, why_now) and a summary;
`noticer_run` puts each issue in the brain's inbox (`brain_inbox.add_noticer_issues`, source `noticer`, each its own
matter) and pokes the brain. It reads concerns by title only, so it copies no id. Removed: `persist.apply_noticer_output`,
`compute_pressure` and the dispositions, the noticer's questions and their answer journalling (`annotate_concern_answer`,
`trigger_noticer`, `get_for_noticer_processing`), the tick log, and the concern door's anchor suppression. The
paragraphs already written stay in the records until the conversion in concerns_as_state_2026-10-09.md.

### The brain's thread deadlocked the app's database writer: a read inside a write in ingest_tickets — FOUND 2026-10-09, FIXED
`brain_inbox.ingest_tickets` opened a write connection and, inside it, called `reports(work_id)`
(`concern_feedback.reports_to_brain` → `persist.concerns_holding_work` → `concern_store.load_register` →
`ensure_schema` → `connect(True)`). Both take `db_manager`'s writer slot, a non-reentrant `threading.Lock`, so the
brain-wake thread waited on itself. Every writer in the process then queued behind it (py-spy dump at 23:58: brain-wake,
the dayflow tick, the ingest service, the LLM call logger, routines all at `db_manager.transaction` line 115; the WAL
last written 23:54:01), and raw-session writers failed with "database is locked". It fires only when an answered ticket
belongs to dayflow work, so it first showed on the 23:53 restart. Consequences that night: the Google OAuth token save
failed (next entry), `fetch_email` was auto-disabled after three failures, and its auto-disable ticket could not be
written. Introduced by 8e96d9d0 (2026-10-09, 22:01). The tests used scratch connections without the shared lock, so
they could not see it. Fixed: which replies to skip is decided before the write
(`test_which_replies_the_work_reports_is_decided_before_the_write` fails if anything runs inside the write).

### A database error while saving refreshed Google credentials is reported as a revoked token and opens the re-auth browser — FOUND 2026-10-09, OPEN
`google_credentials` wraps `creds.refresh(...)`, `fetch_principal_email` and `store.upsert_credentials(...)` in one
`except Exception`, which opens the OAuth browser and raises "Google OAuth token refresh failed (token expired/revoked)".
On 2026-10-09 at 23:56:12 the refresh succeeded and only the save failed (sqlite "database is locked", previous entry);
the owner got a re-auth browser window, and the error counted toward `fetch_email`'s auto-disable. Intended: a failed
save is a storage failure, raised as such, without asking the owner to re-authenticate. Not fixed (outside the task).

### test_brain_stories' day view failed between midnight and 1 AM local — FOUND 2026-10-10, FIXED
`test_the_day_view_gives_the_ribbon_the_lines_and_the_cards` seeded its events an hour before now and then read
"today" (`_window_days(NOW)[-1]`); between midnight and 1 AM local the seeded events fall on yesterday, the day view
had no story, and the test failed (seen at 00:25 on 2026-10-10). Test-only; the code was right. Fixed in 860d75b3: the
test reads the seeded events' own day.

### A placement whose time moves loses its work: the new calendar concern gets no entry while the old one's work looks in motion — FOUND 2026-10-10, FIXED
A calendar concern's identity is its belief, day and time (`calendar_concerns.concern_id`). At 06:09 on 2026-10-10 the
day pass placed the day again and moved the evening AC belief (B149) from 9:30 PM to 9:00 PM: `apply_calendar_concerns`
closed the 9:30 concern (871531fe, "withdrawn") and raised a new 9:00 one (562653f0). In that same pass the day's tasks
ran (06:10:58) before anything cancelled the closed concern's entry (`brain_list.cancel_for_closed_concerns` runs
earlier in `brain_wake.run_brain`, before the day pass), so it read the old work (work_03706aba78af, 9:30 PM) as in
motion, "made from a concern's entry not shown here", and gave the new concern no entry (`entries: []`). The next
pass cancelled the old entry (06:11:31) and the day's tasks ran once more (06:12) with the old work still listed in
motion (its `end` instruction was delivered only at 06:21): `entries: []` again. From then on the day's tasks report
`unchanged`: nothing it is woken by changes when that work ends, so the 9:00 PM concern has no entry and, once the
architect ends the old work, nothing sets the AC to 70°F tonight. The same move closed two more (the 9 PM dog-walk
belief and the 11 PM lights). Intended: a placement that moves keeps its concern and its entry (the entry is revised
and its work re-planned), and the day's tasks never see a withdrawn concern's work as covering a live one. Suspected
causes, not yet decided: the time in the identity; the order in the brain's pass (the day pass closes concerns after
their entries are cancelled); the day's tasks not woken by the end of work it relied on. Also seen the same morning,
a design question rather than a defect: near-duplicate beliefs each become a concern (two "Bonnie and Clyde are taken
outside" at 8:00, two "lights off" at 10:00, three dinner/family-time ones), and the day's tasks made its own `day:`
entries for the dog outings instead of entries on the dog concerns.
Fixed 2026-10-10 (all three causes): a placement names the concern from earlier in the day it continues, so a moved
time keeps its concern, entry and work (`calendar_concerns`, `subconscious::day_placements`); entries of closed
concerns are cancelled just before the day's tasks read the list, after the day pass (`brain_wake.run_brain`); and
work from a cancelled entry not in view reads as being ended and covering nothing (`day_tasks._unshown_entry`).

### Calendar concerns are raised one per belief, past the concern door, so one moment of the day becomes several concerns — FOUND 2026-10-10, FIXED
`calendar_concerns.from_plan` raises one concern per placed belief, by code, with the identity belief + day + time, and
never passes the concern door (by design in e5d281aa). The day pass places each approved belief separately, so beliefs
about the same moment become separate concerns with near-identical titles: on 2026-10-10 the morning dog walk became
three (B181 the 8-9 window, B575 the owner sometimes walks them, B8 another household member takes them), dinner and family time three (B26,
B135, B555), the 10 AM lights two (B211, B478), the evening liquids two (B608, B146). Only B211/B478 say the same thing
(next entry); the rest are distinct beliefs about one moment. Intended: a concern per thing the day holds, with the
beliefs it rests on. Fixed 2026-10-10 (owner: "we already had an agent that looks at all the beliefs and picks out
the ones that apply today"): `subconscious::day_placements` makes the plan's lines into one placement per thing the
day holds, each one concern with every belief it rests on. Until the plan became data (2f7e317e, 2026-10-02) the
writer wrote one markdown line per moment citing several beliefs; the per-belief placements of 2026-10-02 and the
per-belief judgments in batches of 10 of 2026-10-05 left it no way to say two beliefs were one thing.

### An owner's edit in /beliefs can turn a belief into a duplicate of another; nothing checks the catalog after intake — FOUND 2026-10-10, OPEN
Intake checks each new statement for duplicates against the beliefs held, once, when it is formed. `routes/beliefs.py
_correct_statement` revises a belief to the owner's wording and checks nothing: on 2026-10-03 B211 (formed 2026-04-11
as "a daily 9:30 AM check for lights left on, no 9:30 PM prompt") was edited to "<owner> wants house lights turned off at
10AM.", which B478 (2026-08-18) already said. Both are approved for the calendar, so each day both are placed and both
get work. `belief_engine/matching` (the duplicate matcher) reads only the legacy `user_beliefs` store, not the intake
catalog. Also seen: B478's only "said" evidence is "Do not code by yourself without permission!" (2026-08-18), a source
unrelated to the statement (suspected mis-attachment, not traced).

### The architect's refused answer gets no correction: a goal stays empty and is retried every tick — FOUND 2026-10-10, FIXED
`dayflow_orchestrator::work_architect`'s form refuses a node with both `wake_at` and `wake_ref`
(`agent_form._validate_wake`: "a node waits on a time (wake_at) OR a condition (wake_ref), not both"). The refusal
raises out of the structured-output call ("LLM structured_output failed"); `work_architect_node` logs "decompose
failed" and leaves the goal empty, and `_undecomposed_goals` offers it again on the next tick, with no correction
telling the model what was wrong. On 2026-10-10 the lights-off-after-bedtime goal (work_160c0bf978c4, "Turn off the
household lights after the owner has been inactive for a while following bedtime", 11:00 PM) failed this way at 09:14
and 09:17, each time a smart-tier call; the same goal was planned as a single 11:00 PM node earlier that morning.
Intended: one correction round carrying the validation error, then a loud failure, as the brain's agents do (a time
the state mover can hold past, or a condition, not both). Consequence until fixed: the goal does not run, and every
tick spends a call on it.
Fixed 2026-10-10: `work_architect_node._ask` asks once more with the form's refusal named (`_form_refusal`, the shared
`architect_refused.j2`), on every architect call; a second refusal fails the goal's planning as before
(test_architect_form_refusal_correction).

### The day pass judged a moment-of-day condition as "not today" and flipped beliefs in and out of the day — FOUND 2026-10-10, FIXED
Each rewrite of the day's plan judged every approved belief afresh (`day_pass._judge`), and the writer read a
condition naming a moment of the day as a test of whether the belief applies: B488 (lights off after he has gone to
bed) was placed at 05:06, not at 06:09 and 07:19 ("no indication he has gone to bed"), placed at 08:21, and not at
09:24; B558 (reminders to stop coding and sleep) not at 09:24 ("no bedtime specified"). Each flip-to-false withdrew
the belief's calendar concern, cancelled its entry and, with the brain's end binding, ended its work: the
lights-off work (work_160c0bf978c4) was ended at 09:27. Fixed: the writer's prompt says a condition naming a moment
of the day sets when the belief applies, never whether (replayed 3x on the day's inputs: both placed, 6/6); and a
belief placed earlier the same day keeps its placements when the plan is written again unless the schedule ahead or
the belief itself changed (`day_pass._kept`); only the others are judged again.

### The email-forward watch of 2026-10-10: a duplicate forward, a watch that ended after one email, and the assistant's own forward read as the owner's — FOUND 2026-10-10, PARTLY FIXED
The owner asked in chat (10:13) to watch emails from a Yahoo address today and send their contents to his Gmail. The
brain made a concern (f51da012, expiring at midnight), the day's tasks an entry (con:86d121f2a2, 10:20), dayflow
work_33dd13e0cbf5 (10:26) with two steps: forward the email already received, and "forward further matching emails
today" (a `wake_ref`). Four defects, the first three confirmed in the store:
- **Duplicate forward.** At 10:26:52, before the first step ran, the state mover woke the "further emails" step with
  the same email the work was made for (wake evidence: the 10:19 email, subject "Hi <owner>"): an arrival from before the step
  existed counted as the further email it waits for. Both steps sent it: actions 10:27:07 and 10:29:35 (message ids
  1a126daafb4de75e, 1a126dcf27156b0a). The second step's finalizer saw it ("appears to be a duplicate forwarding")
  and judged it achieved.
- **The watch ended after one email.** A watch "through the end of today" was planned as one `wake_ref` step; once it
  fired the work rolled up to done (10:29), though both finalizer accounts say monitoring "remains active". A later
  email from that address today would not be forwarded by this work.
- **The assistant's own outbound email re-enters as the owner's.** The forward the assistant sent lands in the owner's Gmail inbox and was
  ingested as a new email event (brain event 5004, 10:27:08); the gate wrote "<owner> forwarded the Yahoo message" and
  the brain noted "At 10:27 AM, <owner> forwarded the matching Yahoo email". Nothing marks mail the assistant sent.
- **A "now" list entry waits for the next spaced tick** (suspected design gap): the brain's list changed at 10:20:37,
  but the scheduler kept the tick already set for 10:26 by a calendar update ("Keeping sooner scheduled run"), so the
  work was made six minutes after the entry.
Duplicate forward, fixed 2026-10-10 (owner's standing rule: "all nodes that refer to artifacts must site the refs for
those artifacts"): the root was that the work held no ref to the email. Work made from the brain's list carried no
sources, so the architect saw the email only as text in the brief and pasted it into the step. Work made for a concern
now carries the concern's messages and email/record pods as source records with their refs
(`brain_list_intake._sources`), the architect's prompt says a step that acts on or refers to an artifact cites it in
`uses`, and the state mover sees when each wait began and, for each arrival, the tasks that already acted on it
(`state_mover_prep_node`). The second worker saw the concern's note that the email was already forwarded and still
trusted the wake label: fixed 2026-10-10 ("fix the worker"): the worker sees what the work already did outside (its
actions) and which attempt a wake is for, and its prompt says a wake is the state mover's judgment, not a check, so an
arrival the work already handled is recorded as handled and nothing is sent (shared/work/worker.j2). The watch ending
after one email: fixed 2026-10-10 by the standing watch (owner: "Success but keep node open if the node is of the type
where it needs to be ran repeatedly"; event waits only): a `wake_ref` step with `repeats_until` waits again after
each run the finalizer judges `achieved_keep_open`, and closes at its end (05_DAYFLOW.md, "A standing watch"). The
echo of the assistant's own mail (sent from the owner's Gmail, so its sender is the owner; the echo carried the very
Gmail id the send returned, 1a126daafb4de75e): fixed 2026-10-10 (owner: "ok try it"): `send_email` puts its own headers
on every email (`gmail_api_client.SENT_BY_ASSISTANT_HEADER`, with the work and task on a work session); the fetch
records `sent_by_assistant`/`sent_for_work`/`sent_for_task` on an email carrying them whose sender is an account the
assistant holds, the owner's or its own, however many (owner: "There could be multiple accounts"; the OAuth account
store's addresses, `email_utils.held_addresses`, read on each fetch; `email_utils.sent_by_assistant`), and `email_pods.email_sender_line`, which every reader uses (brain events, the
brain's conversations, dayflow items, tickets), says "the assistant, sending from <owner>". Mail sent before the fix and
the two pods of 2026-10-10 are unmarked; the email tools agents read with (`get_email_messages`, `get_email_thread`)
do not read the mark. The tick spacing remains open.

### The presence line says the owner was away until now while they are at the keyboard — FOUND 2026-10-10, FIXED 2026-10-11
`context_sources.get_current_presence_status` builds "Last away from X to Y" from
`afk_statistics.get_afk_intervals_overlapping_range(..., include_provisional=False)`. The active segment the owner is
in right now is provisional (open) until it ends, so it is left out, and the "gap after the last active segment" runs
from the end of the last finished segment to now. That gap is reported as the most recent completed absence. At 9:07 PM
on 2026-10-10 the brain's situation (`shared/brain/situation.j2`, read by the day's tasks) said: "The owner is at the
keyboard, since Sat 10-10 08:58 PM. Last away from Sat 10-10 08:22 PM to Sat 10-10 09:07 PM." The real absence was
8:22 to 8:58. Intended: the last away period ends where the current active spell began. Consequence: every reader
told the owner is at the keyboard is also told they were away until this minute. The 9:07 PM run of the day's tasks
(after the owner said in chat they were drunk, with the 9 PM dog-walk notice out) wrote "the evening dog walk is
ongoing" and made work telling the owner, who was at the keyboard, not to keep walking the dogs. Only the brain
rendered the field: the daily context tracker received it in `presence_status` but never showed it, reading its
"Activity Pattern" from `context_sources.activity_spans` instead (the stretch still going included, blips absorbed).

Fix (2026-10-11): the brain reads the same timeline. `situation.activity` gives the day's `activity_spans` from the
day's start, and `situation.j2` lists each span local ("… until now: at the computer") in place of the "Last away"
sentence. The `most_recent_afk` calculation was deleted from `get_current_presence_status`; nothing else read it.
Pinned by `test_situation.test_time_back_at_the_keyboard_is_not_read_as_away`. Still open, not part of this fix:
`activity_spans` returns an empty list when it fails (logged as a warning), so the brain then sees no timeline
rather than an error.

### The conversation starter delivered work findings outside the loop, and the brain then cancelled the full delivery — FOUND 2026-10-11, OPEN (design question)
On 2026-10-10 the brain made an entry (con:756383adc4) to check an advertised 8% Treasury offer and tell the owner on
Sunday at 10:00 AM. The research step finished at 9:43 PM; the step telling the owner waited for 10:00 AM. At the 5 AM
day rebuild the daily context tracker wrote the research's finding into the day's `day_theme` ("The 8% bond screenshot
was researched; without a CUSIP or terms, the offer could not be verified …"). Where the tracker read it during a rebuild
that suppresses chat is not verified. When the owner returned at 5:47 AM the dayflow pipeline's `conversation_starter`
stage, reading the daily context, sent a two-sentence version on its own (intent `topic_seed`). At 5:59 the day's tasks
read that message as the delivery ("<the owner> has already received the verified findings … in the 5:48 AM message") and
cancelled the entry; its work was abandoned. Consequence: the owner got the verdict but not the risks the research
found (loss on an early sale, money tied up to maturity, taxes, inflation, the security's own terms), and nothing will
send them. Two departures from the one loop: a spoke (the tracker) carrying a work outcome as the day's theme, and a
spoke (the conversation starter) speaking to the owner about the brain's matters without the brain deciding it. Not
fixed; the owner decides whether the conversation starter stays, and what it may draw on.

### The day's record and the day's tasks read finished things several times over — FOUND 2026-10-11, FIXED 2026-10-11
In the day_record prompt of 2026-10-11 06:03 AM, most of the text repeated something already shown:
1. **A concern raised today shows its description twice.** `day_record._today_notes` puts the note a concern was
   raised with first among "today's" notes when the concern was raised today, and `open_concerns` also shows it as the
   concern's description. All 16 calendar concerns raised at 5:01 AM appear twice, word for word.
2. **One finished work arrives as two full reports.** The finalizer's judgment and the work's ending each become a
   brain event worded by `shared/work/work_report.j2`: the objective, the concerns it was made for and the whole "where
   the work stands now" view. For the one-step 6 AM AC work the ending added only "completed automatically". The
   look-back (`day_tasks.matters_today`, read by both the day's record and the day's tasks) shows each event's full
   stored text, then the brain's reason, then the note it wrote, which says the same thing a third time.
3. **The brain's notes on open concerns show twice.** Each note appears under its concern ("today …") and again
   under the matter that wrote it ("note on a concern").
Consequence: a large part of the prompt is repetition (not measured), and it grows with each finished work through the day.
The day's tasks also reads the ended work again in the situation's past work.

Fix (2026-10-11; owner: "reasonable compressed or summarized is fine, particularly for closed concerns", as long as a
consumer looking closer can see the full data). `day_tasks.matters_today` (the look-back both readers share, now
rendered by `shared/brain/matters_today.j2`) shows the events of one work as one item, the work as it stands now (title,
status, when it last changed, why it ended; `_work_brief`, read live from the store) with the brain's decision on
each report, and leaves out notes on concerns still open. Messages and emails keep their own words. The full report
stays on the event, read by the brain step when it arrived. The full data is still there for a closer look: in the
day's tasks the work carries its label (`project`), which resolves to the work in full in the situation's work or,
past its window, among the earlier things; an open concern carries its whole record, with its notes and attached work.
`day_record._today_notes` no longer repeats the note a concern was raised with; the concern says when it was raised.
On this morning's data the look-back went from about 45 lines to 9. Not changed: the day's tasks still sees ended work
in the situation's past work, in full; that is where its label leads.

Follow-up (2026-10-11; owner: the end result should be one line, "set AC to COOL 75F at 6AM. Done successfully or
something like that"). Code could not write that line truthfully, so `subconscious::work_line` (nano tier;
`subconscious/work_line.py`) reads an ended work's full report once and writes it; the brain's pass writes the line of
each work that ended in the last 30 hours (table `brain_work_lines`, a failure recorded and not retried), and the
look-back shows an ended work by its line alone, without the brain's decisions on it; the brain's notes on concerns
are no longer in the look-back. Tried on 26 works that ended 10-08 to 10-11 before integrating: the done ones were
right from the first prompt; work dropped before its time was first written as if the thing itself had not happened
("the lights were not turned off", for work dropped at 9:27 AM for an 11 PM task), fixed in the prompt ("Dropped at
9:11 AM: the day's plan was rebuilt"); a line now and then runs to two sentences. The owner, of the agent: "i kinda
hate this as it becomes a new failure surface, but lets try".
